- **Owner.** A lead's owner is the user who works it. None means nobody works it yet, and any user can take it. Territory is shared, so a lead's area never picks its owner. Change history keeps who owned it before.
- **Stage.** A lead's stage comes from the lead stages in `stage`: New, Qualified, Outreach Active, Engaged, Nurture, Opportunity and Archived. Nurture replaces named drip campaigns. Do not contact is the party's `do_not_contact` row, and deal outcomes are deal phases, not lead stages.
- **How found.** Each lead and client says how they were found. The `how_found` choices are referral, vendor, marketing post, public records, cold outreach, own research, inbound, national account or corporate lead, existing relationship, and event. Not recorded is only for records copied from the old system with no source, and a recorded source replaces it on a later copy (owner, #604). It is a fact about the person or company, so it holds in every role, and a lead that becomes a client copies nothing. A list can be counted for lead attribution, where free text cannot.
- **Stage evidence.** `lead_stage_move` keeps applied evidence, with the activity, reference and strength. Pending weak moves use the shared `proposal` queue below. Applying evidence holds the lead and changes its stage only if it still matches `from_stage`. Unattended jobs apply strong evidence only along New → Qualified → Outreach Active → Engaged → Nurture or Opportunity. Weak evidence and archiving require a direct user, and archiving a nonblank reason. Ordinary user stage edits use change history. Applied evidence is immutable; undo is a new move or correction. Matching suggestions and evidence collapse on merge, with removed rows retained in change history.
- **Working details.** The lead role keeps its `lane` from `lead_lane`, `segment`, and `estimated_lease_on` with `lease_estimate_source` and optional high, medium or low confidence. An estimate always has its source; neither can stand alone. Lanes are independent of how found. `party.how_found_detail` keeps the attribution detail in every role. A merge keeps the survivor's working details, filling gaps from the duplicate, and moves an estimate with its own source and confidence. How-found detail fills only when the source agrees or the survivor had none.

- **Outreach drafts.** `lead_contact_draft` keeps one draft per lead, its subject, body, owner, intended send time and time zone. `requires_human_send` is always true and `dispatchable` always false. Approval never sends. The engine inserts only draft content, so approval and revision take their defaults. A user or an agent working for one may edit or approve only while the lead is qualified and has no active do-not-contact restriction. Any edit to the text, owner or intended time clears approval and increments the database-written revision. A caller approves only with the revision it read in its update condition. Approval actors and times stay in change history. A merge keeps the survivor's draft and retains the removed duplicate's text in change history.

- **Candidates.** `candidate` is the separate list from sweeps and public records. Its source and source key identify a row once, with its original object-shaped context, name and qualifying reason. Unreviewed rows name no party. A user, including an agent working for one, promotes to a party holding the lead role, or declines with a reason. Jobs may suppress with a reason; a user must reopen before promoting. Promoted and declined decisions stay decided, so sweeps cannot put them back in the list. Only a merge changes the promoted party. Source and source key never change. Sweeps may refresh source context, name, qualification and estimates only while unreviewed; change history keeps earlier facts. Decline reasons stay as the user decided, and candidates are never removed.
- **Not kept.** The 0 to 100 lead score and named drip campaigns do not come across (owner, #196).

Moving today's rows across:

- Each `lead` becomes the lead role on its party, and each `client` the client role on its party, keeping its `created_at`. A client merged away today is not copied. A party with several leads or clients keeps the earliest `created_at` and the stage and owner of its most recently changed lead, and is listed for a partner.
- Stages `new`, `qualified`, `outreach_active`, `engaged`, `opportunity` and `archived` stay. `nurture_drip` and `closed_lost` become nurture, as the Leads screen already shows Closed-Lost. `active_deal` and `closed_won` become archived with the reason "became a deal in the old system" in stage history. Archiving keeps it out of Prospects even when the deal link did not survive the copy. A `do_not_contact` stage or `suppressed` lead becomes archived under the existing contact restriction rule. Today that sets only the lead, not the person's `contact_state`, so the lead copy needs a partner-reviewed reason to create its `do_not_contact` row. Without a reviewed reason it lists the restriction for correction.
- `owner_id` maps through `outside_id` to an existing `app_user`. Leads keep `party_role.owner_user_id`. A lead owned by automation or a system actor copies unclaimed. Clients keep nullable `client.owner_user_id`, separate from national-account ownership. For a client owned by automation or a system actor, all its old deals must name the same mapped user. A reviewed client owner takes precedence. The copy lists unresolved owners, label-only owners without reviewed input, conflicting owners on clients mapped to one party, and an existing different client owner. It retries missing client owners without overwriting existing owners or changing stamps. A record with neither an ID nor a label keeps no owner unless the owner supplies a reviewed client owner.
- How found comes from `client.acquisition_source`, asked of the client, or else `lead.source_type`. Both are free text today. A `prospect-pool` lead is mapped by its candidate's own pool source. Every other value is listed for a partner to map to the list, and so is a party whose two values disagree. A lead or client with neither waits, listed, since the database refuses one without how it was found.
- A lead whose `client_id` points at a client on another party, such as the practice a doctor runs, keeps its lead role on the person, and the client role stays on the practice.
- `score`, `score_reason`, `drip_campaign`, `drip_added`, `report_back_due` and `sf_deal` do not move. Only the old spreadsheet import wrote the last four, and nothing reads them.
- Each applied old `lead_stage_move` becomes evidence on its lead party; a pending one becomes a shared proposal, keeping its id, activity, evidence reference, strength and reason. `nurture_drip` becomes `nurture`. Proposal creation and approval map to their respective change-history entries, retaining the old actors and dates. An undone row remains applied evidence followed by its undo in change history. A missing activity, blank evidence or reason, unknown actor or contradictory stage is listed for a user rather than invented. The owner loads historical rows without replaying the application trigger; ordinary stage events map to change history only. Run `schema/copy-lead-stage-moves.sql` in its own owner transaction after the lead and activity copies. Existing evidence and proposals stay unchanged; conflicting source changes are listed. Undo and ordinary stage events become role stage changes in history, with the recorded actor and time. Matching keys, stage values and stamps make reruns add no history.

- The old lane list becomes `lead_lane`, and `lead.lane` and `segment` stay on its lead role. An unknown lane or whitespace-only segment is listed for a user.
- `est_lease_event`, `event_source` and `event_confidence` become the estimate date, source and confidence. A promoted candidate's `est_basis` supplies a missing source. An estimate without a source, confidence without an estimate, unknown confidence or an unparsed `est_lease_event_raw` is listed rather than inferred.
- `client.acquisition_detail` becomes `party.how_found_detail`, otherwise `lead.source_detail` does. If both differ, the lead detail becomes a separate note activity so both survive. A detail without mapped how found waits with its source.
- `notes` becomes a note activity about the party, dated at the old row's last change. `last_touch` comes from the latest contact activity; a standalone old date or imported last-touch-stamp note with no contact activity is listed rather than invented as contact. `notes_path` is an old file reference, not note text; its file must be copied as a document or listed for a user.

- Each lead's or client's `next_action` becomes a next step about its party, with its description trimmed of surrounding whitespace, due date, hold and owner, as today's lead export shows it. It ends as the deal next-step copy describes, never at the row's last change: done at its completion event or with no time, cancelled with its recorded reason or the approved import wording, and expired with its Clear the deck event when that proves it. Conflicting cancellations, unproven expiries, blank descriptions and `source_ref` are listed. A step without a partner owner takes its copied client's owner or its copied lead's owner. A step on an unclaimed lead stays unclaimed. Actions of leads and clients copied earlier are retried on each run, so a corrected or newer action still arrives. For matching party, text and due date, one step copies, with open actions ahead of ended ones. Open source actions with an inferred partner owner keep their own ids so a collision can be recorded as a cancellation. `lead.next_action_date` is only a fallback: a lead with no `next_action` row gets a next step on that date, with text marking it as an imported lead follow-up (#347).
- A lead's `registry_ref` and a client's `roster_ref` become outside ids under `old_system_ref`, as a party's `ref` does, unless another record holds it, which is listed.
- A client's verbatim roster labels (`contact_label`, `deal_type_label`, `possible_duplicate_label`, `specialty_type_label`, and `owner_label` beside a real owner) are listed for a partner, not stored. So is `prospect_engagement_status` on a prospect or an inactive client; elsewhere it holds only its default.
- Each old `lead_contact_draft` becomes a draft on its lead party, preserving id, subject, body, owner, schedule and time zone. The old revision and approval become its revision and approved flag, with old creation and approval actors and times in change history. The owner loads old approval and revision values through their own insert privileges. A missing lead, unknown owner, blank subject, body or time zone, several old drafts mapping to one party, or an approval without a known user is listed rather than guessed.
- Each old `candidate_pool` row becomes a `candidate`, retaining its id, source and source key. `pool` becomes `unreviewed`, `suppressed_dup` becomes `suppressed`, and promoted and declined stay. `expired` becomes reversible suppression with its recorded expiry basis, or the factual reason "Expired by Clear the deck". Email intake's `confirmed` can target an existing record or deal without creating a lead, so those rows are listed for a user with their existing target; they never become invented promotions. `score_basis` becomes qualification; `est_lease_event` and `est_basis` become the estimated date and its source. The complete old source row and qualifying context, including channel, location, segment, duplicate review and candidate ranking fields, stay in `source_row` as `{"row": <old source_row>, "pool": <old typed columns except source_row>}`, including `source_seq` and the old confirmation values in `pool`; candidate ranking never becomes a lead score. A promoted lead id maps to its lead party. Promotion events name the deciding user, including the user a sponsored agent acted for; `declined_by` names the declining user. `confirmed_at` and `confirmed_by` become a historical confirmation entry, and their original values stay in the copied context. An automated decision without a recoverable user is listed. Decline or duplicate basis supplies the reason. Missing qualification, ambiguous promotion, a reasonless rejection or an unsourced estimate is listed for a user. Moving an estimate and verified contact channels onto a newly promoted lead is part of the promotion save, not an unattended sweep.

## Prospects and the Monday review

The Deals screen's Prospects lane and Monday review (`docs/screens/deals.md` § "Prospects" and § "Monday review", plans #51) sit on records the design already has. Question numbers below are that spec's. Pending a question means not built until the owner answers.

- **A prospect** is a party holding the lead role at stage `opportunity` that is on no deal in any role (question 10, owner, 2026-11-18). Nothing marks it a prospect; the read derives it.
- **Notes** are note activities about the party (door 19), from the partner's person, shown newest first by activity time, including notes from before it was a prospect.
- **A follow-up** is an ordinary next step about the party (door 18) with `replaces: "current"`, owned by the partner who set it. It cancels that partner's open ordinary step on the party as superseded, including a cadence check-in, so the one-open-step-per-owner rule holds. Each partner may have one. The card shows the soonest. Send the setting partner's step ID and change ID with the replacement, or null if the detail had no step for that partner.
- **Claim** is the lead role's `owner_user_id`. A new prospect has none until a partner claims it (owner, 2026-11-18).
- **Drop** is an archive: a `lead_stage_move` to `archived` with the partner's reason, which rule 71 already requires.
- **Turn into deal** uses door 16 with the client the partner picks; when that is another party, such as a doctor's practice, the prospect joins the deal as `client_contact`. Either way it is on a deal and leaves the lane.

Two facts are new, each typed and constrained:

- **Needs attention on a lead.** `party_role.needs_attention`, a boolean that is never unknown, true only on the lead role, set or cleared only by a user or a job working for one, like a deal's (rule 70). The engine's role may update it.
- **`weekly_review`.** One row per item reviewed in a week: `week_of`, a Monday, and exactly one of `deal_id` and a `party_id` holding the lead role (decision 21), stored once per week and item and stamped with the user who marked it. Both partners share the mark and either can undo it (question 12, owner, 2026-11-18). Undo removes the row and change history keeps it.

Slice 36 defines the database rules for both. The prospect read adds lead attention under rule 70.

The new doors below use only the signed-in partner entrance, so a job gets 404 on them. Doors 16, 18 and 19 keep both entrances. Every save follows the shared save rules (§ "What every door shares"), including `idempotency_key`.

| Door                                                 | Request                                                                                                                                                                 | Answer                                               |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| Add prospect: `POST /api/engine/prospects`           | `idempotency_key`; `party_id` of a stored person or company, with `how_found` only when it has none; or `name`, `kind` and `how_found` for someone new; optional `note` | `201 { party_id, activity_id, change_id, existing }` |
| Add note: door 19, `POST .../activities`             | `idempotency_key`, `kind: "note"`, `about: [{ party_id }]`, `summary`                                                                                                   | door 19's                                            |
| Set follow-up: door 18, `POST .../next-steps`        | `idempotency_key`, `party_id`, `what`, `due_on`, `replaces: "current"`, `seen_current_step: { next_step_id, change_id }` or `null`                                      | door 18's                                            |
| Claim, highlight: `POST .../prospects/{id}/changes`  | `idempotency_key`, `seen_change_id`, `fields`: any of `owner` (`joe`, `dell` or `null`) and `needs_attention`                                                           | `{ party_id, change_id }` or the rebased answer      |
| Turn into deal: door 16, `POST .../deals`            | door 16's body plus `prospect_id` and exactly one of `client_id` or `client: { name, kind, how_found }`                                                                 | door 16's                                            |
| Drop: `POST .../prospects/{id}/drop`                 | `idempotency_key`, `reason`                                                                                                                                             | `{ party_id, stage: "archived" }`                    |
| Mark reviewed: `POST /api/engine/weekly-reviews`     | `idempotency_key`, `week_of`, `seen_change_id`, one of `deal_id` and `party_id`                                                                                         | `201 { review_id }`                                  |
| Undo reviewed: `POST .../weekly-reviews/{id}/remove` | `idempotency_key`, `seen_change_id`                                                                                                                                     | `{ review_id }`                                      |

- **Add prospect.** Adds the lead role at `opportunity` with no owner, or moves an existing lead there keeping its owner, and stores the note, in one save. `activity_id` is null when no note was sent. A party already in the lane answers `existing: true`, and a note sent with it is stored as a new note about it. `how_found` is required whenever the party has none, and rule 63 refuses a lead without it (`422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "lead_or_client_how_found" }`). A party already on a deal in any role answers `409 not_a_prospect { party_id, deal_ids }`, so the app offers to open their deals. A client on no deal answers `409 already_client { party_id }`, so the app offers Add deal. A stored party with no source, including a copied `not_recorded` source, asks for how found. A supplied source leaves a known source intact.
- **Changes** follow the deal rule: `409 version_conflict { current_version }` when a later change touched the same field. `404 not_found` for a party with no lead role.
- **Turn into deal.** The chosen client gains the client role in the same save. If the partner chooses another or new client, the prospect joins as `client_contact`. The save keeps the prospect's notes and follow-ups on the original party. It moves any mark for the current Central-time week to the new deal, keeping the original reviewer and time (owner, 2026-11-19). Saving Add deal as a signed-in partner with an active prospect as the client also moves that week's mark. Retrying with the same key replays the answer. A party outside the lane returns `409 not_a_prospect { party_id }`.
- **Drop.** A blank reason is `400 missing_required`, and a party not in the lane is `409 not_a_prospect`. The move and the stage change save together under the lead's lock. An owner or attention edit for a lead that has left the lane also answers `409 not_a_prospect`.
- **Mark reviewed.** A `week_of` that is not a Monday is `400 invalid_field_value`. With the current version, marking a marked item answers `200 { review_id, existing: true }` and keeps the original partner and time. Mark and Undo both answer `409 version_conflict { current_version }` when the mark changed since the read. The version is the last change for that week and item, including an Undo, or zero when the item has never been marked that week. Undo answers `404 not_found` for an unknown review ID or when the supplied version is current and the mark has already been removed.
- **Lane read.** `GET /api/engine/prospects` accepts `?owner=all|joe|dell` (default `all`) and answers `{ count, prospects: [{ party_id, name, kind, how_found, owner, needs_attention, follow_ups: [{ next_step_id, owner, what, display_title, due_on, change_id }], latest_note: { summary, by, at } | null, change_id }] }` in lane order. `what` is the stored wording that the follow-up editor prefills. `display_title` is the wording to show, with an automatic check-in read as on Home. Either partner's filter includes unclaimed prospects. The count is for the selected filter.
- **One prospect.** `GET /api/engine/prospects/{party_id}` answers the lane fields plus `notes: [{ activity_id, summary, by, at }]`, every note about the party, newest first. Send the detail's `ETag` in `If-None-Match` on each five-second poll. The read answers `304` with no body if the detail is unchanged. The tag covers notes and follow-ups too. Use `change_id` for edits to the lead role. It is zero when the lead has no recorded change. A dropped prospect answers the same detail with `state: "dropped"` and `dropped: { at, by, reason }` from the archive move. `at` is an instant and `by` is the partner's user slug. The prospect page is read-only. Fresh prospect field, follow-up, conversion, and drop saves answer `409 not_a_prospect`.
- **Review read.** `GET /api/engine/weekly-reviews?week_of=` takes one Monday date and answers `{ items: [{ deal_id or party_id, state, seen_change_id, reviewed: { review_id, by, at } | null }] }`. Deal items also carry `name`, `type`, `owner`, `attention`, `client_id`, `client_name`, `account_client_id`, `account_name`, `account_owner`, `workspace_kind`, `phase`, `phase_started_on`, `phase_change`, `outcome`, `closed_on`, `lost_reason`, `invoiced_on`, `operating_state`, `parking_reason`, `parking_note`, `parked_at`, `parked_by`, `parked_automatically`, `next_date`, `market`, `seen`, `last_touch`, `next_step`, `items`, `market_agent` and `vendors`. `next_step` reads an automatic check-in as on Home, here, on the board and on the deal page. Only the read output changes. The stored wording stays as saved, and saves check `base_version` as before. Prospect items also carry `name`, `kind`, `how_found`, `owner`, `needs_attention`, `follow_ups`, `latest_note` and `change_id` as in the lane read. `seen_change_id` is the version of the weekly mark and is zero if the item has never been marked that week. A Dropped row also carries `dropped: { at, by, reason }`. Open the read-only prospect detail with the row's `party_id`. The row still supports Reviewed and Undo.
  The read covers open deals shown on the board in either workspace, plus prospects, regardless of owner. Active deals are ordered by their phase at the start of `week_of` in Central time, then by name. Prospects follow by when the lead reached `opportunity`, then active deals and prospects that arrive during the week, by arrival. Parked deals come last, also ordered by Monday phase and then name (question 13, owner, 2026-11-18). Older deals without a recorded Monday phase and prospects without a recorded Opportunity entry sort after the dated items in their group. The read keeps those items without supplying a missing phase or date. `state` is `open`, `closed`, `lost` or `dropped`, so items that left during the week keep their place. A new deal takes the prospect's place in the week it was turned into a deal (owner, 2026-11-19). It shows the prospect's carried mark, and either partner can undo it from the deal's review item.

These saves write only the new schema, so the lane and the review go live with the Deals switch. A prospect's follow-up stays with the prospect and appears on Leads with its late follow-up badge when applicable. Mark done uses the step's version and the ordinary end door. Slices, in order: 36, then 37 and 38 in parallel, then 39 after 30.

## Suggestions from mail, calls and meetings

Clear lead mail is an intake save. See [Leads from email](#leads-from-email) for the exception to partner review. Existing automatic filing likewise saves an email link through door 19. It does not accept a filing proposal.

`proposal` is the pending decision queue for a next step, deal phase move, new lead role on a known party, or weak lead-stage move. Typed columns and foreign keys constrain each payload. A source and payload are stored once, and a party suggestion requires an activity about that party. Stage suggestions retain their evidence strength. The source is an email, call or meeting activity with nonblank evidence. The originating job is retained independently of the current stamp. Only a direct user accepts or dismisses, once, and the database writes that user's id and decision time. The shared change history retains the original job and decision. Payloads and final decisions cannot be rewritten or removed.

Only dated next-step proposals can record `due_source` as `stated` or `defaulted`.

### Checklist suggestions

An open deal with no ordinary open step and no pending next-step suggestion gets one checklist suggestion from the workflow suggestions job. The door is `POST /jobs/deals/workflow-suggestions` with `idempotency_key`. The answer is `{ proposed, already_proposed }`. The same key replays that answer. Another job is `403 unknown_job`.

The proposal kind is `workflow_step`. It names the deal and the checklist to-do, with no activity and no owner. One pending row is allowed per deal. Evidence is `From the checklist · {label} is next in {phase}`, using the checklist's own label and phase name. The due date is today in Central time plus that to-do's typical length for the deal's kind, when a length is stored, and empty otherwise.

An ordinary open step is a next step with no checklist title. A checklist item step does not count. A pending next-step or checklist suggestion blocks another one. A filing, call log, or phase suggestion does not. Prospective Client and Due Diligence have no to-dos, so the job proposes nothing there, and it proposes nothing when the current phase's to-dos are finished. A parked, closed, invoiced, or lost deal is not an open deal. A later run counts a deal that already has a pending checklist suggestion and adds nothing. A to-do stays settled once a partner accepts or dismisses its suggestion: the checklist keeps no per-deal done state for most to-dos, so the job skips any to-do an earlier checklist suggestion on that deal named, whatever its decision, and proposes the next one or nothing.

Accept and Dismiss are the same decision as any other suggestion. Accept saves that wording and date as the clicking partner's step, or as the owner named in an edit. The suggestion is not that step. It stays off Home until a partner accepts it. The deal read returns pending rows as `workflow_suggestions`, each with `proposal_id`, `what`, `evidence` and `due_on`.

The to-do is the first unfinished, unsettled one in the current phase of the [checklist under the phases](#checklist-under-the-phases). The job and the deal read use the same checklist deal type and lease read, so before any suggestion is decided it is also the to-do the [deal plan](#deal-plan) places first.

The Studio calls the door once a day from carr-system's nightly run (`bin/nightly.sh` and a `tools/` script), the run that carried the [checklist reminders](#deal-watches-and-nightly-checklist-reminders). That call sits behind its own switch, which stays off until `schema/add-workflow-suggestions.sql` is applied.

Acceptance saves the next step, phase history row, lead role or applied stage-evidence row in the same transaction. A failure leaves the proposal pending. New next steps and applied stage evidence use the proposal's id. An accepted proposal that joins an open step records its id in `merged_step_id`. Phase moves check the expected current phase while holding the deal, and stage moves use the existing conditional lead update. A new lead requires an existing party, starts New and supplies how found if the party lacks it. Dismissal creates nothing. Corrections are new proposals.

The owner added the "log a call" suggestion kind on 2026-11-18. A `log_call` suggestion keeps a summary and cites the recorded call about its deal or party. For a shared copied call, acceptance saves a note on that target with the proposal's id, the cited call's time and the suggested summary. For any other call, acceptance keeps the text in the call's summary without filing a note. The cited call remains the only call activity (decision 151).

Today's `email_proposal` becomes this queue, with pending, confirmed and dismissed mapped to pending, accepted and dismissed. Its email activity, quote, text, due date and actors carry across; a phase proposal without its exact target waits for a user rather than inventing one. `capture_candidate` and `capture_post_call_candidate` next steps and lead suggestions use the same queue; confirmed `capture_post_call_action` rows become real next steps linked by id. Reports and session state in `capture_session`, `capture_post_call_report` and `phone_call_session` become source activity summaries and evidence references, without a second review workflow. `ops.meeting` and its notes become meeting activities; actions and revisions map to proposals and change history. Decision requests map to decisions only when a user actually decided. Meeting mode, its stream and processing leases are dropped. Reply drafts are neither suggestions nor outreach drafts; see "Reply drafts". `email_lead_exclusion` holds only a hash of the intake's local mailbox key for mail its model read as not business, so no email, sender or user decision can be found for it; each is listed and none becomes a suggestion. Historical accepted suggestions load with their existing real rows, without replaying acceptance. Missing source, job, payload or decision provenance is listed for a user, never fabricated. This slice defines that mapping; it does not copy production rows.

## Merging duplicates

The owner ruled on #196: the merged-away record disappears and everything points at the survivor. `merge_party(duplicate, survivor)` leaves a `merged_party` row, so an old link finds the survivor, moves every row that points at the duplicate, then removes it. Email matches need nothing more, because the duplicate's addresses are now the survivor's. The foreign keys then refuse any row that still names the duplicate, in any table, including tables added later. The merge finds what to move from the catalog, so a new table that points at a party moves with no change to it.

A conflict keeps the survivor's data and refuses what cannot be settled safely:

- Two people, two companies, or a person and a company? Only two of one kind merge.
- A user? A user is never merged away, because their changes name them. They can be the survivor.
- Both have an NPI? Refused. NPPES issues one number per person or organization, so two numbers are two of them. Otherwise the survivor keeps its own NPI or takes the duplicate's.
- The same email, phone number, role, specialty, company link, territory link or activity on both? One row stays, the survivor's, with its label and title.
- Both have a primary email or phone? The survivor's stays primary. If only the duplicate has one, it becomes the survivor's.
- Do not contact on either? The survivor is do not contact. If both are, the longer restriction stays with its reason, with no end date stronger than either date. Equal end dates keep the survivor's reason.
- Both on one deal in the same role? One link stays, and the duplicate's rounds move to it. In different roles, both stay.
- The survivor keeps its name. Outside ids, next steps, sent activity, social accounts and introductions all move.
- A knows or can-introduce link? It moves. One between the two records is dropped, and where both hold the same link, either way round for knows, the survivor's stays.
- Both leads? The survivor's lead role stays, with its stage and owner, and the duplicate's owner fills a missing one. Needs attention stays set if either lead had it. How found stays the survivor's, or becomes the duplicate's.
- Both vendors? The survivor's vendor role stays, with its stage, level and relationship partner. The duplicate's values fill any it lacks. The stricter disposition stays with its reason: avoid, then parked, then the survivor's. If either is a target, the survivor is, unless the merged disposition is avoid.
- A vendor and someone it introduced, or was introduced to? Refused, because an introduction joins two different records.
- A record merged into the duplicate earlier now points at the survivor, so pointers never chain.

A catalog test lists every unique key that holds a party. A new one fails it until the merge says which row stays.

## Change history

Every insert, update and delete of a stamped row writes one `change` row: the table, the record's key, whether the row was added, changed or removed, and the stamp of who made it and when. An added row's `new_value` is the whole row, and a removed row's `old_value` is the whole row. A changed row's hold only the fields it changed, from what to what. The stamp's own columns are left out, because the change's stamp names who and when. A statement that changes nothing writes nothing.

When a weekly review mark moves to a deal, both sides of its history entry include the week, so both items' mark versions advance. The entry names the partner who converted the prospect. The mark itself keeps its original stamp.

The key is the row's primary key, such as `{"id": …}` for a deal, or `{"deal_id": …, "party_id": …, "role": …}` for someone on a deal. `activity_about` and `post_review` have none, so their key is the whole row. Who moved a deal to which phase, and when, is the deal's `deal_phase_change` inserts: `table_name = 'deal_phase_change' and record ->> 'deal_id' = <deal id>`.

One trigger function, `record_change`, writes history for every table that carries the stamp, and a test fails when a stamped table has no history. It runs with the owner's rights, and no role but the owner may attach it to a table, so a temporary table cannot write history either. The engine's role reads `change` but cannot add, change or remove a row of it, so history is written only by changing a record.

A merge marks each change it makes with the duplicate it merged, in `merge_of`: each link it moves or removes, the duplicate's removal and its merged-into pointer. `merge_party` sets `engine.merge` for its own run, and the mark counts only when a merge of that record was saved in the same transaction, so setting it by hand cannot pass an edit off as an earlier merge's. Set by hand after a merge in the same transaction, it would mark an unrelated edit as that merge's; the change still names who made it and when.

Today's system keeps one `event` row per field changed, with the actor, a verb and a cause, and `/pipeline/changes` reads a deal's events. A change row holds every field one statement changed, so the old per-field rows are `jsonb_each` over its values. A copy of old rows, such as `schema/copy-parties.sql`, keeps each row's old stamp, and its history records the copy itself: an insert by `old_system`, at the time the copy ran. The old `event` rows come across separately as `imported_change`, described below. The deal page's events and the Updates page's Who did what read this table (§ "Updates and notification settings").

Only the schema owner's database role may call `erase_change(removed_change_id, fields)`. It requires a delete change and refuses a record that exists again. It removes the selected non-key text fields from old and new values for that table and record, across inserts, edits and deletions. Unknown fields, keys and non-text fields are refused. Other values stay. Keys, actions, merge pointers, authors and times stay unchanged. An `erase` change records the request with its user or job and time, with the erased field names and without copying their words. Repeating a completed erase changes nothing. The function locks the source table while it checks absence and erases, so a concurrent insert cannot recreate the record during it. The engine cannot call it or change history directly. Linked records and external copies are separate requests.

## Commissions

The owner answered walkthrough 4, topic E, on #196 (2026-11-16). A deal's gross commission, what the brokerage collects, is recorded once, on its `commission` row. An open deal can carry its expected commission there too, so the pipeline has a total. A gross is paid in parts, one `commission_payment` each, with its invoiced, due and received dates. Whether a payment is invoiced, unpaid, overdue or paid is read from those dates and today's date, never stored.

The split pays others first. Each `commission_share` gives a referring agent, the brokerage or anyone else a share of the whole. The two users split what is left. The closing user is the deal's owner. Their ladder, the `commission_tier` rows keyed by that user, gives their part by their lifetime count of closed deals, and their partner, named on the ladder, takes the rest. The count never resets: a deal is the user's nth closed deal if it is the nth they own whose latest phase is closed, by when it closed. A tier runs from its count until the next tier starts, so tiers cannot overlap or leave a gap. A single deal overrides its users' split with its own closing part. The shares and thresholds are the partners' rows, and real ones never appear in this repo, its tests or its docs.

`commission_split` works out everyone's share of each deal and its amount, so no user's take is typed. Each amount is the cents its running share of the whole adds, taking the others first and the partner last, so no amount is negative and together they are the gross. A deal that is not closed yet has no count, so its users' shares wait for it to close unless it overrides them. A gross is refused until its closing user has a ladder, because the ladder names the partner, even for a deal that overrides its closing part. The count and the ladder are read when asked, so a deal that closes, dies or changes owner moves the count for that owner's later deals, and a changed tier changes every deal it covers.

Moving today's rows across:

- Each deal's `commission` rows become one `commission` row. A row with status `expected` adds its `gross_amount` to the expected commission. Every other row becomes a payment of its `gross_amount`, with its `invoiced_on`, `due_on` and `received_on`, and the payments add up to the gross. A `received` row with no `received_on` is listed for a user to date.
- `deal.invoiced_on` becomes `imported_deal_invoiced`, which only the owner's copy fills, so a deal invoiced without a commission row stays invoiced. The invoice-email reconciliation below also moves it onto a payment it identifies. It is never a second editable date.
- `commission_allocation` has no rows, so nothing comes across. Its `referral_fee`, `house` and `other` kinds would be the referral, brokerage and other shares, and `partner_split` is the ladder.
- `sf_commission_placeholder` is not copied. Today's rule already keeps it out of every money figure.
- Today's fixed share per lane, in a report script, is replaced by each user's ladder. The partners enter their ladders themselves.

## Copying today's commissions

After `copy-deals.sql`, load the partners' ladders, then run `schema/copy-commissions.sql` as the owner in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. Load `copy-attribution.sql` first. Resolve deals through their `old_system` outside ids. Expected rows add to `expected`; all other rows become payments with their original ids and dates, and add to `gross`. An absent total stays null. No allocation, placeholder, share or ladder is copied.

Keep the final report for correction in the old system. A deal waits whole for an unmapped deal or author, missing ladder, nonpositive amount, overflowing total, receipt without a receipt or invoice date, invoiced status without an invoice date or with a received date, conflicting saved row or payment dates outside the database's order. A payment today's row leaves undated matches a saved invoice date only when a copied, applied invoice email gave it that date. Dates on expected rows are listed because they have no destination. The aggregate keeps the earliest creation time and latest row's author and change time, breaking ties by id; payments retain their own attribution and times. Reruns leave saved commissions and payments unchanged, including their change history, and list conflicts instead of overwriting them.

## Later slices

"Everything about this person." The rest of leads (#286). Indexes, once real sizes are known. Market figures from outside deals. Moving the rest of today's records across. For marketing: rotation rules, lead attribution and the weekly agent loop.

## Copying today's vendors

`schema/copy-vendors.sql` copies today's vendors as "Vendors" above says. The owner runs it after the party copy and before the deal copy, which finds the vendor roles, and again after the deal copy, so introductions and worked deals find their deals. The practice runner sends the whole SQL file to PostgreSQL. Each run is its own transaction with `search_path` set to the new schema. It only reads today's tables, and ends with its `not_copied` list.

- A vendor's party, found through the party copy's old-id map, takes the vendor role with the vendor's stage, level and target flag, and a role for its category. `outside_id` maps the vendor's old id to that record, so a second run copies only vendors it does not map yet. Its `vendor_ref` becomes an outside id under `old_system_ref`, as a party's `ref` does, unless another record holds it.
- Each `vendor_category` row is a vendor category, with `gc` as contractor, beside the roles of `deal_vendor_assignment`. Each `vendor_stage` row but the nine original stages is a vendor stage. A role or stage already in the new database keeps its label.
- A record's several vendors, such as those of merged parties or vendors, become one vendor role, as `merge_party` would join them: the first stage and level, the record's own vendor's first and then by when each was stored, the strictest ruling with its reason, a target on any unless the ruling is avoid, and every category. A different stage, level or ruling, a dropped target and a vendor added to a record copied before are listed.
- Today's merge joins two vendors of one party, and leaves a value that conflicted on the merged-away one. So that one joins its survivor here, after the live vendors. Its values a live vendor of the record also holds were filled in by today's merge, and are not listed again.
- Optionally supply `pg_temp.copy_vendor_ruling_reason(old_id uuid, reason text)` in the run's transaction with a reviewed reason for each parked or avoided vendor, by its old id. Keep it outside this public repo. The copy uses the trimmed reason or the reserved import reason when it has no nonblank reviewed reason. Two different reviewed reasons for one vendor are listed as `its reviewed reasons conflict`, and its whole record waits. A vendor whose party is missing also waits. A reason for an active or missing vendor is listed. A later run retries a vendor that waited and brings its note, introductions and worked deals. A reason supplied after a vendor was copied changes nothing.
- `originated` becomes `party_role.relationship_origin`, `rivalry_group` becomes `rivalry_note`, and `referral_active` becomes the nullable boolean `refers_business`. Text keeps its exact wording and whitespace. Each field takes the first non-null value in the same vendor order as stage and level; conflicting values are listed by their old field names. False stays false, and unknown stays null. No people, introductions, dates, links or restrictions are inferred. Reruns leave copied values and user edits unchanged. A later `merge_party` keeps the survivor's values and fills only nulls, as for offers and seeking.
- The copy maps `vendor.owner_id` through `outside_id` to an existing `app_user`. It keeps the first mapped partner in the same vendor order as stage and level. An owner id that fails to map or conflicts with the stored partner is listed. A label with no owner id is listed for review unless it is blank or says Unassigned. An owner id takes precedence over the label. The copy leaves the partner empty when no owner maps and preserves user edits on reruns.
- Offers and seeking keep the first nonblank value in the same vendor order as stage and level, with surrounding whitespace removed. Other nonblank values that disagree are listed. Verticals and loan programs are trimmed and unioned across the record's vendors, including merged-away vendors. Blank or null entries are listed, not assigned a substitute. Entries keep the author and times of the first vendor supplying them. Already copied roles and lists stay unchanged on reruns, including user edits and removals.
- These are listed too: an uncategorized vendor's free-text `category`, a level the new list lacks, `trust_override` and set `out_of_market`. Territory is handled and listed by `copy-markets.sql`. `enrich`, `deal_history_verified_at` and `links_label` are dropped. `last_touch` comes from contact activity; the copy creates no contact event from that date.
- A vendor's nonblank `intro_notes`, which today's vendor directory shows as Notes, becomes a note activity about its record, as a lead's notes do: its fixed id is `md5('old_system:vendor:' || id || ':intro_notes')::uuid`, dated at the vendor's last change and signed by the vendor's author. A merged-away vendor's note that its surviving vendor also holds was filled in by today's merge, and is not copied again. Only a vendor copied in that run brings its note, so a rerun adds none.
- Each worked deal in a copied vendor's `deal_evidence`, merged-away ones too, puts the vendor on the deal, in its category, or as a vendor when it has none. Each referred deal there is an introduction of the deal's client, made by the vendor, with that deal and its date in Central time. Each run revisits every copied vendor's evidence, and adds only what is missing. Evidence of another kind, or whose deal is not copied, is listed.
- Each introduction follows "Vendors" above, and keeps its old id, date and note. A `party_link_deal` row is the introduction of its link's other end, who is that deal's client, as today's referral binding checks, so a link with deals is copied through them and not again on its own. A referred deal in a vendor's evidence that a referral already holds is not copied twice. One whose parties are not all copied, whose maker is missing or is neither a copied vendor nor a user, with no single copied vendor on a side, or whose introduced party cannot be told, is listed. The reviewed partner alias in decision 205 applies when identifying introduction makers and interpreting brokered links. A link's author is kept as its author.
- Each `knows`, `can_introduce` and `works_with` link follows "Who knows whom" above, and keeps its old id, date, trimmed note with the shared copied-text rule and author. Links that become one, such as a knows link stored each way round, two current links of one pair, or the links of merged records, keep the first stored, and the rest are listed. So is a link whose parties are not all copied, one that joins a record to itself, a brokered can-introduce link without exactly one user end, an `intro_requested` or unknown kind, and, on every run, a knows link's broker.

The November 21 practice copy had 54 old links. It maps 5 of 9 `introduced` rows to introductions and lists the 4 remaining `introduced`, all 7 `intro` and all 4 `referral` rows for a partner because they lack a maker or a vendor. It kept 20 of 24 `knows` links and listed 4 duplicate pairs with the same start date. It copied all 6 `can_introduce` and all 4 `works_with` links. Those counts predate one current link per pair, so the next practice copy restates them.

For a database that already ran the old vendor copy, the owner can run `psql -X -f schema/backfill-party-links.sql` after approval. Keep `schema/copy-party-links.sql` beside it. The expected additions are 5 introductions, 10 links, 15 change-history rows. The backfill checks the six source counts and the absence of deal bindings before copying. It skips existing rows and reports the destination counts, the number of history rows written in that run, and the unresolved link IDs.

## Copying today's markets

Run `schema/copy-markets.sql` after `copy-parties.sql` and `copy-attribution.sql`, before the deal copy, in its own owner transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. It only reads `public`. The copy uses the existing market, county, territory and membership tables. No new schema rule is needed (rules 40–43).

Before loading the script, optionally create `pg_temp.copy_market_county (market_slug text not null, county_fips text not null) on commit drop` in that transaction. Supply each reviewed market's **complete** county list, one row per county. Keep that private input outside this public repo. With no input the script creates an empty temporary table and lists markets as `counties_not_listed`. An invalid FIPS refuses the whole market; an unknown slug is listed. Boundary text is never parsed into counties, states or distances.

A regular registry market with listed counties becomes a market with its exact name, a territory and one market piece. Duplicate registry names or a name already held by another market are listed. Stable ids derive from `md5('old_system:market:' || slug)::uuid` for markets and `md5('old_system:market_territory:' || slug)::uuid` for their territories. Later copies can resolve the same slug to that market id. The existing deal copy matches exact names, preferring trimmed city over the national assignment's market, so it needs no change. Aliases are listed; the copy does not guess that a city is an alias.

Memberships follow the party copy's `outside_id`, including merged-away parties, and become undated `party_territory` links. Their creation and change time is the source review time, not an invented membership start. Registry rows use their reviewer through `copy_attribution`; free-text territories use their vendor's author and times. A regional registry entry becomes an empty territory with its name and its boundary listed for drawing. A vendor's free-text territory becomes its own empty territory and membership, with its full text as the name and a drawing reminder. Missing parties are listed. The text territory id derives from `md5('old_system:vendor_territory:' || vendor.id)::uuid`.

The final `not_copied` report includes boundary and mapping failures, source text, office presence and service coverage, registry aliases and regional references. Regional references do not define geographic containment. Today's `submarket_condition` is a negotiation tightness list, not geographic submarkets, and is reported as `not_geography`. No separate geographic submarket table exists in today's schema. Rerunning preserves existing copied rows, stamps and history, including edited membership dates. Source-derived membership ids prevent undated duplicates after edits. A deleted membership can be copied again; a deleted market piece is left deleted. Already copied markets need no county input on rerun. A different supplied county set is listed as `market_already_copied` and requires a user correction. PostgreSQL supplies the copy and temporary input tables; no package is needed.

## Copying today's deals

`schema/copy-deals.sql` runs after the party copy, in a separate owner transaction with `search_path` set to the new schema. Load `schema/copy-attribution.sql` after `next.sql` first. It only reads `public`. Shared attribution carries the user, job and sponsoring user onto deals and links. Participants look up their deal's events; vendor assignments use the deal's `vendor_assignment` events. Phase history attributes each change by its own event through `copy_event_attribution`, so a later change to the deal cannot supply its cause or sponsor. Deals keep their ids and timestamps; `outside_id` under `old_system` makes a repeat leave copied deals unchanged. Types split as described under Deals. A missing owner refuses a deal, while an unknown market or nonpositive value is listed and left empty. Client links follow the party map, including merged-away ids. Known non-vendor participants become deal parties, with `landlord_side` becoming `property_owner`; vendor categories must already be vendor roles. On a repeat, new links on previously copied deals are copied too; ended participants copy into the earlier people and owner tables. Vendor assignments whose roles are not available are listed too. A market assignment with a verified `agent_party_id` follows the party map into a `market_agent` deal party, stored and changed when the agent was set and attributed to whoever set it through the deal's `market_agent` events. Its market names the deal's market as above; its stated name is not kept, since the person names the agent. One without a verified party is listed as `agent_not_verified` with its stated name. The separate source-note copier below keeps the assignment's plain source note.

Today's renewal deals have no recorded source lease. An ended deal with the old `renewal` type keeps `renewal_lease_unrecorded` in the owner-only `imported_deal_board` facts. The live reason and source lease stay empty, even if the renewal deal negotiated a new lease of its own. The report lists every missing source as `renewal_without_lease`.

Only the bulk importer's `system` actor leaves an earlier membership's start empty; people and live automation actors keep their recorded starts.

Each deal's `record_source` entries become `outside_id` rows on the deal found through its `old_system` mapping, including sources added after the deal was copied. Sources use the party copy's lower-case underscore normalization; keys keep their case and internal spaces but lose surrounding whitespace. Reserved `old_system` and `old_system_ref` sources, duplicate hints, invalid ids and uncopied deals are listed for correction. Several deals claiming one normalized source and key are all listed without choosing a holder. A key already held by another person, company or deal is also listed. Equivalent entries for the same deal copy once. Reruns leave outside ids and change history unchanged and repeat the correction list.

Parked deals keep `parking_reason` and their whitespace-trimmed `parking_note`, with blank notes stored as null. Active deals have neither. `attention` copies directly to `needs_attention`, including false. The owner load disables the user-only parking and flag trigger alongside the deal stamp and restores both before returning; it preserves the source attribution without inventing an acting user. Parked deals keep `parked_at` and a human parker as `parked_by_user_id` through the `old_system` user mapping. When the recorded parker is an automation actor, the copy sets `parked_automatically` and leaves the user empty. A parker with neither verified attribution stays empty and is listed as `parker_not_a_user`. This script creates no parking history or phase changes from those fields. Existing deal mappings leave all copied values and stamps unchanged on rerun.

Phase events keep their phase, stated reason and two times. The effective time `occurred_at` becomes `happened_at`. The save time `recorded_at` becomes the row's `changed_at`, which call capture reads as `phase_saved_at`. The native `change` entry records the owner's import under the `old_system` job. No-change events are ignored. A recorded loss becomes dead, using `not recorded before this system` when its reason is missing. A deal recorded as lost with no current loss event is recorded as dead at its last recorded change, the time at which its loss is known to hold. That gives it no invented close date. The copy lists a differing current phase. Without phase events, the current phase comes across as one phase change at the deal's last change, stamped by the `old_system` job. It is still listed as `no_phase_history`, because its start date is unknown. Unknown phases, dead without a reason and same-time events are listed.

A repeat leaves matching phase rows and their history alone. The copy also keeps each actual move marked automatic by the old Deals read. Its receipt stores the source event ID, previous phase, and reason against the exact copied phase row. A move with an unknown previous phase stays listed for correction.

The Deals board reads open, closed and lost lists with the same workspace and national account scope. On the first copy, `imported_deal_board` keeps the old card's typed city and the recorded phase, outcome and close date of historical deals. A standalone `market unspecified` or the old `Location #n — market unspecified` placeholder leaves that market value empty and is listed as `placeholder_market`. A real assignment can still supply the market. The first market change replaces the copied city on the card. The board and deal page show the same phase, outcome, and close date. A later phase change supersedes the copied facts. When a deal in Closed or Invoiced takes its win and close date from its latest move into Closed, the date comes from that move's `happened_at` in America/Chicago. Closed Deals includes every ended deal except losses, including copied deals in Closed or Invoiced with no recorded outcome or date. A recorded loss stays in Lost Deals even when its move to dead could not be copied. A payment with no invoice date does not by itself end a deal.

Rounds are numbered across each deal by old creation time, then old id. The refusal report retains original side and round number alongside that proposed number. Our side requires exactly one client party; the other side requires exactly one landlord, property owner or listing agent party. Properties are distinct buildings reached through premises and spaces. Missing or several candidates produce `no_sender_candidate`, `several_sender_candidates`, `no_property` or `several_properties`. A row can list several failures. Uniquely mappable rounds copy with `unknown` status and no response link. Building merges resolve to the final survivor, with cycles listed. Properties keep the survivor id, full street, city, state and ZIP address, and the building's name, class, type (`sub_type`), year built and stories, as the building copy fills them; a blank name, class or type stays unknown. The copy materializes their `deal_property` links first. Rounds keep their old ids, proposed dates at midnight in America/Chicago, expiries, normalized annual rent, term, per-square-foot TI, free rent and submarket condition. The full source row in `import_record` durably retains original side, round number and terms that have no typed destination. Import source and id identify repeat runs, which leave copied offers unchanged. Claims copy through the round’s import mapping, with type labels except deadline, words, author and creation time. Annual per-square-foot floors copy as stated and monthly per-square-foot floors multiply by 12. Unsupported sources or units, blank text, out-of-range floors, missing rounds, authors or sponsors, and existing claim conflicts are listed with the source row for review. Reruns leave copied claims unchanged (#394). A building's construction status, that status's source and its parcel have no destination and are listed as `unmapped_building_fields`. A sender with several matching roles is listed as `several_sender_roles`. A clashing round number or id is listed without aborting other rows. Escalator, options, operating-cost and general notes appear with labels in `other_terms`. A submarket condition other than soft, balanced or tight, gross rent and TI that cannot fill the typed columns are listed as `no_typed_destination`. Properties, links and offers keep source authors and times through `copy_attribution`. The app’s deal read excludes import provenance. No acceptance or response is inferred (#294).

### Copying market agent sources

`schema/copy-market-agent-sources.sql` runs after `copy-deals.sql` in a separate owner transaction, with attribution loaded and the new schema on `search_path`. Each assignment with plain source text becomes a note about its mapped deal, labelled `Market agent source`, followed by a blank line and the source text. Its fixed activity id is `md5('old_system:market_agent_source:' || deal_id)::uuid`. The note and its link keep `set_at` and the attribution from `set_by`; a matching `set-market-agent` event for `market_agent` or `create-national-market-deal` event with no field at that time supplies the sponsoring user, never a later event. An unknown agent sponsor is listed. Sources copy even when the agent is unverified; `copy-deals.sql` lists those identities for correction, and no person is created or matched by name. Missing deals and conflicting activity content or links are listed. Identical reruns leave notes alone; a changed source or an edited note is listed as `existing_note_conflict` for review rather than overwritten. Blank sources and standalone Salesforce opportunity references are listed by deal ID. If a comma or semicolon separates an opportunity reference from more text, the copy lists the prefix and keeps the remaining words. Valid ISO dates separated by whitespace, with optional surrounding prose punctuation, are shown in words and listed as `iso_date_rendered`. Dates inside filenames, identifiers or timestamps keep their written form. The copy keeps the assignment's current source. Events cannot recover earlier sources that were overwritten.

## Space needs, suites and building owners

`space_need` holds a deal's named requirement set with optional minimum and maximum square feet, a nonnegative finite yearly rent ceiling per square foot, space type, market and must-haves. Unknown bounds stay null, and a one-sided range is allowed. A deal can have several sets for alternative searches. Space type holds one type. Searches with several types wait for correction rather than being flattened into one. A vendor-backed need also keeps vendor_id, source_ref, source_revision and applicability together, all present or all absent. The engine can correct this provenance as a unit. The market uses the existing market list. Needs describe the client's requirements; `deal_property` continues to name candidate buildings.

`suite` is space within one `property`, with its label, floor, square feet, area basis and condition. Unknown suite labels remain null and are not treated as duplicates. Labels are unique within a building, so the same label can occur in another. Area basis keeps today's rentable, usable, county_heated and listed_unverified meanings. Area does not imply availability. `lease_suite` joins each signed lease version to zero or more suites by both suite and property. A composite key binds lease_version_id to lease_id. The lease and suite foreign keys both carry property_id, so every suite is in that version's lease identity's building. These stamped links have change history and cannot be rewritten or removed by the engine; a correction is another lease version.

`building_ownership` links a property to a person or company with start and end dates and a source. It retains today's owner, landlord_rep, property_manager and listing_agent kinds. Dates are inclusive, may be unknown and allow joint ownership. It does not pick a single owner for a building. Ending an ownership leaves its row; change history records corrections. A party merge moves these links and keeps the survivor's row when both hold the same property, kind and start.

The needs, suites and ownership tables have the shared user or job stamp and change history. The engine may correct their descriptive fields and ownership dates, and remove mistakes. Their deal, property and party links never change through ordinary edits, except that a space need's vendor provenance, including its vendor, can be corrected as a unit. A referenced suite stays while a lease points to it. This is schema design only; no production migration or search endpoint is added.

Today's rows map as follows. The copiers list unresolved values for correction rather than guessing or discarding them.

| Today's table           | New record and mapping                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `space_search`          | Each deal-bound row becomes a need named `Space need`, keeping id and created_at. spec.min_sf and max_sf map directly; max_rate_sf_yr maps to the rent ceiling. sub_types maps only when it has one type; several types wait for correction. Resolve cities and states to a complete county set and map to market_id only on an exact, unambiguous market_county match; otherwise list for correction. Client-only searches wait for a reviewed deal. statuses is an availability filter and is excluded with listings. Delivery status and report_path describe execution.                                                          |
| `ops.deal_space_search` | Preserve id, name and deal. criteria.min_square_feet and max_square_feet map to min_sf and max_sf. property_types follows the single-type rule above. Resolve counties and map only an exact, unambiguous county-set match to market_id; unresolved geography waits. requirements becomes must_haves. Nonempty query or entrance_verified must be reviewed into explicit requirements before copying; availability is excluded with listings. Draft criteria require confirmation. Archived criteria remain needs until explicitly retired. Cached result ids, run criteria, cursors, tenant keys and staleness are execution state. |
| `deal_client_need`      | Each approved, current, allowed, unwithdrawn row becomes its own need, with its id and deal_id, named `Client requirement`. description becomes must_haves, applicability stays structured, and vendor_id resolves through vendor.party_id and the party outside-id map. source_ref and source_revision keep their columns. No search row is overwritten or arbitrarily chosen. Preserve approval authors and dates in imported history. Pending, stale, restricted or withdrawn assertions wait for confirmation; never promote a proposal into a requirement.                                                                      |
| `space`                 | Preserve id and created_at. building_id maps to property_id, suite to label, floor directly, area_amount to area_sf, and area_basis and condition directly. Missing building mappings or duplicate known labels wait for correction. Preserve unknown labels and areas.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `building_ownership`    | Preserve id, created_at, party, kind and source. building_id maps to property_id, from_on to started_on and to_on to ended_on. Resolve parties through the existing outside-id map. Reversed dates and duplicate link keys wait for correction.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `premises_space`        | For each old lease's premises, every linked space becomes a lease_suite row for that imported lease version. Map space_id to suite_id and take property_id from its suite; the lease must name that same property. Zero links stays zero; several suites remain several links. Missing mappings and cross-property premises wait for correction.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

Both search formats preserve unknown keys in the correction list and refuse to copy an incompletely mapped search. Empty type or geography filters mean unspecified, not an invented value. `schema/copy-space-needs.sql` implements the space-need mappings, `schema/copy-buildings.sql` implements the suite and building-owner mappings, and `schema/copy-leases.sql` implements the lease-suite links.

Existing stamp attribution rules apply to these rows. `parcel` and `availability` are excluded by the owner's coverage ruling on #196. No listing or availability table is added.

## Copying today's space needs

Run `schema/copy-space-needs.sql` in a separate owner transaction after the attribution helper and the market, deal and vendor copies, with the new schema on `search_path`. It reads today's `public` and `ops` tables only. For each nonempty geography filter, supply `pg_temp.copy_space_need_county(source_table text, old_id uuid, county_fips text)` in that transaction with the complete, owner-reviewed county set for that search. Use `space_search` or `ops.deal_space_search` as source_table. A partial or ambiguous market match waits; no geography filter means no market. The script does not infer counties from city or county names.

An old `space_search` may name a client but no deal. Optionally supply `pg_temp.copy_space_need_deal(old_id uuid, deal_id uuid)` in that transaction with the old deal a partner reviewed for each such search; keep it outside this public repo. That deal must exist and belong to the search's client, and must already be copied; otherwise the search is listed as `deal_choice_not_found`, `deal_choice_client_differs` or `deal_not_copied`. Two different deals for one search, or a deal other than the one the search already names, are listed as `deal_choice_conflicts`. Every other check still applies. Without a reviewed deal the search stays listed as `deal_unassigned`.

The correction list retains unknown keys and blocks incomplete searches, multiple space types, invalid bounds, draft criteria and requirements still expressed through query or entrance verification. Availability, photos_available, public_projection_ready and execution state are excluded. client_prefill_source is source context, not a requirement, and is omitted; the mapped criteria already hold the requirements. Saved and archived searches remain requirements. Each approved, current, allowed, unwithdrawn client assertion copies separately, with vendor provenance, its creator on the imported insert and its approval author, date, revision and client in imported change history. An assertion observed after its approval waits for correction instead of copying with an invented time. Missing deal or vendor mappings wait. Existing need ids leave copied rows and history unchanged on reruns. Suites and building owners are copied separately (#362).

## Invoices found in email

`invoice_email` keeps derived invoice facts, not another email body. Its unique `evidence_ref` resolves to the source in the mailbox, as today's local-mail reference does. The mail job saves the facts for the mailbox's partner and keeps the first capture for that reference. The nightly lead stages list captured evidence and get a proposed target when the automatic checks agree. A selected `payment_id` must belong to its `deal_id`. An unknown payment waits for a user to identify or create it under the existing commission constraints. `invoice_mailbox` is the owner's trusted-sender list. The engine reads it but cannot change it.

The schema grants both engine roles execution of `invoice_email_target` and `invoice_name` so the nightly invoice list and application work with owner-only function defaults. The writer role can also execute `npi_check_digit_holds` and `is_time_zone`, which validate party NPIs and user time zones on save.

The `lead_stage` job moves captured to applied only for an exact normalized deal-name match corroborated by the client name or full property address. Every supplied corroborating field must agree, exactly one deal must match, the sender must be trusted and dates nonfuture, and existing payment and deal invoice dates must agree. Only one payment may be undated or already dated that day; several plausible payments wait for a user. A direct user resolves doubtful matches by selecting the target and applying. Applications on one deal serialize on its deal row; concurrent edits on other deals remain independent. The selected payment row is held while its prior date is captured and changed. No sender values are seeded here.

Application appends closed phase history and sets only the selected payment's `invoiced_on`. Its previous phase and date are saved for undo. A deal without phase history needs a user to establish its prior phase first. `deal_invoiced` reads the earliest payment invoice date, including manually dated payments; later installments keep their own dates. Receiving an invoice never sets `received_on`. A user moves applied to undone to restore the previous phase and date. A recorded payment receipt, newer phase work or any newer invoice-date change blocks undo. No evidence is deleted or reapplied; stamps and change history record capture, application and undo.

A different evidence reference for the same deal, payment and invoice date attaches to the original application as `attached`, with its database-written `application_id`. It keeps its own source facts, actor and capture history, but writes no phase or payment change and owns no undo snapshots. This also applies after the original is undone, so a resend cannot replay a user-undone close. Jobs attach only evidence that passes the same automatic checks as an original application; doubtful resends wait captured for a direct user. Attached evidence is final, including a direct user's selected target. Read the original application's status to know whether its close still stands. The deal lock serializes concurrent applications before looking up the original; a unique index also forbids two originals for that identity. A composite foreign key requires attachments to name an applied or undone original with the same target and date, including during owner imports.

Moving today's `deal_invoice_email` rows across maps created_by through the existing actor attribution rules, retains reason in imported application change history, and preserves id, evidence_ref, from_address as sender, deal_name, client_name, property_address, occurred_at and email_date. Captured stays captured. Applied and undone retain their states and their original application and undo actors and times in change history. Map deal_id through the deal copy, identify the commission payment without guessing an amount, and map phase_event_id and invoice_event_id to copied phase history and payment change history. Prior phase and invoice date become the snapshots. Load history through the owner's import with transition and stamp triggers disabled, without replaying closes or undo. Sender addresses that fail the new email-address shape, missing payment, phase, source or actor evidence are listed by record id for a user, never fabricated. Reconcile the old deal date with commission payment dates: attach it to an identified payment only when evidence proves it, list disagreements or dates without a payment, and never overwrite a different date silently. Undone records keep the restored date from current payments. The live mail job uses the capture door below.

## Leads from email

Mail intake reads `GET /jobs/leads/sources` with the jobs Access token and job name. It answers `{ senders: [{ address, domain, name, fields }] }` in name order. Each sender has either an exact email address or a lowercase hostname in `domain`. The engine roles can only read this list.

`fields` is null or an object mapping `name`, and optionally `request`, `email`, `phone`, `street_address` and `city`, to the corresponding labels in the notice body. A request label is optional because a known lead source's notice is itself the request for help. Labels must contain nonwhitespace text and may not end with a colon.

The mail capture job creates an unclaimed lead at New with how found set to inbound. Intake needs a recorded inquiry, a nonblank prospect name and at least one contact fact from the email: an email address, an E.164 phone number, or a street address and city that both appear literally in the evidence quote. A lead-source notice supplies the prospect's details in its body, including the full email address if used. Match parties by exact normalized email or phone. A name or street address alone never matches an existing party. Ambiguous, incomplete, internal and system mail stays in `lead_email_evidence` without creating a lead or a Home suggestion.

Use the exact Message-ID to find or save the source email. Save the lead, source email link through `activity_about`, and the initial **New from email** attention state in one transaction. `lead_email_source` records the prospect details, quote and mailbox partner. It requires a nonblank name, a valid contact fact and the source email's correspondence link to that lead. `lead_email_attention` has one row per lead and is pending while its acknowledgement is empty. Concurrent intake with a common email or phone and repeated intake of the same Message-ID converge. A known lead keeps its owner and acknowledgement. Eligible later emails join its correspondence and add newly verified emails and phone numbers for future matching, preserving the primary contacts already set.

The Leads board and drawer return `new_from_email` as true while the shared acknowledgement is pending. List and detail reads stay read-only. After the drawer opens successfully, the app can send `action: "acknowledge_email"` to the Leads changes route below.
