The first acknowledgement records the signed-in partner and time and leaves the owner and lead version as they were. Later acknowledgements succeed without replacing the first. An already acknowledged intake stays acknowledged on replay.

When bringing forward pending `email_lead` candidates, apply the same eligibility and exact source requirements. Keep candidates whose lead or source cannot be verified as intake evidence. Final proposal decisions stay in history without replay. See [Leads](screens/leads.md) for the screen behavior.

## Leads reads for the app

`LEADS_READ_SCHEMA=next` moves the Leads board and drawer to the new database. The board reads the lead role and the owner's user record. An unclaimed lead has no owner or owner label. A lead that becomes a client or joins the client side of a deal keeps its lead history and leaves the visible board.

The board uses the copied lead ID when available and otherwise uses the party ID. It shows the copied lead registry reference for Quiet follow-ups links. The drawer accepts either ID, including multiple copied lead IDs that resolve to one party. The drawer and reachability keep stored phone extensions, and reachability uses a stored mobile number when available. The drawer reads mail, calls and meetings linked to the party. Recent linked activity carries its recorded user or job into reachability. A reached call counts as successful contact, while mail still needs proof of an exact nonautomated inbound match. Stage history pages applied moves and ordinary stage changes by recorded event time, with the audit ID as the tie-breaker and cursor. The drawer reads the latest linked note for Plans.

When a role moves to a different party and arrives as a lead, the board, drawer and saves count that audit entry toward the destination lead's version. Later changes to that lead role advance its version.

The score and old prospect engagement fields are empty. Timing events and research findings are empty lists. The legacy Undo control is absent.

Each board lead carries `market`, the `id` and `name` of the one market whose name or one of whose `market_city` towns matches its city, ignoring case and surrounding spaces, or null when its city matches no market or more than one. A market named for an area, such as Eastern Shore, places leads only by its towns. This is the city half of the market rule; matching by county waits until a lead has a county (#1370). `market_counts` lists every market by name with its `id` and the `count` of board leads placed in it, zero included.

The drawer's `plans` text omits old research tags. The read keeps the original note in `notes`, with old record codes named as on the deal page.

`GET /api/engine/quiet-follow-ups` reads the new lead and active vendor roles and their current owners. It counts from the most recent linked email, call or meeting, or from the date the role was added when there is no recorded contact. Dates use the Central calendar day. A copied standalone lead follow-up date overrides the cadence until the step has a recorded update. An open step on the record or its linked client keeps the record off Quiet, including an edited copied step or a held or future step. Copied lead IDs and references still resolve to the same drawer. A lead without an owner stays unclaimed.

`GET /api/engine/leads/counts` takes no query parameters and answers `{ schema_version: "lead-counts.v1", new_from_email, follow_ups_late }` for Home's Leads tile. It counts the leads the board shows with no filter: a lead stage other than Archived, no active do-not-contact, not itself a client, not linked to a client and not on the client side of a deal. `new_from_email` counts those with unacknowledged email attention. `follow_ups_late` counts those with a quiet follow-up at the default cadence whose due day is before today's Central day. It has the board's sign-in and partner check, and both partners get the same counts.

With `LEADS_READ_SCHEMA=next`, `POST /api/engine/leads/:lead_id/changes` accepts the board's UUID, `expected_actor`, the lead's `base_version`, and an `idempotency_key`. Claim gives an eligible unowned lead outside Archived to the signed-in partner and clears any pending email badge in the same transaction. A concurrent claim using the same lead version receives `409 version_conflict`. Acknowledgement can succeed even if the supplied lead version has since changed. Stage edits use change history. An archive records the partner's reason in `lead_stage_move`.

For an existing schema, apply `schema/add-lead-email-ack-grant.sql` after production approval. The grant covers only the two acknowledgement columns. Until it is applied, eligible Claim and acknowledgement saves return `503 database_unavailable` and roll back. Reads keep working. Fresh schemas include this grant.

The board suggests clients with the same name after trimming and ignoring case. Link stores the chosen client's exact party ID in `party_role.lead_client_id`. The database lets a direct partner set the link once to an existing same-name client on a different party. If the selected client exists and its name no longer matches or was never a match, the save returns `409 client_not_suggested`. The card leaves Leads. Both parties keep their roles and histories, and the link has no effect on Deals Prospects.

On an existing `next` schema, apply `schema/add-lead-client-link.sql` as the schema owner before deploying the engine that serves these saves. Fresh schemas include the column and its constraints.

On an existing `next` schema, apply `schema/add-market-cities.sql` as the schema owner before deploying the engine that reads market towns, then `schema/seed-market-cities.sql`, each after the owner approves it for production. Both can run twice. The seed lists the towns of Orange Beach / Gulf Shores, Eastern Shore and 30A / Santa Rosa Beach, as the owner ruled on #1374, by market name, and skips a market that does not exist yet. Fresh schemas include the empty table.

## Copying today's leads and clients

Run `schema/copy-leads-clients.sql` as the owner, after the party copy and attribution helper, in a separate transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. Run this copy before `copy-deals.sql`, or rerun the deals copy afterward so it can attach client roles. It only reads `public`. It copies roles, client profiles and national accounts, working details, notes, next actions and reference numbers. Existing old-system role maps keep a repeat run from changing copied records. Merged party ids resolve through `outside_id`; merged client rows are listed and skipped.

Before running the file, create this temporary source mapping in the same transaction. Fill it with reviewed exact old values. The file seeds the approved how-found choices, including event. For an additional choice, add its `how_found` row first.

```sql
create temp table copy_how_found (
 old_type text, source text, how_found text,
 primary key (old_type, source)
) on commit drop;
insert into copy_how_found values ('lead', 'Example reviewed source', 'referral');
```

For a reviewed client owner, supply `pg_temp.copy_client_owner(old_id uuid primary key, owner_actor_id uuid not null)` with `on commit drop` in the same transaction. Use the old client ID and the partner's old actor ID. Keep the reviewed rows private.

`old_type` is `client` for acquisition source, `lead` for ordinary lead source, and `candidate_pool` for a prospect-pool lead's unique promoted candidate source. Missing mappings are listed in `not_copied`; a party with no mapped source gets no lead or client role. Client testimony takes precedence when mapped sources disagree, and each disagreement is listed. A nonblank client source without a mapping makes the party wait without new roles, notes or a lead-derived source. Any existing source and roles stay; additional copies wait for the client mapping. Its source and detail are listed until mapped; rerunning then uses the client answer. Unrecognized client statuses, including prospect, and types wait without creating a client role or profile. An unknown lead stage likewise waits without notes or follow-ups. The old client type `group` maps to `group_practice`. The newest lead supplies stage and owner; the earliest row supplies role creation time. Notes from duplicate rows remain separate activities. Unknown types and owners, incomplete estimates, file references, standalone last-touch dates and undated brand or account-owner links are listed for correction. No contact event or history date is inferred.

For suppressed or `do_not_contact` leads, an optional `pg_temp.copy_lead_restriction(old_id uuid not null, reason text)` supplies partner-reviewed reasons in the same transaction, with `on commit drop`. `old_id` names the old lead, not the party. Keep the private reviewed rows outside this repo. Without a reviewed reason for the party, the copy keeps the restriction with `not recorded before this system`. A supplied null or whitespace-only reason is listed for correction. The copier trims surrounding whitespace and accepts identical repeated reasons. Different reasons for one lead or surviving party, or an existing restriction with a different reason or any end date, are listed as `restriction_conflicts`. Existing restrictions stay unchanged. Unrelated input rows do nothing.

A copied restriction follows the old party's `outside_id` mapping to the surviving party, independently of whether the lead's source or stage can be copied. It has no end date because the old suppression instruction has none. It uses the lead's last update for both restriction timestamps and keeps its attribution, because the old system records no suppression time. Rerunning can fill a missing restriction after review, even if the lead role was already copied; it never updates an existing restriction or adds duplicate history.

This is the first part of #329, tracked in #333. `schema/copy-candidates-drafts.sql` copies candidates and outreach drafts (#334), and `schema/copy-lead-stage-moves.sql` copies stage evidence (#335). Copy contact activities before resolving listed last-touch dates.

### Copying today's client research and practice types

Run `schema/copy-client-research.sql` after the lead and client copy, as the owner in a separate transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. Research and practice types follow old client merges to the surviving client role. Identical practice types collapse to one row with the surviving client's stamp when present. A single recorded subtype follows that practice type, including when the practice type was already copied. Conflicting practice types or subtypes wait for review. The copy lists missing roles and invalid values by ID and reason.

Research keeps the old finding ID, observation time and expiry. The original research kinds keep their source locator or URL. The stamp uses the usual user or job attribution and the observation time. Each named fact has its own row. The original research kinds retain their JSON types, including nested facts. The observation and its facts copy together. Reruns leave copied rows and history as they were. A later party merge carries research forward and keeps one copy of a shared practice type. A missing subtype takes the other client's subtype. If the practice types or two recorded subtypes differ, `merge_party_client_verticals` refuses the merge until a user settles them.

Identity candidates use the same observation ID, dates and attribution as other imported research. Each successful old result stores one named fact (`npi`, `address`, `social` or `website`) containing the structured result, plus a `locator` fact with the old source URL or registry reference. The observation uses source `found by research tool`. An NPI recorded as a JSON number is stored as text digits. An NPI already held by the client is omitted and counted, including when the result has other candidate numbers. A search with no candidate is listed by ID. Client addresses, research social links and public websites have no authoritative client fields in this schema. The property, publishing and deal portal records serve their own purposes.

| `record_flag.practice_research`                    |     1 | `client_research` and its facts                                                                                       |

## Copying today's candidates and outreach drafts

Run `schema/copy-candidates-drafts.sql` as the owner after `copy-leads-clients.sql`, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. It reads the source rows in `public`, follows the mappings under "Leads and clients", and lists missing values and records in `not_copied`. A rerun only inserts new drafts, candidates and their suggestions.

A pending Outlook candidate becomes an email lead suggestion when it has a valid sender address, a mapped mailbox user and an unknown sender. Missing mailbox users, invalid addresses and known contacts are listed by candidate id. Those candidates still copy as unreviewed. The copy keeps the old context and brings the address, message reference, first seen time and reading to the top of `source_row` for the mailbox user. The suggestion stores the original summary and carries the mailbox owner's `mail_capture` stamp. A reading about an existing deal stays with the candidate so the partner can see it and dismiss the suggestion. Accepting any of these suggestions creates a new inbound lead.

A draft or candidate finds its lead on the party the lead copy recorded. Its history is written from today's facts, not from the load: a draft's creation by its creator and its approval by the approving user; a candidate's finding by its creator, as unreviewed when a decision came later, then its confirmation and its decision. The row names whoever made the latest of these, and when. A promotion's or email confirmation's user comes from its status event, and a decline's from `declined_by`, each through a sponsored agent where one acted. A creation takes the event its own transaction recorded, so mail capture keeps its job and sponsor. An expiry takes the Clear the deck drop that named the candidate, or else the row's last change. Today's rows record no expiry basis, so every expired candidate takes "Expired by Clear the deck". A draft's earlier edits stay in today's events; the copy keeps its current text and revision.

A draft waits when its lead was not copied, several drafts map to one party, the party already has another draft, its owner is not a user, its subject, body or time zone is blank, or its approval names no user. A candidate waits when it is an email confirmation without a lead, its source or key is blank or untrimmed, another candidate holds its source and key, its name or qualification is blank, a decline or duplicate has no reason, a promotion or decline has no recoverable user, or its promoted lead was not copied. An estimate without a source, a source without an estimate and a confirmation without a user are listed, and the candidate copies without them.

## Copying today's invoice emails

Run `schema/copy-invoice-emails.sql` after the deal and commission copies and the attribution helper, in its own owner transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. It only reads `public`. Capture events supply the storage time and original actor; application and undo keep their times, actors and reason in explicit change history. The load disables transition, stamp and automatic history triggers for this transaction, following decision 73. It never replays a phase move. When invoice evidence identifies one undated payment, it moves the old deal invoice date onto that payment with the original event stamp. A different payment date is never overwritten.

A targeted invoice needs exactly one copied payment whose source ledger date is unknown or agrees with today's deal date. Applied invoices require that date to equal the email date; undone invoices require the restored prior date. Phase and invoice events must describe that deal's close and invoice date, with the prior phase present in copied history. Missing capture evidence, actors, deals, payments or phase history, invalid facts, conflicting saved evidence and duplicate historical applications are listed by invoice id for correction. The earliest application for a deal and date copies; later historical applications are listed rather than rewritten as resends. An old deal date the copied deal's invoiced date does not match is listed, even when no invoice email exists. Reruns compare typed facts and application history, preserve copied rows and history across time zones, and list source conflicts. Undone invoices retain payment restore history. PostgreSQL's transaction controls and the existing `pg` test harness handle the copy without a new dependency.

## Copying today's activity

Run `schema/copy-activity.sql` as the owner after `copy-parties.sql` and `copy-deals.sql`, and before `copy-lead-stage-moves.sql`, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. It only reads `public` and ends by listing in `not_copied` what it did not copy.

- The spreadsheet import's Last Touch date stamps are listed by ID and left in the old system. They record dates with no contact details, so they supply neither partner notes nor evidence of a call or meeting.
- Today's calls, meetings and notes keep their kind, and a tour becomes a meeting (see "Tours as meetings"). Each keeps its id, its time, and who last changed it, read from its `log-activity` event so an agent's or the mail job's work names that job. `from_party_id` stays empty, since today's row names who logged it, not who it came from. The summary is today's summary, then its detail after a blank line, then what the row says is still missing, such as a figure or a name, under the line `Information still missing`. Notes, calls and meetings use the shared copy rule for dates. That text never becomes a next step and names no one who owes it.
- Each deal, client, lead, vendor or party the row names becomes one `activity_about` row, created when the row was recorded. A client, lead or vendor is about its person or company, found through the old-id map, so a merged-away record reaches its survivor. A link whose record is not copied is listed. A row with no copied record is listed and waits.
- Following the coordinator's ruling on #363, the old kinds `text`, `analysis`, `loi`, `counter_sent`, `counter_received` and `lease_signed` become notes. Each note opens with its kind's label from today's `activity_kind` list, the word the partners see, such as `LOI` or `Counter Sent`, then a blank line and today's text with the shared copy rule applied.
- A deal note of kind note becomes a note about its deal, at its time, signed from its `add-deal-note` event on the deal, which has the same text and time. An agent's activity or note whose partner is unknown is copied as the agent's and listed.
- A deal note of kind next step records what was written each time a next step was set or restored. Every one, older, superseded or current, becomes a note about its deal at its time that opens with `Recorded next step`, then a blank line and its text with the shared copy rule applied. It is signed from the deal's `next_step` event that wrote it, not from `add-deal-note`. That event has the same actor and text, started before the note and was recorded after it. A write waiting on the deal's lock can start before an earlier note, so a note and an event pair only when each is the other's nearest such match. A note with no single pair stays unsigned, and an agent's is listed. It makes no next step, due date or completion; the next-step copy decides those.
- An old deal note with a list of dated entries copies as one note. Each entry keeps its words in source order, with the shared copy rule applied. Exact dates display in words and approximate date labels keep their original wording. Empty notes, including empty lists, and lists that cannot be parsed in full are listed by ID.
- Old `activity.connected` copies unchanged into `activity.reached`, including texts kept as notes. True, false and null stay distinct. A rerun leaves copied outcomes and later edits untouched.
- Emails, and the notes the mail intake files with an email's full text, are left to the email copy below. A note the mail intake files with an email's links is copied as a note about its deal. Listed and not copied: the old kind `task`, which waits for an owner ruling. Listed but copied without the value: a detail that is a JSON object, which is a job's own record. `source`, `version` and `calendar_event_key` record how today's system captured the row and are dropped.
- A rerun copies only rows not yet copied, and adds the links of records copied since, so an unchanged rerun changes nothing.

## Copying today's emails

Run `schema/copy-emails.sql` as the owner right after `copy-activity.sql`, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. It reads legacy rows from `public` and optional private input from `pg_temp.copy_email_evidence`. It ends with `not_copied`.

- Today an email filed to several records is one `email_in` or `email_out` row per record, and mail both partners filed is a row per mailbox. Rows with the same Message-ID are one email. Mail filed before today's system kept the header (carr-system #3233) has none. A blank legacy header also counts as missing. Such a row is the email with the same sender, exact sent time and subject, ignoring only the subject's case, as today's intake does. It joins the email with a Message-ID that matches it, if only one does. No Message-ID is guessed, and a row filed later can bring one to an email copied without it.
- Each email becomes one email activity, keeping the id, time, summary and signature of the row already copied, or else of its earliest recorded live row. It gets one `email` row and an `activity_about` row for every record its live rows name. A link keeps the time and signature of the earliest row that names it. The email keeps the first known Outlook conversation id from its live rows' `email_context`, preferring the row whose id it keeps.
- Each live source row adds its known user to `email_mailbox` on the copied email. Each membership is stamped `old_system` with the earliest recorded time among its live source rows. The copy reads the mailbox from the row's email source or identity, or from the stored mailbox account if it matches one user. An older row can use its human author or its own `log-activity` event when the author is that partner's local agent and the event names that partner as sponsor. An email with no known mailbox is listed as `mailbox_unknown`.
- The sender is the first known sender address among its rows: today's sender address, or the partner's mailbox address for sent mail. The subject is today's subject, or the summary after its `Email from` or `Email to` label. The body is the full-text note filed for it, or else its longest preview. Either is today's whole text, quoted history included, since removing it would be a guess.
- Before the copy, you can load `pg_temp.copy_email_evidence` in the same transaction with columns `old_id` (UUID), `sender` (text), `subject_start` (integer) and `message_id` (text). Use the sender, subject boundary and any original Message-ID verified against the old activity's original write key in the old intake ledger or Outlook index. Leave disputed Message-IDs null. `subject_start` is the one-based character position in the old summary. The copy validates the input and gives stored legacy fields precedence. A rerun uses `outside_id` to keep previously copied rows with their emails, even when you omit the private input.
- Deleting is per mailbox, so an email is copied while any mailbox's row of it is live. Listed and not copied: each row without a Message-ID or a sender; each row without a Message-ID that matches several emails with one; each row recorded as deleted, recalled or superseded; an email with no sender address; an email about no copied record, and each link of a live row whose record is not copied; a full-text note that names no single email; and any other row that carries email context.
- `outside_id` maps each row of a copied email, under `old_system`, to the email activity it became, so a later copy that names one of today's rows finds its email.
- A rerun adds any new record links and mailbox memberships. It fills a missing Message-ID or Outlook conversation id when a source row supplies it.

## Copying today's email summaries

Run `schema/copy-email-summaries.sql` as the owner after `copy-emails.sql`, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. It looks up each `public.email_message_summary` result using the source activity's `old_system` email mapping in `outside_id`. It copies each result's original key, status, text, provenance and `updated_at` without changes. When several source activities became the same email, their results stay distinct on that email.

The copy lists an unmapped source as `email_not_copied`. The list names the source activity, generator version and source hash. A stored result with the same original key but different values or email is listed as `summary_copy_conflict` and stays as stored. A repeat with the same input adds nothing.

## Copying today's email attachments

Run `schema/copy-attachment-emails.sql` as the owner right after `copy-emails.sql`, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. It only reads `public` and `ops`, and ends with `not_copied`.

- Today's intake marks each attachment it files to a deal with an `attach-deal-file` event, and names the email of an outside sender's attachment in an `ops.requirement_reply_proposal`. A partner's own mail names none.
- The copy links each deal file to every copied email its proposals name, using the `outside_id` map. An attachment with the same bytes as an earlier filing links the email to the retained file. The file keeps its original stamp.
- The correction report identifies unresolved links by attachment id and, when known, the source email activity id. It lists missing or deleted attachments, files with no copied document, and unknown or uncopied emails. A successful link to another email does not clear these entries. A rerun adds newly resolvable links and keeps existing links.

## Reply drafts

`reply_draft` is a reply to one email, which its owner's mailbox sends. It is apart from outreach drafts, which a user sends by hand, and from suggestions, which create records. It keeps the email it answers, its owner, its origin, the quote that asked for a reply, its subject and body, its attachments in `reply_draft_document`, and a revision. Its delivery is one status: pending, dismissed, approved, sent or failed. Approval freezes the text and attachments with the one recipient and one copy shown to the owner. A sent draft keeps when it went and the sending mailbox's id for the message. A failed one keeps its reason, which decides what it may become, as in today's sender: `aborted_before_dispatch` and `submission_recipients_invalid` never pressed Send and may be approved again with corrected text, recipients and attachments; `unconfirmed_after_<seconds>` pressed Send without seeing the message in Sent Items and may only be found sent; any other reason stays failed. Recording sent clears the reason, which change history keeps. The revision is door 22's `version`: each change its owner makes advances it, approval included, and a recorded try never does, so the outbox keys each submission on draft id and revision. Rule 99 holds the moves, so a draft goes out at most once.

Apply the [reply draft origin migration](../schema/add-reply-draft-doc-origin.sql) after the Doc confirmation grant on an existing database. Every draft has exactly one origin, either `proposed_by_job` or `confirmed_doc_turn_id`. A Doc draft's turn belongs to the same owner and can create at most one draft. The insert requires that owner's confirmation of a reply proposal for the exact email. Set the turn's confirmation key before inserting the draft in the same transaction as the receipt. The engine writer can set the origin at insert. Later edits preserve it.

For #1048, the router sets a new automatic draft's subject to `"Re: " + email.subject` using the exact source email it sends to door 22. The old router's prompt uses the activity summary as the subject (`lib/email_router.py`, `fenced`), and the engine adapter passes the proposed subject through (`lib/engine_router.py`, `store`). The partner can edit the draft's saved subject in review.

Repair the two affected pending drafts with a private, guarded file. Derive each replacement from the stored source subject. Check the exact draft id, source activity id, pending status, expected revision, and old subject under lock. Apply both changes in one transaction and advance each revision as for a pending edit. Abort on a mismatch and count the results. A rerun recognizes the two exact repaired revisions and makes no changes.

Run `schema/copy-reply-drafts.sql` as the owner after `copy-emails.sql`, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path` and attribution loaded. It only reads `public` and ends with `not_copied`.

- Each `email_proposal` reply draft finds its email by its row's id or, when the email copy kept another mailbox's row, by its Message-ID. Production holds pending, dismissed and failed drafts, which keep their status. A failed draft keeps the text it was submitted with, its lowercased recipient and copy, and its reason.
- The old system drafted each one, so it is drafted by `old_system` at its creation time. The decider and time are today's resolver and resolution time, and the try is recorded by `old_system` for the owner at its result time. Change history holds the draft, its decision and its try.
- Listed and not copied: a draft approved to send, sent or marked handled, none of which production holds; an owner who is not a user; an email not copied; blank evidence, subject or body; a decision by someone who is not a user, or an approval not by its owner; recipients that are not one valid address each; a submission with attachments, which today are files on a partner's computer, not stored documents, and which production has none of; a failure without a reason or result time; and a draft that changed since it was copied or answers the same email for the same owner as another.
- Listed but copied without the value: a snooze, which waits for Home marks (10), and a resolution note. Confidence, match evidence, and the deal it was filed under, which is its email's link, are dropped.
- A rerun leaves copied drafts and history as they were, and copies a draft whose email was copied since.

## Copying today's calls

Run `schema/copy-calls.sql` as the owner after `copy-activity.sql`, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path` and attribution loaded. It only reads `public` and ends with `not_copied`. It is the source activity the call suggestions copy needs (decision 83).

- Each `capture_session` becomes one activity with the session's id, a call when it ran the post-call workflow and a meeting otherwise. Its time is when recording started, and it is signed by the session's actor at its last state change, so a partner's phone import names that user and a recording device names `old_system`. A `phone_call_session` shares the session's id, so a phone call is one activity.
- The summary is the phone report's summary, then its decisions and open questions as lists after a blank line. A meeting capture's summary is its one meeting record's summary, then its detail after a blank line; several records with text are listed and the summary stays empty. A skipped record, and any record beside a logged one that no activity carries, is listed. Post-call sessions stored no report text, only `capture_post_call_report`'s hash, so their summary is empty and the hash is listed.
- A meeting capture with a confirmed meeting record that names an existing activity is that activity, the greatest one if several, which the activity copy carries. It is not copied again; it gains the links of its session's other suggestions once that copy has carried it, until then it is listed, and the suggestions copy uses it as their source.
- A call is about each deal, person or company its suggestions name: a post-call suggestion's deal, and a capture suggestion's deal or record when it is an id. A client, lead or vendor is about its person or company. A name or a record not yet copied is listed, and a call about nothing copied waits.
- Listed and copied without the value: an unfinished or failed session, the recording's hash, since the audio is not stored, and the Call Studio review state. Device, nonce, token, expiry, consent time, file facts and the outcome review are capture machinery and are dropped.
- A rerun copies only sessions not yet copied, and adds the links of records copied since, so an unchanged rerun changes nothing.

## Copying today's call suggestions

Run `schema/copy-call-suggestions.sql` as the owner after `copy-calls.sql` and `copy-deal-next-steps.sql`, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path` and attribution loaded. It only reads `public` and ends with `not_copied` (decision 86).

For an existing database, apply `schema/add-call-review-merge.sql` before copying call suggestions.

- A `capture_candidate` next step or phase move and a `capture_post_call_candidate` assigned action become a `proposal` on their deal, cited from the call or logged meeting `copy-calls.sql` made of their session, with their quote as evidence. Pending, confirmed and skipped become pending, accepted and dismissed. The old system's call capture proposed each one, so it is proposed by `old_system` at its creation time; the deciding user and time are today's resolver and resolution time. Change history holds the pending insert and, when decided, the decision.
- An assigned action's owner is its assignee's user. A phase move goes from the phase its deal had before the call, read from the base event capture recorded, to its stated phase.
- For dated assigned actions, `due_defaulted` false becomes `stated`, true becomes `defaulted`, and null stays null. Undated actions and `capture_candidate` suggestions keep null provenance. On a rerun, the copy preserves the proposal and reports `existing_proposal_conflicts` if recorded provenance differs from the source. It keeps null provenance on earlier proposals and skips the provenance comparison for those rows.
- A pending next-step or phase-move suggestion without an assignee retains its session's reviewer when the session names a user or a recorder assigned to one. The recorder mapping follows the old `ops.resolve_action_card` review lookup. [Carr-system migration 1168](https://github.com/DoctorCRE/carr-system/blob/7a8ca32e737a89b98b3bccbac9d55f021f3c25e8/migrations/1168_post_call_capture_recorder_versions.sql#L59-L69).
- The 10 pending "log this call" suggestions from three unreviewed calls are old `capture_candidate` activity notes. They become `log_call` proposals with their old ids, deal, summary, evidence and creation time, as the owner directed on 2026-11-18. The copy stamps the Studio recorder's suggestions as proposed by `old_system` for Joe, who reviews those calls under carr-system migration 1168.
- An accepted next step is not made again. Its proposal takes the id of the copied next step it made, found by its result or by the deal items and post-call action that name it, as acceptance would have. A step another suggestion made, or merged into, stays that suggestion's.
- Listed and not copied: new deals, other activity notes and email drafts; an applied suggestion that was undone; a target or source activity not copied; a call step or log whose source lacks the target link; a log with a source other than a call; blank evidence or text; an invalid due date; an assignee or resolver who is not a user; a phase move to an unknown or dead phase, to its current phase, or without its prior phase; an accepted next step whose step was not copied, made several steps, or shares one; and a suggestion that changed since it was copied or has another's payload. Meeting records are the session's activity and are left to `copy-calls.sql`.
- Listed but copied without the value: a resolution note, a phase move's start date, a follow-up's contact and a call suggestion's own `occurred_at`. The accepted note uses the recorded call's time. Confidence is capture machinery and is dropped.
- A rerun leaves copied proposals and history as they were, and copies a suggestion whose deal, activity or next step was copied since.

## Copying today's preferences and Home marks

Run `schema/copy-preferences.sql` as the owner after `copy-leads-clients.sql` and the deal next-steps copy (#366), in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. It reads `public` and `ops`, follows "Moving today's rows across" under "Users' preferences and Home marks", and ends by listing in `not_copied` what it did not copy, keyed by the old actor.

- A preference whose actor is not a user, whose zone the database does not know, or whose quiet hours start when they end waits whole, so no zone or quiet hours are invented. Today's row keeps no time, so its user is named at the time of the copy (decision 79).
- A mark finds its next step by the next action's or post-call action's id, which the copies keep, on the action's deal, or on the person or company its lead, client or vendor became. A disposition matches `item_id` or `v2_item_id` with the old Home's exact-id, latest-change and item-id precedence. Waiting matches only `item_id` and combines with the disposition on that item. A step with that id on another record is not the action's copy. Until the next-step copy has run, marks on a deal's next actions and post-call actions are listed as `next_step_not_copied`, and a rerun after it adds them. A mark also waits when its actor is not a user, its item is neither a next action nor a post-call action, the one it waits on was not copied, or its reason is blank. A mark is its user's, from its earliest to its latest change today.
- A user's copied preferences and marks are never changed again, so an unchanged rerun changes nothing and a corrected row arrives on the next run.

## Copying today's deal and vendor next steps

Run `schema/copy-deal-next-steps.sql` as the owner after the party, vendor, deal, activity and call copies, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path` and attribution loaded. It only reads `public` and ends with `not_copied`.

The copy leaves behind the exact old automation template that says its row exists because the partner screen cannot clear it. The report lists the step ID and the `text` field as `automation_note_step`. The matching set-action event is also listed by ID, so the note stays out of the deal Timeline.

Other no-outstanding-work notes lose an abbreviated step code only when the code names another action on the same deal and is separated from the remaining words. The report lists `technical_step_code`. A deal action stored as a recognizable Python dictionary with a valid due date copies its text, any waiting-on words on a separate line, and the typed date the Timeline shows in words. The report lists `serialized_next_step`. Ambiguous dictionaries and conflicting dates keep their original words and recorded date.

Deal next actions keep their ids, descriptions, owners and nullable due dates. Each copies with its end state, taken only from the old event that closed it, and never from a last edit or the copy time:

- A done action takes the time of its latest completion evidence, ordered by mutation order. `complete-one-action`, `complete-deal-item` and `complete-action` name the finished action. `log-outreach` names only the first finished action in `new_value.completed`; its `next_action` is the new step, and other finished next actions keep no time. A later `reopen-one-action` removes that proof. Without proof, completion time stays null. A post-call action's explicit `completed_at` still takes precedence.
- A dropped action is cancelled with the reason, time and id of its `drop-action` event when it has exactly one, with a reason, and the row's version is still the one that drop left. A drop with no recorded reason uses `not recorded before this system`. Conflicting drop evidence with a recorded reason is listed as `cancellation_evidence_conflict`.
- An expired action is expired. When Clear the deck's latest decision on it is the drop that expired it, the step keeps that event's id and time, and one deck event can close several steps; a later keep, or no event, leaves both empty and lists `expiry_unproven`.

A current next-step note used as a fallback keeps the same signing as its recorded activity. Both use `copy_next_step_event()` to pair it with its own `set-next-step`, `undo-call-change` or `undo-call` event. Tied or missing matches leave an agent's sponsoring user unknown and listed as `agent_user_unknown`.

Ended steps stay in detail reads. Home also shows a completion in Done for seven days after its recorded completion time. A hold copies as the step's `hold_until`. Steps without a partner owner take the deal owner or vendor relationship partner. The copy lists steps that still have no owner. Post-call actions keep their titles, ordered bullets and source references unchanged. The coordinator counted five open titled post-call actions and zero titled `next_action` rows on 2026-11-17 (#541). No checklist copy path is built for `next_action`; its source references and display fields remain listed.

Vendor next actions follow the same rules, as next steps about the person or company the vendor became, never about a deal. A merged-away vendor's actions reach its survivor. An action whose vendor is not copied is listed as `vendor_not_copied`, and a later run retries it.

When neither the step nor the vendor names a partner owner, use the step's recorded creator if that actor is a partner. Otherwise, use the partner who last worked with the vendor. The latest work is the newest by occurrence time of a live activity filed directly to the vendor or by creation time of a partner-created step on that vendor, including merged-away vendor ids. An activity can name the partner through its original actor, its logging event's recorded sponsor, or the known mailbox for sent mail. Tasks and imported last-touch stamps supply no work evidence. If the latest time names both partners or there is no attributed work, the step stays unclaimed and is listed as `vendor_owner_unknown`.

Both next-step copies give open steps with a recorded partner owner first claim on that partner's slot. If an inferred owner already has an open step on the same record, the incoming step copies with that owner as cancelled, with reason `replaced at the move`. Its resolution time and source event are empty. A recorded-owner step that cannot copy due to a collision is listed as `open_step_exists` with the assigned step's id in `value`. A previously copied assigned step keeps its place. Among inferred steps, the deal copy favors the current note and the lead and client copy favors recorded actions over date-only follow-ups. Remaining ties use creation time and id.

The board's current-action view decides whether the newest unsuperseded note copies as a fallback. Checklist items and pending assessments do not hide a note; a current done plan suppresses it and its date. Older, superseded and hidden notes stay listed without an invented completion, and the activity copy keeps each as a recorded note. A newer note can replace a completed plan. The fallback takes `deal.next_date`, including null, and the deal's mapped owner. A deal with a date but no actions or next-step notes gets an `Imported deal follow-up`, with a stable id derived from its deal id. A date matching an open action's due date needs no report; otherwise a date without a fallback is listed.

Post-call actions in `capture_post_call_action` copy too, with their ids, owners, nullable due dates, acceptance time as creation time and explicit `completed_at`. Only `drop-action` records a reason, and it never names a post-call action, so dropped post-call actions keep the approved reason for an unrecorded cancellation. Source references and display fields are listed like ordinary actions. Attribution follows the action's own event when available, otherwise its accepting actor.

Following the coordinator's ruling on #363, every activity of kind `task` stays listed as `task_kind_unconfirmed`, pending an owner ruling. Logged task history supplies no proof that work remains open or was completed, so no task becomes a new Home to-do and no completion time is invented. Notes retain their event attribution; an unknown agent sponsor is listed. Existing source ids prevent duplicates and leave rows, stamps and history unchanged on reruns. A later run retries records whose deal has since copied.

## Copying today's Home updates and call follow-ups

- Run `schema/copy-home-updates.sql` as the owner in its own transaction, with the new schema on `search_path` and attribution loaded. The populated update kind is the nightly deal checklist reminder in `ops.notification`. It keeps its id, recipient, deal, wording, creation time, the checklist item and due date named by its event, and the recipient's read time from `ops.notification_read`. Missing links and invalid facts, including a missing or invalid checklist due date, are listed in `not_copied`. A rerun leaves existing updates alone. The copy reports the count of old work alerts left behind in a separate `left_behind` result, following the owner's 2026-11-18 ruling. Rehearsal had zero deal-watch updates.
- `copy-deal-next-steps.sql` finds a post-call action's source by its candidate id. The candidate names the call session, deal, quote and any stored contact. A missing call link or blank quote leaves the action listed. A missing contact mapping is listed and the contact stays empty. An old session with no summary keeps an empty summary. Rehearsal had zero ordinary `next_action` rows with a call-candidate reference.

## Copying today's change history

`schema/copy-change-history.sql` loads today's `public.event` after the record copies and `copy-attribution.sql`. Here "today's" means the existing system's history, without a calendar cutoff. The owner runs it in one transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. Following decision 73, only the imported-history stamp trigger is off during the load and back on before commit. No current record, native `change` row or existing imported row is rewritten.

An old event is evidence of a command, sometimes one field and sometimes a partial creation payload. It cannot honestly become a native `change` insert with a whole new row. Each supported field event instead becomes one `imported_change`, keyed by its old event id, with its complete original event in `source_event`. The `old_value_absent` and `new_value_absent` flags distinguish SQL null from JSON null. The source retains old and new values, actor id, occurred and recorded times, mutation order, verb, cause, sponsor and other provenance. The attribution helper supplies the user or job and sponsor, using that event alone; `changed_at` is its `occurred_at`. No old values are reinterpreted as current values. A deal owner event with cause `automation_job` is attributed to the move (`old_system`). The original actor and payload stay in `source_event`.

| Old subject | New record and key                     | Supported old fields                                                                   |
| ----------- | -------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| party       | `party`, `id`                          | name, npi                                                                              |
| deal        | `deal`, `id`                           | owner, won_value, city, operating_state, attention                                     |
| deal        | `activity`, `id`                       | note → summary, when the event uniquely matches a copied deal note                     |
| lead        | `party_role`, party_id and role lead   | stage, owner, owner_id, lane, segment, est_lease_event, event_source, event_confidence |
| client      | `client`, party_id                     | status, client_type                                                                    |
| vendor      | `party_role`, party_id and role vendor | stage, offers, seeking                                                                 |
| vendor      | `activity`, id                         | intro_notes → summary                                                                  |

`outside_id` with source `old_system` resolves party, deal, lead, client and vendor ids, including merged-away ids. Roles and clients must actually exist after resolution. A field is mapped only when an old writer records it. Destination field names are explicit in the script; they describe where the fact belongs, without claiming the old payload has the new format. Events outside those mappings are listed. The deal history copy below handles known deal-page events that have no other home.

An `add-deal-note` event maps to the copied note's `summary` only when exactly one source note and one event match by deal, author, time and text. The copy lists a missing or ambiguous match as `note_not_matched`. A unique match whose copied note or deal link is missing is listed as `record_not_copied`. The history row keeps its event ID, attribution and original timestamps, and follows the existing erasure rule for the activity's summary.

Vendor `intro_notes` events point to the note activity created by `copy-vendors.sql`, using `md5('old_system:vendor:' || subject_id || ':intro_notes')::uuid`, and map to `summary`. The activity must be a note about the vendor's copied party. Each event keeps its own author and time, including earlier wording and cleared text; current note text stays unchanged. Blank notes and merged-away notes whose text the survivor holds have no separate activity and wait listed as `record_not_copied`. Their history is never redirected to the survivor's note. Existing imports with different destinations or source events wait as `existing_history_conflicts`.

The Deal Room writer's `operating_state` event wraps each old and new value as `{operating_state: {state, reason, note}}`. Active values have null reason and note. Parked values have a known parking reason and a string or null note. Each complete event stays one imported row. If either side contains a note, its destination field is `parking_note`, so an owner can erase that text. Otherwise its field is `parking_reason`. This covers parking, revival, reason changes and note changes without inventing phase history. Copied parking and revival events can sit under either destination field. An `attention` event wraps a boolean on both sides and maps to `needs_attention`, including false. Missing values, unknown states or reasons, wrong types, missing keys and extra keys wait listed as `payload_shape_unknown`. No current deal column fills a gap in an event.

The same copy keeps old deal-page events with no other home in `imported_deal_history`. Checklist pings are outside its supported shapes and copy through Home updates. A phase move or won or lost outcome stays out when the matching phase row has both source times. A critical date must match the copied deal and due date, the original kind, and a creation time within five seconds. An activity must be linked to the deal by its recorded ID. A next-action creation must match the deal, step text, due date and a creation time within five seconds on a copied action. If the copy changed the text or date, an action with the same source ID and creation time can establish that match from the legacy values. A resolved post-call candidate must name the copied step on that deal. A completion also requires the step's done time to match. An invoice application is already represented if its copied invoice has a payment change.

The source event ID is the key. Each supported combination of `verb` and `field` has named payload fields with PostgreSQL types. The copy keeps the surviving payload keys, both times, original actor ID and provenance. It uses the same attribution as the Timeline. The copy lists standalone technical values and empty next-step date changes. It keeps finding-source text with business words and removes a bare URL, path or old system name only from the side that holds it. Finding text also loses separable transcript file paths and parenthesized row IDs, listed as `technical_finding_path` and `technical_finding_row_id`. Each side contributes its own correction reasons before the report combines them by event. A dismissal keeps its reason. The old `loi-lease` template displays as LOI lease. National market placeholders use the same rule as the deal copy and are listed as `placeholder_market`. A bulk import line is technical when it exactly matches the deal’s recorded import key and known source file. The copy drops that line when it stands alone or when a newline or semicolon separates it from business text. Other import summaries keep their wording. Salesforce references and notes paths stay out of the deal history. Untyped or invalid payloads and missing deal or user mappings stay listed by event ID. Reruns leave imported rows as they were.

The final report lists event ids, fields and reason codes, never payloads. Missing copied records, unknown actors, humans without users, unknown or uncopied sponsors, unsupported fields and conflicting existing imports all wait listed. A registered agent with no recorded sponsor keeps that unknown sponsor and is listed too. Such an event can still copy because no sponsor is invented. Repeating an identical import writes nothing. A changed source or a newly resolved destination for an existing import is listed as a conflict, leaving that history intact. The engine may read imported history but cannot add, rewrite or remove it. Serving a combined feed remains a later slice.

An owner's `erase_change` request also covers matching imported records. It removes the old and new payloads for a selected destination field, plus every matching event's quote and rationale because those free-text explanations may repeat the words. Other event payloads, keys, verbs, causes, authors and times remain. `erased_fields` records which fields were erased, so a repeated erase adds no history and a copy never restores the words. This extends decision 49's existing owner ruling to the second history table.

## Copying today's meeting notes

Run `schema/copy-meetings.sql` after attribution, `copy-activity.sql` and `copy-calls.sql`, in a separate owner transaction with the new schema on `search_path`. It reads `ops.meeting`, `ops.meeting_note` and today's public activity, actor, capture-session and capture-candidate tables. One meeting activity holds the title and the latest revision of each note, numbered in note order. Imported history retains each source note id, revision, author and time, with before and after summaries. Its `import-meeting-edit` verb and `old_system_import` cause label the conversion, not a recovered user command. Meeting mode, native capture identity, end state, streams, leases, client instances and action tables do not move. No sender or business-record link is inferred from the activating user.

The old meeting tables have no activity link. Optionally supply `pg_temp.copy_meeting_activity(meeting_id uuid primary key, activity_id uuid not null unique)` in the transaction, mapping an ops meeting to its reviewed public meeting, tour or copied capture activity. A reused capture call keeps its kind. That activity must already be copied. Its id, time and existing record links remain; its logged summary follows the meeting title before the current notes. Without a mapping, a time within 24 hours, an equal title, or a source UTC date matching the meeting's Chicago date identify candidate pairs only, for both logged activities and capture sessions. Capture times and dates are checked even before an activity can copy, so an unfinished or unlinked capture holds the meeting too; title matching checks copied text and recoverable source text. Source text is the call copy's summary from `copy_capture_summary`: a nonblank phone projection (trimmed summary, decisions and open questions) takes precedence, otherwise exactly one non-skipped meeting record supplies its summary and detail, only when no confirmed logged link exists. Several text-bearing records supply no guessed title and remain listed by the call copy. A capture already linked to a logged activity by its confirmed meeting record remains that logged activity, as in the call copy; its candidate uses that logged identity instead of its session id. Several source facts about one logged activity list only one pair. As in the call copy, the greatest logged activity id wins if there are several confirmed links. A stale session-id copy is neither a candidate nor a valid reuse target after that link exists; the copy does not delete it. The date comparison covers evening meetings whose hand-logged date became UTC midnight. Every such pair is listed as `possible_duplicate`, and the meeting waits. To clear a false match, supply that pair in optional `pg_temp.copy_meeting_distinct(meeting_id uuid, activity_id uuid, primary key (meeting_id, activity_id))`. Every remaining candidate must be reviewed before a separate activity copies. Other meetings keep their ops id. A copied meeting with no known record links is listed as `about_nothing_copied`. A reused logged activity retains a newer last-change time and its author stamp. This coordinator choice on #396 is open to an owner ruling.

Unknown authors, invalid or conflicting mappings, reversed revision chronology and conflicting imported history wait listed. An agent without a known sponsor keeps the agent stamp and is listed. Reruns preserve current rows, native history and imported edits, including later user changes. New source revisions on an already-copied meeting wait as `meeting_changed_since_copy` rather than overwrite it. PostgreSQL transaction controls and JSONB, then the existing pg test driver, cover this copy without another dependency.

## Copying today's meeting suggestions

Run `schema/copy-meeting-suggestions.sql` as the owner after `copy-meetings.sql` and the next-step copies, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path` and attribution loaded. It reads `public` and `ops` and ends with `not_copied` (decision 88).

- An `ops.meeting_action` whose current revision names a `set-next-action` or `set-next-step` command becomes a next-step `proposal` on the deal, person or company its command names, cited from the activity `copy-meetings.sql` made of its meeting, with the revision's summary as evidence. Proposed, accepted, executed and delegated, and declined become pending, accepted and dismissed. It is proposed by `old_system` at its current revision's time.
- A decision copies only with the partner's matching `ops.meeting_action_decision_request`, its deciding user and time. An explicit instruction accepted itself with no request, so it is listed.
- An accepted action is the step it made, never a second one: its proposal takes the id its dispatch's `tool_call` or deal-room event names, when that step was copied. Otherwise it takes its first revision's id.
- Every revision becomes imported history of the proposal, as decision 81 describes, with its summary before and after, command, basis, author and time.
- Each verb's own arguments are read: `set-next-step` its deal, text and next date, `set-next-action` its ref, description, due date and owner. The deal room's text is trimmed as it trimmed it. Text that is not a JSON string, a date not written as a `YYYY-MM-DD` string and a deal-room step without its numeric `base_version` are listed, as the old deal room refused them. So is a pending deal-room step whose deal's version has moved since, since it would have been refused and the queue keeps no version to check. A step conditioned on `expected_action` has no place in the queue and is listed.
- Listed and not copied: actions with no command or another verb, a revision id another copy's imported history holds or history already held without its proposal, a dispatch result that names no valid id, a meeting, record or made step not copied, a party the meeting is not about, blank text, an invalid due date, an owner, author or decider who is not a user, and an action that changed since it was copied or has another's payload. Listed but copied: a delegate and an agent's unknown sponsor.
- A rerun leaves copied proposals and history as they were, and copies an action whose meeting, record or step was copied since.

## Copying today's email suggestions

Run `schema/copy-email-suggestions.sql` as the owner after `copy-emails.sql`, in its own transaction with the new schema on `search_path` and attribution loaded. It only reads `public` and ends with `not_copied` (decision 112).

- An `email_proposal` next step becomes a next-step `proposal` cited from the email `copy-emails.sql` made of its activity, as `outside_id` maps it, with its quote as evidence, its text, due date and owner's user. Pending and dismissed stay pending and dismissed; it is proposed by `old_system` at its creation time, and a dismissal keeps today's resolver and time. Change history holds the pending insert and, when dismissed, the decision.
- A step with a deal is on that deal. Without one, it uses the source row's one client, lead, vendor or direct party link. The copied email must be about that party. Home shows the step to its owner. The email's mailbox remains a separate fact.
- A pending filing suggestion or possible new deal uses its live inbound email, quote and source mailbox. Filing keeps the suggested deal. A possible new deal has no target until the partner decides where to file the email. As in door 21, one stored email and payload produce one proposal. A fresh copy keeps the earliest source proposal's id and evidence. For an email in both mailboxes, both memberships point to the same proposal's source.
- Listed when a row cannot copy: an accepted next step, a missing email or target, blank evidence or step text, an unmapped step owner or resolver, or a conflict with stored history. Filing suggestions also list a missing mailbox, an outbound or non-live source, a resolved status, or a duplicate payload. `copy-reply-drafts.sql` handles reply drafts.
- Listed but copied without the value: a dismissal reason, a resolution note and a snooze. The filing payload keeps the evidence quote. It has no field for the proposed matter label, sender role, confidence score or matching diagnostics. The Nov 18 copy left those values behind on 46, 70, 70 and 54 copied suggestions, respectively.
- Each `email_lead_exclusion` is listed by its hash, as above.
- A rerun leaves copied proposals and history as they were, and copies a suggestion whose email, record or owner was copied since.

## Who reads and writes the three features' records

The owner's three features, the Deals board and deal pages, the Home inbox, and email and calendar, rest on deals, activity, emails, attachments, email suggestions, reply drafts, next steps and Home items. They move together (#580). Every program below reads or writes those records today. Each one moves to the engine or retires before the switch, or it would read a stale copy or write to a table nobody reads.

Places are `repo/path:line` at old system `d4e2e1d`, app `74f3ad3` and engine `dc1aa24`. "Live" means it wrote in the 30 days before 2026-11-17, or a screen or job calls it today. Write counts are old `event` and `tool_call` rows from those 30 days, grouped by verb only. Slice numbers are the steps listed after the tables.

## Client Fit

Charts counts wins in the closed phase with a recorded close date through today. It uses the Deals board's phase, close date, client, deal type and market. A client linked to the deal counts even if they have no separate client profile, as with a newly converted prospect. The vertical comes from `client_vertical.vertical` or the client's recorded specialty labels. The response's state comes from the leased property's `property.county_fips` or from `market_county.county_fips` when the market's recorded counties are all in one state. Client addresses have no state field in the new schema. Missing dimensions are null and Charts displays them as “Unrecorded.”

## Deals reads for the app

Steps 13–15 and 29 use `GET /api/engine/deals` and `GET /api/engine/deals/{deal_id}`. The board accepts `workspace` (`all`, `team`, or `national_account`) and `account_client_id`. It returns the signed-in partner's slug, open, closed and lost lists, and national account summaries. The detail returns the deal header, steps, checklist items, critical dates, files, lease, and history under the app's existing section names. The new detail route takes the deal UUID the board supplies.

Each new-schema board row and deal detail returns separate `kind` and `reason` slugs, with null for either unknown fact. The detail also returns `renews_lease_id` as the source lease UUID or null. The legacy `type` still uses the kind when known and otherwise uses the reason with its legacy spelling. An ended imported Renewal with an unrecorded source lease returns `reason: 'renewal'` and `renewal_lease_unrecorded: true` until it has a live reason. `parked_automatically: true` means the old automation parked the deal, and `parked_by` is a user slug or null. `next_critical_date` is `{ id, kind, due_on }` for the earliest confirmed date still open, or null when none exists. `due_on` is a calendar date in `YYYY-MM-DD` format. Open dates that are already late still qualify.

Each national account's `overdue_deals` counts its active open deals with at least one open ordinary next step due before today in Central time. A retained date from a completed step and a checklist item's due date do not qualify.

The detail sends `checklist` as `deal-checklist.v1`, with one group for each of the eight phases in [Checklist under the phases](#checklist-under-the-phases). The template lives in engine code and needs no doctrine hash check. Its labels use sentence case and display as sent. Saved deal items and their due dates still come from `next_step`. The template shows progress from `current_lease` and its source file, the deal's invoice date, and commission payments. A copied renewal without a source lease shows the common checklist until a partner links the source lease. Commission received is complete when the deal has at least one saved payment and each payment has a receipt date. The expected amount is a forecast and has no effect on receipt progress. Confirmed open critical dates count as due or overdue by the Central calendar day. Document requests stay on the old side because Pass D found no rows to move (#718).

Both partners' detail reads return every file filed to the deal, including earlier versions. Each `documents` entry gives `id`, `name`, `kind`, `note`, `version`, `bytes`, `content_type` and `prepared_at`. Open the file by its `id` through `GET /api/engine/documents/{id}` (door 20).

The new detail read returns `properties` with each linked property’s `id`, `name`, and `address`, ordered by address then ID. It returns an empty array when none are linked.

The new detail read returns `correspondence`, an array of email messages and meetings linked directly to the deal by `activity_about`, newest first by `happened_at`. A filed email appears even when it has a pending follow-up. An unfiled email with a pending filing suggestion appears in `email_follow_ups`. Each correspondence entry gives `activity_id`, `kind`, and `happened_at`. An `email` also gives `sender`, `sender_name`, and `subject` from the stored message. Messages in the same Outlook conversation have their own entries. A `meeting` gives `title` from the part of the stored activity summary before the first blank line. The meeting notes copy puts the recorded title there. Other meetings use the saved summary caption, and an absent or blank caption returns null. A meeting with property links also gives `properties: [{ id, name, address }]` with the current names and addresses. Deal notes still come from `thread`. The old-schema read supplies `email_threads`.

`GET /api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/emails/{activity_id}` opens one filed email from the deal's Activity. It takes the deal UUID and a `correspondence` email's `activity_id`, and no query parameters. It answers `{ thread }` from the same thread read and mailbox rules as Home's email reviews ([Home inbox reads for the app](#home-inbox-reads-for-the-app)): the messages of that email's Outlook conversation in the signed-in partner's mailbox, oldest first, each with `activity_id`, `sender_name`, `happened_at`, `body` and `folded`. An email filed to the deal but outside the partner's mailbox answers `{ thread: [] }`. An activity that is not an email filed to that deal, an unknown deal or a malformed ID returns `404 not_found`.

Both partners' open board cards and deal pages return `client_portal_url` for an active registration belonging to the deal's current client. It is the site's address on `share.doctorcre.com`, or null when there is no matching active registration. The reader database role can read the fields used to form the link.

### Closing anniversaries

`GET /api/engine/closing-anniversaries` takes no query parameters and requires the same signed-in partner as the Deals board. It reads the new schema through `DATABASE` as `engine_reader`. It uses the board's shared closed deals with a won outcome, a client, and a recorded closing date.

The window includes today and the next 30 calendar days in `America/Chicago`. A closing first appears on its one-year anniversary. A February 29 closing falls on February 28 in non-leap years.

The response is an array ordered by `anniversary_on`, with `deal_id` breaking ties. Each row has `client_id` (party UUID), `client_name`, `deal_id` (deal UUID), `deal_name`, `closed_on`, `anniversary_on`, `years`, and `days_until`. Dates use `YYYY-MM-DD`. When the window has no matches, the response is `200 []`.

`GET /api/engine/closing-anniversaries/next` takes the same partner, deals and February rule. It answers `{ "next_anniversary_on": "YYYY-MM-DD" }`, the earliest anniversary after the window, or `null` when no deal qualifies. The screen shows it when the window is empty.

### Compare terms

`GET /api/engine/market-terms` serves the deal page's Compare terms tab. It takes optional `market`, `use_type`, `as_of` and `exclude_deal_id`, requires the same signed-in partner as the Deals board, and reads the new schema as `engine_reader`. It needs no table of its own, as [Markets and territories](#markets-and-territories) rules: it reads each offer round and each signed lease version on a deal, on a property in the named market's counties whose `building_type` matches the use type. Both matches ignore case, and a blank or missing filter matches everything. An unknown market has no terms. `as_of` is a calendar date, or the read refuses with `invalid_field_value`. With it, a round counts when it was offered by that date in Central time and a lease version when it was signed by then; a version with no signing date is left out. `exclude_deal_id` is the deal the partner has open: a deal is never its own comparable, so its rounds and lease versions are left out. It is a deal id, or the read refuses with `invalid_field_value`.

The response is `{ schema_version: "compare-terms.v1", market?, use_type?, as_of?, exclude_deal_id?, rows }`, echoing the filters it applied. Each row is one recorded figure: `deal_id`, `deal_name`, `property` (`id`, `name`, `address`), `stage` (`round` or `lease`), `number` (the round or version), `recorded_on` (offer or signing date, or null), `term` (`rent_per_sf_yr`, `nnn_per_sf_yr`, `ti_per_sf`, `free_rent_months`, `term_months` or `escalation_pct`), `value`, `expense_structure` (the lease's, null on a round), `superseded` and `documents`, the round's filed documents or the version's source document, each with `id` and `name`. A round is superseded by a later round on the same deal and property, and a version by a later version; with `as_of`, only by one recorded by that date. Rows run newest first, then by deal name, stage, number and term. The old system's classified receipts and quarantine have no counterpart: every recorded round and lease is a comparable.

### Activity deal attribution

`GET /api/engine/activities/{activity_id}/deal-attribution` takes the exact activity UUID from the deal's history and requires a PartnerApp session. Either partner can read attribution for activity filed to a shared deal.

- With one deal in `activity_about`, the read returns `{ kind: "credited", deal: { id, name } }`.
- With several deals, the read returns `{ kind: "ambiguous", candidates: [{ id, name }, ...] }`, ordered by deal name and ID. None receives credit.
- With no deal link or no matching activity, the read returns `404 not_found`. A signed-out request returns `401`.

### Call recorder context

`GET /api/engine/call-context` reads the new database for a signed-in partner. Pass 1 to 50 distinct deal UUIDs as repeated `deal_id` query fields. A missing list, invalid UUID, duplicate UUID (ignoring case), or oversized batch returns `400 invalid_field_value`.

The response is `{ deals, unavailable_deal_ids }`. Each list follows request order and preserves the requested UUID spelling. A visible, active open deal returns `{ id, name, owner, operating_state, participants }`, with the owner's user slug and `operating_state: "active"`. Copied deals and deals opened in the new database use the same read. As on the Deals board, either partner can read both partners' shared deals. A missing, inaccessible, parked or ended deal appears only by its requested UUID in `unavailable_deal_ids`. It does not prevent the rest of the batch from loading.

Each `participants` entry is `{ party_id, ref, name, email, role }` from a current deal link, including the linked client. Internal app users stay outside the recipient roster. `ref` uses the party's sole retained `P-` reference when available, otherwise its current party UUID. `email` is the party's saved primary address or null. A party with more than one role has an entry for each role. A deal with nobody on its roster returns `participants: []`.

`GET /jobs/call-context` gives Studio the same response and deal ID validation. Use the existing Jobs Access token, `Engine-Job: call_capture` for the registered job, and `Engine-For-User` with a provisioned partner's slug. Pass the exact deal UUIDs as repeated `deal_id` fields. Omitting the IDs returns `400 invalid_field_value`. Use `party_id` to identify a participant and `ref` for display.

### Deal call outcomes

`GET /api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/call-outcomes` requires a PartnerApp session. It takes the deal UUID in the path. An unknown or malformed deal ID returns `404 not_found`.

The response is `{ deal_id, deal_name, calls }`. It selects up to 50 calls linked to the deal, newest first with activity ID breaking ties, then builds their proposal arrays. A call has `activity_id`, `started_at` (an instant), `call_date` (Central time), and `proposals`. Calls recorded for the signed-in partner can have an empty proposal list. An unattributed call from the old system appears only when the deal has a proposal from that call that the signed-in partner can see.

Both partners see every call, its summary, its follow-ups' wording and quotes, its phase reasons and its proposals' evidence ([decision 210](decisions.md)). The database records the partner who recorded a call, or for whom a job recorded it, in `activity.call_owner_user_id` and keeps it when someone edits the call. That partner still gets the call's follow-ups. Either partner can correct a call's caller through the engine. Partner read transactions, including Doc's record tool, set `engine.user_id` from the authenticated partner. The partner reader role reads records through the `next_partner` views. It cannot read their underlying tables and refuses requests when the protected schema is unavailable. The views keep each partner's Home updates to that partner, give a queued checklist reminder whose item is gone a generic wording, and keep imported quotes and rationale out of imported history. The protected change view keeps the audit keys directly selectable so Home can use the existing index, and the phase history view selects its columns directly so the Deals board narrows it by deal through the primary key. The Updates list and mark-read response count the same unread updates.

A database that already has [the call read policies](../schema/add-call-read-policies.sql) applies [the call privacy removal](../schema/remove-call-privacy.sql). It puts the call quotes and phase reasons that the policies kept apart back into their history rows, drops the call audience rules and rebuilds the `next_partner` views as `schema/next.sql` defines them. It can be applied twice.

A proposal has `proposal_id`, `kind`, `status`, `evidence`, and the fields the card can display, `what`, `owner` (the user's slug), `due_on`, `from_phase`, `to_phase`, `phase_on`, and `next_step_id`. Fields that do not apply are null. The response includes visible proposals in their stored state, including accepted and dismissed proposals, ordered by creation time and proposal ID. A proposal for another deal stays off this deal's cards, even when the same call discussed both deals.

The app confirms or dismisses a pending suggestion through [the same proposal decision as Home](#21-suggestions-stored-and-decided). It uses the returned `proposal_id` and sends `decision: "accept"` or `decision: "dismiss"` with a UUID `idempotency_key`. Call Outcomes requires the partner to be able to decide the suggestion. The same decision door also serves the assignee of a Home next-step suggestion or completion review from the other partner's call. A hidden suggestion returns `404 not_found`, the same as an unknown ID. A transport retry with the same key replays the receipt. For a copied phase suggestion, acceptance checks the recorded starting phase against the live deal phase. A mismatch returns `409 version_conflict`. Refresh the call outcomes after a decision.

Each proposal includes `accept_blocked`, which is null unless a pending copied action conflicts with the owner's open ordinary step. For a call or meeting action on a deal, acceptance merges into that owner's existing open ordinary step when the text matches after collapsing whitespace, trimming and ignoring case. It returns that step's ID in `created.next_step_id`, keeps the existing step open, and changes its due date only when the proposal's due date was stated. A copied action whose effective text differs from that owner's open step returns `409 open_step_exists`, leaves the proposal pending and does not cancel the existing step. The read reports `accept_blocked: "open_step_exists"` for a text conflict; edited text and owner are checked again on acceptance. Dismiss remains available.

Accepting `log_call` from a shared copied call still files a note on its deal or party. A shared copied call has `changed_by_job = 'old_system'` and neither a user nor a for-user stamp.
