Accepting a log from any other call saves no note activity. Its text becomes the call's summary when that summary is null or empty; otherwise it is appended after a blank line. Saving the summary stamps the deciding partner as its latest author, so activity reads show that partner in place of the capturing job. The response returns the call's ID in `created.activity_id`. There is no private-call acceptance block. Install [the private call log migration](../schema/add-private-call-log.sql) after [the step merge migration](../schema/add-call-review-step-merge.sql) on an existing database.

### Set up a client website

`POST /api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/client-website` is a signed-in partner save. It takes `idempotency_key`, `slug` (1 to 63 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, starting with a letter or digit), and `password` (1 to 1,024 characters). It returns `200 { ok, deal_id, client_portal_url, created }`. A repeat with the same name and password and a fresh key returns `created: false`. A transport retry with the same key returns the original answer and `replayed: true`. The receipt fingerprint uses the deal and name, omitting the password as the old save did.

The save refuses a name reserved by another registration (`409 portal_slug_taken`), a different password for the same registration (`409 portal_password_differs`), or a new name for a deal with an active registration (`409 portal_deal_already_registered`). A missing deal or current client returns `404 portal_deal_not_found`. The database stores a bcrypt cost-12 verifier of the hex SHA-256 password digest, compatible with the copied registration. Setup registers the website's address and credential. Publishing the website remains a separate operation.

### Open a client website

`POST /api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/client-website/open` uses the signed-in session and CSRF token and returns `200 { url }` for the new tab. Either partner can request it. A deal with no active registration for its current client returns 404. The URL uses the engine's configured `CLIENT_WEBSITE_ORIGIN`. Without a valid HTTPS origin the door returns 503 `client_website_origin_unconfigured` before it issues a grant. The one-use grant expires after 60 seconds. The engine stores only its digest, tied to the website and signed-in user.

The share Worker calls the engine's private `ClientWebsiteAccess` service binding. `redeemGrant(slug, grant)` rechecks the current registration and consumes the matching grant atomically. The share Worker starts its own secure site session and redirects to the address with the grant removed. `verifyPassword(slug, password)` checks the same registration's stored verifier. The fifth failed password locks that website's password check for the rest of its 15-minute window. A correct password clears the failure count. The share Worker still owns the session cookie and the asset-decryption key.

Each `email_follow_ups` entry is one distinct pending decision targeting the deal, from either partner's mailbox. Filing suggestions of the same kind in one Outlook conversation are one decision, given as the newest suggestion, as on Home. It gives `proposal_id`, `kind`, `deal_id`, `evidence`, `title`, the proposed step fields (`what`, `owner`, `due_on`) or phase fields (`from_phase`, `to_phase`), and an `email` with `activity_id`, `sender`, `sender_name`, `subject`, `happened_at` and `summary`. The `title` uses the partner’s Home card title when that card shows the same source email. Otherwise it uses the saved action from that email or the title for its pending decision. The `owner` field gives the app user slug. A filing suggestion drops from this list once the email is on the deal. Use the proposal ID to accept or dismiss through door 21. Accepting a filing suggestion files the email.

Set the engine Worker's `DEALS_READ_SCHEMA` variable to `next` to use the read-only `next` schema through the existing `DATABASE` binding. The login must be able to assume `engine_reader` on the copied database. An absent setting or any other value uses today's readers. Once rehearsal verifies the remaining app dependencies, set the engine variable first, then set `deals-from-engine` to `on` in the app's `ENGINE_ROUTING` KV. To return the app to CARR, set the KV value to `off`.

Timeline rows for a new-schema deal update show the fields and values the update changed. Owner changes show user slugs and market changes show market names. Parking reason and note changes share one `operating_state` line. Native and copied parking events return `{ state: "parked", reason, note? }`. The reason is the same code the board returns in `parking_reason`. A change that records only the note omits the reason. Revival returns `{ state: "active" }`. The card and Timeline use the same display wording for parking notes, including the October 1 import note. A blank or missing note is omitted from the event. The app uses the card's reason labels to render these events. Imported rows keep the original event's verb, field and recorded time. A matched note event appears on its source deal. Its `activity_id` matches the note's `thread[].id` so the Timeline can show the note once. Other events have a null `activity_id`. A dated phase move shows the recorded phase, author and save time. When the earliest phase row has the `old_system` stamp and equal save and effective times, the read treats it as the copied current phase and leaves it out of `events`. Later phase moves can have equal times and still appear. National account cards show the retained client roster reference when the account has one unambiguous `C-` reference in `outside_id`.

Old record codes in a new-schema deal's notes (`thread[].text`), other activity summaries and step display text read as the record's name (#1287). The Leads drawer's `notes` and `plans`, with `record_links`, and client Recent activity summaries read the same way. A code is C, L, P, T or V, an optional letter group and three or four digits, such as `C-013`, `L-032`, `P-0010`, `T-123` or `V-CPA-006`. It is looked up under `old_system_ref` in `outside_id`. A code that matches no named record reads "an old record", capitalized at the start of a sentence. A code anywhere in a word that holds `/`, `@` or `=`, such as a link, path, email or query, stays as written, and so does one inside a longer code. So does a unit number after Suite, Ste, Unit, Bldg, Building, Room, Space, Level, Floor, Fl, Lot, Bay, Apt, Office or `#`, singular or plural, through a list such as "Suites C-101 and C-102". Stored text and each step's `title` and `description` keep the code. `record_links` lists each named record once as `{ text, url }`, with the name shown and `/relationships?node=party:{id}`, so the app can link the name.

The deal page returns `imported_deal_history` in ascending `recorded_at` and `id` order, or `[]` when empty. Each entry has `id` (the source event UUID), `recorded_at`, `occurred_at`, `actor` (a user or job slug), `verb`, nullable `field`, `old_value`, `new_value`, and `imported: true`. Each value is null or an object with the recorded facts for that verb and field. Missing values are omitted. An explicit clear of a previously recorded fact keeps a null in `new_value`. Website links, research source URLs, internal paths, and next-action routing summaries stay in the source record. Use `verb` and `field` to label what happened. In imported deal openings, the old `deal_type` values `relocation`, `startup`, `additional_office`, `expansion`, and `renewal` appear under `reason` for the Deal reason label. Kinds stay under `deal_type` for the Deal kind label. A document attachment event stays out of the read when every attachment ID matches a filed document on the same deal. The list is read-only and the signed-in read includes it for either partner.

Each row in `critical_dates`, `documents`, and `next_actions` gives an `actor` with the user or job slug from its last save. A critical date gives its last save time as `updated_at`. A next step also gives its assigned `owner`. An open row in `next_actions` gives its latest `change_id`, or zero if there is no recorded change. An ended row gives `null`.

When the nightly invoice job moved the deal to its current Closed phase, the board and detail reads carry its receipt. The app shows the invoice caption and offers invoice Undo in Closed deals. The receipt uses the invoice application ID and its linked prior phase. Its date comes from the closing phase row in Central time, the same source as the close date. Partner applications, invoices on already-closed deals, later phase moves and invoice undos leave the current phase without that label.

A copied or fresh automatic call move appears as `phase_change` on the board and deal page while its phase row is current and the deal is not lost. A recorded loss suppresses the receipt and refuses Undo even when the old phase says Closed. A current invoice application uses its own receipt and undo.

All next-schema automatic receipts give `event_id` (UUID), `prior_phase`, `phase`, nullable `reason`, Chicago `evidence_date` (the phase's effective day), `recorded_at` (the save time), and `automatic: true`. An automatic invoice close gives `source: "invoice"` and `call: null`. A copied receipt gives `source: "copied"`, its original event ID, and `call: null`. It uses the phase row's reason if the copied reason is empty. A fresh call receipt gives `source: "call_capture"`, the phase save's idempotency key as its event ID, and `call: { activity_id, happened_at }` with the stored call's UUID and start timestamp. Use `evidence_date` for the move caption. The receipt sets `undoable: false` for a copied close with an invoice date and no invoice application for the current move. The receipt and invoice date stay visible, with Undo hidden. Other receipts set `undoable: true` and follow the usual Undo rules.

The signed-in partner sends `{ idempotency_key, event_id, base_version }` to `POST /api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/phase/undo`. Use the card's `seen` value as `base_version`. Undo locks the deal and checks that version and exact current move before appending the previous phase today through the usual phase save. It returns `{ deal_id, phase, phase_changed_at }`. Change history records the partner's save. A stale card or a move that is no longer current returns `409 version_conflict { current_version }`. Undo returns the same status and error for a previous phase of Closed, Invoiced, or Dead, with a hint that Undo would return the deal to Closed deals or Lost deals. A copied invoice-dated close returns the same status and error with the hint "Closed because an invoice was recorded; Undo isn't available". A same-key retry returns the original answer.

### Starred deals

Stars are gone (owner, 2026-11-25: "every deal is important"; #1457). No door saves a star and the board read gives none. `deal_star` still holds the stars saved before, one row per user and deal, until the owner approves dropping the table in production. Until then the database keeps its rules: one row per pair, only the user saving it, never a job, removed with the deal, each star and unstar in change history.

Fresh schemas include the table. [The deal stars migration](../schema/add-deal-stars.sql) adds it to an older `next` schema and can be applied twice; no engine needs it any more.

### App work for the Deals switch

The app still needs its remaining save and live refresh paths (app #953 onward), including routing invoice undo and copied phase-move Undo to the engine. New-schema change IDs and party IDs have different meanings from legacy save versions and client IDs.

The app routes watch settings to the [signed-in engine watch door](#deal-watches-and-nightly-checklist-reminders) when using engine sign-in.

The deal step editors need to send `seen_current_step` through door 18 before enabling the Deals save switch.

"Mark inactive" is outside #674 because the Deals spec calls for Park with the reason "prospect never active" and pass D had zero prospect clients.

The app does not display premises or offer-round summaries. Deal links use the UUIDs kept by the copy, so missing legacy deal references are invisible. Pass D kept the overall board count but placed person clients' national-account deals under Team. The brand copy now includes their dated memberships (#653, #697). The board places each deal under the client's current national account once that membership is copied. The account list uses the national accounts admitted by the copy, which excludes old prospect clients.

### People and company search for the deal page

`GET /api/engine/parties/search` uses the signed-in partner's session and reads through `DATABASE` as `engine_reader`. It takes one `query` of 1 to 100 characters, an optional `kind` of `person` or `company`, and an optional `role=client`. The read trims the query and matches a literal name prefix without regard to case.

The response is `200 { ok: true, schema_version: "party-search.v1", parties }`. Each result carries `id`, `display_name`, `kind`, `city`, `deal_count`, and `is_national_account`. The city is the party's recorded city or null. The deal count is the number of distinct deals linked directly to the party in `deal_party`, across all roles and phases. The national account flag is true when the company has a `national_account` row. The read returns the first 25 matches in case-insensitive name order, with ties ordered by ID. With no `kind`, the list can contain both people and companies. The Market agent picker will send `kind=person` so the limit applies to matching people. The list includes live parties, including do-not-contact records, and excludes merged-away duplicates.

Add deal sends `role=client` so the limit applies to people and companies with the client role. Use their `id` as `client_id` when saving the deal.

No match returns an empty `parties` list. Missing, blank, overlong, or repeated query values, queries containing a NUL character, and invalid or repeated `kind` or `role` values return 400. Session and database refusals use the shared read rules. App #977 routes the deal-page picker to this door.

### Add and edit people and companies

These saves use the signed-in partner's session, `idempotency_key` and the shared replay rules. Apply [the party edit install](../schema/allow-party-edits.sql) to an existing database first.

- `POST /api/engine/parties` takes `kind` (`person` or `company`) and a nonblank `name`, and optionally `email`, `phone`, `city`, and for a person `company_id` and `title`. It stores the email lower case and the phone in E.164 as primary work rows (§ "Emails and phone numbers"). A ten-digit number in any punctuation becomes +1 and its digits, and a trailing `x12` becomes the extension. A malformed email or phone, a company field on a company or without `company_id`, and a `company_id` that is not a company return `400 invalid_field_value` with the fields. A person's company becomes a current `party_company` row with the title.
- A same-kind name that matches ignoring case and surrounding spaces, or an email already on a record, returns `409 party_exists` with up to 10 `candidates`, carrying the deal-page search's match fields. Saves of an email take the same lock as the email-lead promotion, so two saves of one address are seen one after the other. Use a candidate, or resend with `force_new: true` to keep a separate record. The save answers `201` with the record.
- `GET /api/engine/parties/{id}` answers `{ party_id, kind, name, city, email, phone, company }`. `email` and `phone` are the primary rows, `phone` as `{ number, extension, shown }`, and `company` the current company link as `{ company_id, name, title }`, each or null.
- `POST /api/engine/parties/{id}` changes any of `name`, `city`, `email` and `phone`. Each is `{ from, to }`, where `from` is the value the partner saw (the phone's `shown`) and `to` the new value, or null to clear it. A name cannot be cleared. A field whose current value differs from `from` returns `409 version_conflict` with those `fields` and the `current` record as the read answers it; fields changed by someone else and not sent stand. An email already on another record returns `409 party_exists` with that record as a candidate, until the partner resends with `force_new: true`. A changed email or phone removes the primary row and any row with the new value, and adds the new value as the primary work row. It answers `200` with the record.
- The database refuses a blank name from the engine (`party_name_saved_nonblank`). Copied blank names stay until a partner names them.

### Add and edit clients and vendors

These saves follow the same session, replay and `{ from, to }` edit rules as people and companies. Choices come from their lists: an unknown `client_type`, `how_found`, vendor `stage` or vendor category returns `400 invalid_field_value` naming it, and the Clients and Vendors list facets supply the labels.

- `POST /api/engine/clients` takes `party_id` and optionally `status` (`roster`, `cold`, `engaged` or `paused`; roster when absent), `client_type`, `how_found`, `vertical` and `subtype`. A record whose how found is empty or Not recorded needs `how_found`. Not recorded marks a copied record nobody has asked about, so `not_recorded` from a partner returns `400 invalid_field_value`, here and in an edit. A subtype needs a practice type. It adds the client role and profile, owned by the signed-in partner, and answers `201 { party_id, status, client_type, how_found, vertical, subtype, owner }`. A record that already has a client profile returns `409 already_client`. A record a deal made a client without a profile gets one.
- `GET /api/engine/parties/{id}` also answers the stored `client` and `vendor` profiles in these shapes, or null. A client's stored status is the manual one, not the computed directory status.
- `POST /api/engine/clients/{id}` changes any of those fields. Status and how found cannot be cleared. A subtype belongs to the practice type it was recorded under, so clearing or changing the practice type clears the subtype unless the edit also sends one.
- `POST /api/engine/vendors` takes `party_id` and optionally `categories` (vendor category role slugs), `stage`, `relationship_level` (0 to 3), `owner` (`joe` or `dell`; the signed-in partner when absent), `offers`, `seeking`, `is_target`, `disposition` and `disposition_reason`. It adds the vendor role and a role for each category, and answers `201 { party_id, stage, relationship_level, owner, offers, seeking, is_target, disposition, disposition_reason, categories }`. A record that is already a vendor returns `409 already_vendor`.
- `POST /api/engine/vendors/{id}` changes any of those fields. `categories` is the whole list, so a removed category's role is removed and a new one added, and `categories: null` removes every category. An `is_target` edit to false turns Want to meet off, and one to null returns `400 invalid_field_value`, because the flag is never unknown. `owner` can be cleared to null, leaving the vendor without a relationship partner, as some copied vendors are. A ruling without a reason, or an avoided target, is refused by the database's vendor rules (`422 database_refused_the_statement` naming the rule).

### Record introductions

A partner records an introduction that happened, as § "Vendors" describes it. A referral is one the vendor made. Offered and requested introductions stay links (§ "Who knows whom"). These saves use the signed-in session, the shared replay rules, and change history.

- `POST /api/engine/introductions` takes `idempotency_key`, `vendor_id`, `made_by` (`vendor`, `team` or `client`), `introduced_party_id`, `introduced_on`, an optional nonblank `note`, and `deal_id` (required for a client maker). A team introduction defaults to the signed-in partner and accepts `introduced_by` for the other partner. Other makers refuse that field. Change history names the partner who saved it. The answer is `201 { introduction_id, version, vendor_id, made_by, introduced_by, introduced_party_id, introduced_on, deal_id, note }`. A client maker must be the named deal's client. The vendor introduced to itself answers `400 invalid_field_value`, a missing party or deal answers `404 not_found`, and a missing vendor role or wrong client answers `422 database_refused_the_statement` naming the constraint.
- With `unrecorded_only: true`, the save requires a deal and answers `409 introduction_already_recorded` if a confirmed introduction already names that vendor and deal. To correct a recovered save, `replaces` and `replace_version` name the introduction and version in the recovered receipt. The save removes that row and records the new choice in one transaction. An edit to that row since the receipt returns `409 version_conflict`. The check and save run under the introduction's vendor lock. Retrying the same request key returns its original receipt.
- `POST /api/engine/introductions/{id}` changes `introduced_on`, `deal_id` or `note` as `{ from, to }`. The date and note are required when changed. A client-made introduction must continue to name its client's deal. A missing introduction or deal answers `404 not_found`, and stale values return `409 version_conflict` with `fields` and `current`.
- `POST /api/engine/introductions/{id}/remove` takes `idempotency_key` and the `version` shown to the partner and answers `200 { introduction_id }` after removing a confirmed introduction recorded outside email capture. Email findings stay protected and answer `404 not_found`, as does a missing introduction. A stale version returns `409 version_conflict` and keeps the introduction. The deletion is in change history and leaves deal roles alone.

### Link people and companies

These saves follow the same session, replay and `{ from, to }` edit rules. They keep the links in § "Who knows whom"; recorded introductions stay with vendors.

- `POST /api/engine/party-links` takes `kind` (`knows` or `can_introduce`), `party_id`, `other_party_id`, and optionally `started_on` and `note`. For `can_introduce`, `party_id` can introduce the team to `other_party_id`. It answers `201 { link_id, kind, party_id, other_party_id, started_on, ended_on, note }`. A link of the same kind between the same parties with no end date returns `409 already_linked` with its `link_id`; a knows link matches either order. The same party twice returns 400 and an unknown party 404. Party ids are stored lower case, so one party spelled two ways is the same party and finds the same current link.
- `POST /api/engine/party-links/{id}` changes `started_on`, `ended_on` or `note`. Ending a link keeps its row; a later link is a new one. Clearing the end date of a link whose pair has another current link of its kind returns `409 already_linked` with that link's `link_id`. An end before the start is refused by the database (`party_link_ends_after_it_starts`).
- Relationships shows a link as an edge `link:{link_id}` from its start date, when it has one, until its end date, when it has one. A link ended on a later date still shows until then, though it no longer counts as current for the rule above.

## Deal page redesign

The owner approved the [deal page](screens/deals.md#deal-page) redesign on 2026-11-25. These lines are its design for the engine. The checklist grouping, deal attention, attention alerts and invoice dates are built. The rest of this section is not built yet.

### Deal attention

The deal read and the board read answer `attention` as `{ reason, since }` or null. `reason` is one sentence. `since` is the Central date (`America/Chicago`, `YYYY-MM-DD`) that sentence starts from. The board badge and the deal page's Needs attention line both show that sentence.

`POST /jobs/deals/attention-alerts` requires the jobs Access token, `Engine-Job: attention_alerts` and an `idempotency_key` UUID. The Studio calls it once a day from `carr-system`'s nightly run, like [checklist reminders](#deal-watches-and-nightly-checklist-reminders), behind its own switch that stays off until [the deal attention alerts migration](../schema/add-deal-attention-alerts.sql) is applied and the engine change is deployed. It sweeps deals in one transaction (`sweepDealAttentionAlerts`), locking the deals it acts on in `id` order (`for update`) and re-evaluating them under the lock before writing so concurrent sweeps and deal edits serialize without alerting a stale owner or deadlocking. When an open deal owned by an account partner gains a new reason or changes owner while attention holds, it inserts one `home_update` (`kind: "update"`, `what` set to the reason sentence, null `checklist_item_ref`) and upserts `deal_attention_alert`. The update appears on `/api/engine/updates` and its unread badge, not on Home Reminders. When the sweep runs inside the owner's `user_preference` quiet hours, `held_until` holds the update until `quiet_until` in the owner's time zone. A staying reason for the same owner does not repeat. When attention clears, or the deal is parked, ended or not owned by a partner, the `deal_attention_alert` row is deleted so a returning reason alerts again. The answer is `{ alerted, unchanged, cleared }`. Existing databases need [the deal attention alerts migration](../schema/add-deal-attention-alerts.sql) after owner approval before deploying the sweep. The phone push joins when engine Web Push ships (T3 idea 3).

The first match wins, in this order:

1. An open step is late. A step is a `next_step` on the deal with no checklist title (`item_title` is null). It is late when `due_on` is before today. Due today is not late. `since` is the earliest such `due_on` plus one day. Reason: "A next step is late."
2. A critical date is proposed or open, and `due_on` is today or within the next 7 days, or earlier. Done, dropped and passed dates do not count. `since` is the earliest such `due_on` minus 7 days. Reason: "A critical date is within 7 days or past and not done."
3. The deal has no open step and no pending suggestion. A checklist item is not a step. `since` is the latest Central day among finished steps (`done_at` or `resolved_at` on a step that is not open) and decided suggestions (`decided_at` on a proposal that is not pending). When none of those exist, `since` is the day the deal was created. Reason: "This deal has no next step and no suggestion."
4. An email filed to the deal has had no answer for 2 working days. The sender's address is on a person or company on the deal in the role client, client contact, listing agent or landlord rep, and that address is not on an `app_user` with `is_account_partner`. An answer is a later email on the same deal sent from an `app_user` with `is_account_partner`. It has to be in the same conversation when the inbound email has a conversation id. When the inbound email has none, any later partner email on the deal is an answer. A working day is a Monday through Friday. Holidays are ordinary working days. The wait is the second working day strictly after the email's Central day, and the rule matches when that day is today or earlier. `since` is the earliest such day. Reason: "A client or broker email has had no answer for 2 working days."
5. A pending suggestion's Central created day plus 2 calendar days is today or earlier. These are calendar days, not working days. `since` is the earliest such created day plus 2. Reason: "A suggestion has waited 2 days."
6. Nothing has happened for 14 days. The latest Central day among the deal's activities, its steps' created and done times, its phase changes and the day the deal was created, plus 14, is today or earlier. A day exactly 14 days ago matches. A cancellation's `resolved_at` and a suggestion's decision do not count as something happening. `since` is that day plus 14. Reason: "Nothing has happened for 14 days."

A parked deal, and a deal that is closed, lost, invoiced or otherwise ended, answers null. A parked deal still appears on the open board. A deal that matches none of the six answers null, including one whose stored `needs_attention` flag is set. The flag and its save stay. No screen reads the flag for a deal. A prospect keeps its hand-set flag. The numbers are the starting guess from the approved design.

### Invoice dates

The deal page shows three marks for each commission payment: triggered, sent and paid. The deal read answers `invoice` as `{ trigger, triggered_on, payments: [{ id, sent_on, paid_on }] }`. `trigger` is the event that lets the brokerage invoice: `lease_signed` for a lease or renewal, `purchase_closed` for a purchase or land deal, and null for a deal of no known type. `triggered_on` is the day it happened, or null before it: the current lease's `executed_on` for `lease_signed`, and the deal's close day for `purchase_closed`. It is read from those rows, never stored, so no column holds it. The trigger is deal-wide, so a deal with no payment yet still names it. Each payment's `sent_on` is its `invoiced_on` and `paid_on` its `received_on`, ordered by `sent_on` with unsent payments last.

### Checklist under the phases

The `deal-checklist.v1` read groups items under the eight deal phases, in order: `pending` Prospective Client, `research` Research, `site_selection` Site Selection, `negotiation` Negotiating, `legal` Legal, `due_diligence` Due Diligence, `closing` Closing, `closed` Closed. Research, Site Selection, Negotiating, Legal and Closing keep the items of the old groups with the same meaning. Invoice's two items sit under Closed. A purchase's diligence items stay under Legal. Prospective Client and Due Diligence have no items until the partners' workflow table arrives. Items still tick themselves from what is filed and recorded.

### Deal plan

The owner approved the timeline design on 2026-11-25. The deal page's [Plan](screens/deals.md#plan) tab and Deals [Timeline](screens/deals.md#timeline) draw each deal's checklist and steps in time. Recorded days on the deal read, typical lengths and the plan read are built; own dates follow.

**Placement.** The workflow is the [checklist under the phases](#checklist-under-the-phases), in its fixed order. Each row is recorded or expected, never both.

1. A finished to-do ends on the day it was recorded as finished. It starts on the day the to-do before it finished, or the day its phase began.
2. The to-do the deal is on starts where the last one finished, or at the last phase move. It runs to today and reports its day against its typical length, such as "day 9 of 21". Nothing records a percent done.
3. Each to-do ahead is expected. It starts where the one before is expected to end and runs its typical length, or ends on the partner's own date when one is set. Phases follow each other the same way.
4. Arrows follow the workflow's order. No link between to-dos is stored.
5. A step runs from its `created_at` day to its `due_on`. Both are recorded, so a step is never expected. A late step is marked late.
6. A critical date sits on its own day; a proposed one is marked proposed. A date whose kind names a to-do that must finish first gets an arrow from that to-do: LOI expiry follows Terms comparison, and lease commencement follows Commencement letter. Other kinds get no arrow.
7. A to-do expected to finish after the critical date it must beat makes that date at risk.
8. Each phase spans its days from `deal_phase_change`: spent time up to today, then expected time.
9. Move-in and the 90-day check-in sit outside the chain. Move-in sits on the deal's target move-in date when one is recorded, and the check-in 90 days after it.
10. A deal that has sat on its current to-do for more than twice its typical length is stale. It gets no expected days, and the plan says how long it has sat.
11. A to-do in a finished phase that never ticked is "not recorded" and has no days.

**Typical lengths.** One row per deal kind and to-do holds its typical days and who last changed it and when. They ship as these defaults for a lease deal and partners edit them in the Typical lengths panel; there is no confirmation step. Only the phase totals were measured, from at most three finished stays per phase. Purchase and land deals get their own rows later; until then they show order with no expected days.

| Phase              | To-do and typical days                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Prospective Client | 7 days before Research begins                                                                                                                     |
| Research           | Representation agreement 7 · Intake and requirements 7                                                                                            |
| Site Selection     | Market survey 7 · Tours and tour notes 12                                                                                                         |
| Negotiating        | LOIs sent 14 · Terms comparison 21 · Agreed business terms 20                                                                                     |
| Legal              | Lease draft to attorney 10 · Attorney comments 21 · Healthcare lease review 10 · TI scope and bids 14                                             |
| Due Diligence      | Nothing listed for a lease                                                                                                                        |
| Closing            | Executed lease 14 · Commencement letter 10 · Executed comp 7 · Lease end recorded 6 · Move-in on its date · 90-day check-in 90 days after move-in |
| Closed             | Invoice 3 · Commission received 30                                                                                                                |

Once a to-do has five finished examples with recorded days, the panel shows the measured median beside the default and offers **Use measured**. A number changes only on a partner's edit or click.

`typical_length` stores `(deal_kind, todo, days)` with the standard change stamps (`schema/add-typical-lengths.sql`, seeded for `lease` by `schema/seed-typical-lengths.sql`). Prospective Client's stay uses the slug `before_research`; the other lease rows use their checklist item slugs (`move_in` sits on its own date and has no row). `GET /api/engine/typical-lengths` takes an optional `deal_kind` (default `lease`) and returns `{ deal_kind, items }` in workflow order, each with `phase`, `todo`, `label`, `days`, `changed_by` (the partner slug, or null while still the seeded default), `changed_at`, `base_version`, `measured_days` (rounded median when at least five positive recorded examples exist for that deal kind, else null; only Before Research, Invoice and Commission received are measured, and Executed lease stays null because Closing begins at signing and the design gives it no start day) and `measured_count`. `POST /api/engine/typical-lengths` takes `{ idempotency_key, deal_kind, todo, days, base_version }` for a signed-in partner, checks `base_version` against the row's latest change id (`409 version_conflict`), saves the positive whole-day count and returns `{ deal_kind, todo, days, changed_by, changed_at, base_version }`.

**Expected signing date.** Each open lease deal's expected signing date is the day its Executed lease to-do is recorded or expected to finish. A stale deal has none.

**A partner's own date.** A partner may set their own end day on one of a deal's to-dos by dragging its expected bar. It is the only plan date stored. It saves through the change history, so Undo restores the one before, and Reset to typical clears the deal's own dates. It cannot be a past day. The column or table needs the owner's yes on the schema file's commit before production.

**The plan read.** One pure engine function (`src/deals/plan.ts`) takes a deal's checklist, phase spans, steps, critical dates, the typical lengths of its deal kind and today in Central time; own dates join it when they are stored. It returns every row with its recorded or expected days and state, the arrows, the at-risk dates, the expected signing date and the stale flag. The new-schema deal read serves it as `plan`. The board read will give each open deal its phase spans, expected signing date, stale flag, at-risk count and current phase's rows from the same function. Expected days are worked out on every read and never saved, so they never appear on Home or Calendar.

`plan` is `{ phases, rows, arrows, expected_signing_on, stale }`. Recorded days are `from_on` and `to_on`; expected days are `expected_from_on` and `expected_to_on`, so no field holds both.

- `phases` lists the eight phases in order, each with its last recorded span's `from_on` and `to_on` up to the current phase, `expected_from_on` for a phase ahead and `expected_to_on` for the current phase and those ahead. Invoice's checklist items are already on Closed, and an invoiced deal counts as Closed.
- A `todo` row has `id` (the checklist slug), `phase`, `label`, `state`, `typical_days` and `day`. `state` is `finished`, `current`, `expected`, `not_recorded` or `undated`. The to-do the deal is on is the first unfinished one in the current phase; a phase with none leaves the chain to start today. Its `day` counts from its start, the start being day 1; a start no record holds gives no `day`, never stale, and an expected end its typical length after today. A to-do past its typical length but not stale is expected to end today. Move-in and the 90-day check-in are `undated` until move-in is recorded; then the check-in is expected 90 days later. Prospective Client's stay (`before_research`) leads the chain without a row.
- A `step` row (`step:<id>`) is an open or done step with `owner`, `state` (`open`, `late` or `done`), `from_on` its set day, `to_on` its due day and `done_on`. It sits under the phase the deal was in on its set day. Cancelled and expired steps are left out.
- A `critical_date` row (`critical-date:<id>`) is a proposed, open or cleared date with `label`, `on`, `status` and `at_risk`, under the phase of the to-do it follows, else no phase. A cleared date is never at risk.
- `arrows` are `{ from, to, at_risk }`: one between each to-do with days and the next one in workflow order, skipping rows without days and the two that sit off the chain, then one from a to-do to each date that follows it.
- Nothing is expected for a lost deal, whose unfinished to-dos are `not_recorded`, or for a deal whose phase is not one of the eight. A to-do with no typical length, such as every purchase and land to-do and a renewal's own ones, ends the chain: it and every to-do after it are expected with no days.
- `stale` is `{ phase, days }`, the days the deal has sat on its current to-do or in Prospective Client, or null.

**Recorded days on the deal read.** `phase_spans` lists each `deal_phase_change` row in order with its `phase`, `from_on` and `to_on`, as Central days. The current phase has no `to_on`, and a phase the import seeded without move evidence has no `from_on`. Each step in `next_actions` gives `set_on`, the day of its `created_at`, and `done_on`, the day of its `done_at`. Each checklist item gives `finished_on` when its record holds the day: the lease's `executed_on` for the executed lease, the day the lease's first version was saved for the lease expiration, so an amendment does not move it, the deal's `invoiced_on`, the last `received_on` once every payment is received, and the day a document request was fulfilled. Every other item's `finished_on` is null, and the old-schema read leaves the lease expiration's `finished_on` null too.

### Doc skill sessions

A button on a deal or lead starts a Doc session for one record and one skill, as [Doc](screens/doc.md#start-doc-from-a-button) describes. A typed list in engine code names each skill, `client_website`, `requirement_email`, `compare_lois` and `lead_outreach`, with its record kind, its chat title and the skill Doc runs. A session is a [Doc conversation](#doc-conversations-and-turns) tied to its record and skill, so the button reopens the same chat. The deal and lead reads give each skill's state: not started, working, waiting on the partner, or done on a date. A finished job or a question from Doc sends one update and one phone push through Updates. A file dropped in the session goes through the existing upload door to the record's documents, and Doc reads its extracted text. Not decided: the storage that ties a conversation to its record and skill, and what the other partner sees, since a Doc conversation is private to its owner.

### Send a new email from Doc

**Approve and send** sends a new email, not a reply, from the signed-in partner's own mailbox, with exactly the recipients, subject and body on the card. Each recipient gets their own copy. Only the partner's click sends, under the [confirmed save](#confirmed-saves-and-receipts) rules, and the model has no route to send. The engine files the sent email to the deal or lead and answers sent or failed. A failed send keeps the draft for **Retry**. Not checked: how much of [reply delivery](#22-reply-drafts-and-their-delivery) and the 2026-11-21 change that lets Doc start a reply already covers this.

## Global Search and Recent Activity

Set `SEARCH_READ_SCHEMA=next` for `GET /api/engine/find` and `ACTIVITY_READ_SCHEMA=next` for `GET /api/engine/recent-activity`. Each read uses the public schema until its variable is set to `next`.

Search finds current deals by case-insensitive name. It finds contacts by name, including fuzzy matches ranked by similarity, or by exact email address. The response carries the deal owner's current user slug, the contact's current role, and the specialty label. Search matches and connection endpoints use the copied typed reference from `outside_id` when unique for the role, or the next ID. Merged-away names stay out of the results. Lead and client links come from shared roles or a current company relationship. Related deals include those that name a matching party as a client contact.

With the next read enabled, `X-DoctorCRE-Search: search.v1` requests the Search screen's response. It returns `schema_version: "search.v1"`, `parties` and `deals`. A person or company appears once under its canonical Relationships ID, with its name, kind, city, roles, linked deals and Leads drawer ID when it has one. Roles name a lead only for a lead on the Leads board, which always carries its drawer ID. Linked deals include the party's deals and its current companies' client deals. Each linked deal carries its ID and name. Each direct deal match also carries its current phase and owner. An email query matches a complete stored address, or the exact domain of a stored address, with or without a leading `@`. Address and domain matches ignore case and spaces around the query. Other queries match by name or reference. The read returns up to 15 people or companies, five direct deal matches and 20 linked deals per person or company. Clients that omit the header still get the original response.

Recent Activity requires `query` and accepts an optional `ref` on both paths. The next read resolves `ref` first, preferring a canonical reference over a shared ID or a copied alias. If `ref` is missing or has no match, the read uses `query`. A query prefers an exact reference or ID, then an exact name. After these preferences are applied, each contact's party ID counts as one match across all roles. An exact role reference stays the response's match target. Multiple distinct records, including people with the same name, return a list to disambiguate. The public read uses `query` as before.

The next timeline shows calls, emails, meetings, notes, and next steps linked to the selected deal or contact. A client's timeline also shows those linked to its deals, to its current companies, and to the current people at those companies, newest first and once each. Each entry filed somewhere other than the selected record carries `filed_on` with the kind (`deal`, `company` or `person`) and name of the record it was filed to; the selected record's own entries carry null. An activity filed to more than one of these is named by the first of the client, a deal, a company and a person. Both partners see every call and its summary, including any accepted notes. Email `detail` carries the stored body. Other rows have null `detail`, and `owed` is null for all rows. A next step carries `step_owner`, its owner's user slug such as `joe`, or null when it has no owner; every other row carries null. Calendar history contains the signed-in partner's confirmed meetings for that record.

## Relationships read for the app

The app uses `GET /api/engine/relationships?contract=relationship-network.v1` when `relationships-from-engine` is on. Set `RELATIONSHIPS_READ_SCHEMA=next` on the engine to read the new database as `engine_reader`. An absent or different value uses the old tables.

The graph includes people, companies, and deals, including contacts with no links. Nodes use `party:{id}` and `deal:{id}` and carry that record's UUID in `record_id`. The copy keeps surviving party and deal IDs and uses `outside_id` to map imported role and participant references to those records. Home uses the same party ID for a step's destination, including a step copied from an old role. Party ownership comes from the current vendor, client, or lead records. An unclaimed lead has a null owner. The party summary shows the vendor offer, then the copied client or lead note. The territory filter uses a current saved territory or the party's city. Only active contact holds show. Deal ownership, phase, and outcome match the Deals read. A blank name excludes the record and its connections and referral credit.

Edges show typed links during their active dates, recorded introductions, and deal participants. A team introduction names a separate recorded introducer in `via`. Deal connections appear once per party, deal and kind, with dates, roles and notes in the details when recorded. A vendor introduction counts once per deal toward referrals. Offered introductions remain visible on switched reads, which send `can_request_introductions: false` to hide the request action. Old brokered links and referral credit without a copied typed link or introduction stay out of the graph, as do uncopied `works_with` and `intro_requested` links.

## Saved action links

`GET /api/engine/action-links/{ref}` requires a PartnerApp session and takes no query parameters. Pass the `al1_` reference from an existing `/a/{ref}` link. The encoded reference is an address. The engine checks access with the session and reads the new database without changing records.

| Reference | When the link opens                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Success response                                                                               |
| --------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `rd`      | The signed-in owner can still review the named pending draft. If the draft was replaced, open the newest pending reply for the same owner and Outlook conversation that the owner can read. An older version in the link opens the current draft for a fresh review. | `{ kind: "reply_draft", path }` with the exact `/api/engine/home/cards/reply_draft:{id}` read. |
| `ld`      | The copied outreach draft still points to a lead visible on the shared Leads board.                                                                                                                                                                                  | `{ kind: "lead", path }` with `/leads?lead={id}` using the board's lead ID.                    |
| `cr`      | Retired. Open call review from the deal page.                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Unavailable.                                                                                   |
| `fe`      | Retired.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Unavailable.                                                                                   |

Finished reply drafts without a visible successor, missing or inaccessible targets, malformed references, and retired links all return the same `404 action_link_unavailable` body and headers, with the hint “This link is no longer available.” This route omits `Server-Timing`. A signed-out request returns `401`.

## Home inbox reads for the app

Slice 23 starts with `GET /api/engine/home/inbox`. Set `HOME_INBOX_READ_SCHEMA` to `next` on the engine to enable the read. It uses `DATABASE` as `engine_reader` and reads the signed-in partner's work and marks. With the variable unset, the route returns 404 and the app still calls CARR. The live inbox uses the request without a query parameter.

The [Home dashboard](screens/home-inbox.md) requests `?view=dashboard` for **Reminders**, **Inbox** and **In motion and waiting**. The response gives `reminders` and `waiting`, each with `count` and all eligible `items` in activity order. With `&contract=home-inbox.v6` it answers `schema_version: "home-inbox.v6"` and splits the open cards in two: `reminders` keeps steps, call follow-ups and checklist reminders, and `inbox` holds every decision, meaning reply drafts, suggestions, call and meeting reviews, completion reviews and tour feedback. Every card carries `tile`, `reminders` or `inbox`, so a waiting card names where it returns. The v6 contract is refused without `view=dashboard`. Without it the dashboard keeps the v5 shape. The app takes each preview from those items and uses the same list for **See all**. Use `card_id` to keep a card in place when its leading decision changes. Use `item_id` and `base_version` for saves. Both views return the same nullable `days_late` in Central calendar days for the same card, including completion reviews and dated decisions. Dashboard cards also give `late_label`, `waiting_reason`, and `returns_at`.

The flat list returns `days_late: null` when the dashboard omits a checklist reminder because it already shows the linked step or the linked checklist item is due later.

`GET /api/engine/home/cards/{item_id}` uses the same Home switch and signed-in partner. It takes a `reply_draft:<uuid>` or `suggestion:<uuid>` ID and no query parameters. A reply draft link opens the named draft even if another draft leads the conversation in the dashboard list. A suggestion link returns the dashboard card containing that review, with the current leading decision, each pending decision shown once, and any draft the card carries. Pending suggestions that repeat a filing decision still resolve to that card. When a suggestion link resolves to a card with a different `item_id`, the response includes `requested_item_id` with the linked ID. A finished review returns `{ item_id, state }`, with `state` equal to `done`, `dropped`, or `replaced` and `reason` included when a reason was saved. An unknown or unauthorized review returns `404 not_found`.

Reminders includes open steps due today or earlier and undated follow-ups on the same eligible records as the [Today panel](#today-panel). Past-due deal and vendor cards get calendar days late in Central time. Pending reply drafts and other partner decisions each appear once and belong in Inbox. The v6 contract returns them in `inbox`. The v5 contract keeps them in `reminders`. Held, snoozed, and processing work belongs in In motion and waiting. A future dated step stays off Home until its due day. Lead-only steps and their completion reviews stay with the lead, including held or snoozed steps. Completed steps, sent drafts, and other finished cards stay in their record history. Calendar outcome reviews join today's meeting tile when the meeting is in it. An outstanding outcome for an earlier day belongs in Inbox.

The response uses the app's `home-inbox.v5` card fields and carries `home_mark_drop: true` and `home_proposal_drop: true`. Steps, call follow-ups, completion reviews, suggestion groups, reply drafts, checklist reminders and tour feedback use a plain-word `title`, a one-line `why` from recorded facts and a `primary_action` label. Step cards follow the [Home step title rule](screens/home-inbox.md#every-item) and include the full display instruction in `why` when it differs from the heading. Pending decisions use the title for their kind with the recorded name and put the saved instruction, summary, or feedback in `why`. The app uses existing IDs and links to run the action. Display text omits copied client, lead and vendor codes, loop numbers, lease offsets and Markdown. The saved step and source evidence keep their original text. Dropped call follow-ups, Home updates and failed reply drafts stay off Home for that user. It includes the step's date, destination and linked source when known. A call source includes its date, contact when known, summary and quote, for both partners. An assigned follow-up from the other partner's call opens the deal step. An accepted suggestion links a step to its source. The step's change ID gives `action_version`, and `base_version` includes the mark's revision and current disposition. An available Undo uses the exact `action_version` of the signed-in owner’s direct completion. A step completed by accepting a proposal has no Undo. A step card's `covers` lists each copy it stands for under the [copies rule](screens/home-inbox.md#every-item), newest first and itself included, as `{ item_id, action_id, action_version, base_version, record_name }`, and `merged_source_ids` lists the same step IDs. The app writes each copy with its own versions. A completion review card has no `covers`.

A deal-step card has `open_url` set to `/deals?deal={deal_id}&focus=next-step&step={next_step_id}`. The `step` value is the `next_step.id` of the card's step, also carried as `action_id`. Match it to the step row's `id` in the deal detail's `next_actions`. New-schema deal detail adds `display_title` and `display_instruction` using the same rule, alongside the stored `title` and `description`. `display_instruction` is null when the instruction is empty or matches `display_title`. Those rows return the recorded creation time as `set_at`, the creator's name as `set_by` when known, and `set_from` for a linked call, email, meeting or automatic follow-up schedule. Copies without creation history have no attributed creator. Call follow-ups open the call. A building-only step uses `/deals?deal={deal_id}` when exactly one open deal is linked through `deal_property`. Its `open_url` is null when zero or several linked deals are open.

The prospect detail link `/deals?prospect={party_id}&focus=follow-up&step={next_step_id}` remains available from the prospect. The prospect detail accepts `?step={next_step_id}` and returns the requested completed follow-up as `completed_follow_up`, with `display_title` as in the lane read, or `null` if that step is unavailable on the prospect. Open steps match by `follow_ups[].next_step_id`. Both carry the same attribution fields as deal steps. The lane and its ordering use open follow-ups.

A person or company step that opens in Relationships uses `/relationships?node=party:{party_id}&step={next_step_id}` with the canonical party ID. On the next-schema Relationships read, `GET /api/engine/relationships?contract=relationship-network.v1&step={next_step_id}` includes `focused_step`, with the step's ID, party ID, text, status and the same attribution fields. It returns `null` when the requested step is unavailable or belongs to another partner. The app shows it on the matching node. Turn on the app's Relationships routing and the engine's next-schema Relationships read when switching Home to the new database.

An open step can be held, snoozed or in the inbox. A snooze ends at its saved time, and a hold ends on its date in Central time. Cancelled, expired, and completed steps leave the inbox. Eligible open steps stay on Home even when the saved record lacks context. Use the saved wording, cleaned for display, and say when no reason was recorded.

Reply drafts are visible to their owner while pending, sending, or failed. One active draft appears for each known Outlook conversation. An approved draft takes precedence while it sends. Otherwise the draft answering the newest email appears. An email without a conversation ID is grouped by its own activity ID. The card gives the draft ID and the email it answers. A pending draft offers Review reply. A failed draft on its own card offers Review delivery and explains the recorded failure in plain words. A failed draft whose reason is not `unconfirmed_after_<n>` leaves Home once its owner's own reply to the draft's recipient in that Outlook conversation, sent after approval, is in their mailbox. Its link then reads as `replaced`. Nothing changes what it may become. Sending and snoozed draft cards have a null `primary_action`. When a pending draft and visible suggestions share a conversation, the draft card carries the pending `suggestions` and is the only card for that work. The draft card keeps the leading suggestion's due date and any late label. Snooze applies to the whole card. When the draft is approved, sent, failed or declined, remaining suggestions use the suggestion card. An approved or failed draft in this group travels on the suggestion card as `fallback_reply_draft: { id, version, status, failure_reason }`. The card keeps its `card_id` and moves to In motion and waiting while Outlook delivery is unconfirmed, including when a suggestion leads. The card's `base_version` accounts for both the draft and the suggestions while both are on it.

An assigned next-step suggestion appears only for its owner. Other pending email suggestions follow the source email's mailbox membership or the recorded partner the job acted for. A call or meeting can reach the partner who recorded it or had it recorded for them. A suggestion to log a call uses the partner stamp on its source call. When a copied call has no partner stamp, its Log a call suggestion uses the reviewer recorded on the copied proposal. One card groups the suggestions from each known email conversation, call, or meeting. Call and meeting groups give the source time in `source_activity.happened_at` and name the first pending decision and its destination in the title and why line, including the proposed phase or lead stage when applicable. An email suggestion card uses the first pending decision in review order. Both the suggestion and draft card use the saved action title and summary for the exact source email behind the draft or decision. Add the decision count when there is more than one. For older mail or an unavailable result, use the title for the exact pending decision and put the saved request or recorded facts in `why`. When the saved title describes an action other than the pending decision, the pending decision determines the title. A possible lead can join a group by conversation ID. Its card uses candidate facts unless the exact Message-ID matches. `source_activity` is null for leads without a verified source message. A snoozed email suggestion has a null `primary_action`. In both suggestion and draft cards, `source_activity.happened_at` gives the source email's time. Each suggestion member carries its proposal ID, activity, and target. An assigned suggestion from the other partner's call shows that call as its source, and the proposal detail gives its evidence. A job suggests filing on each message of a conversation, so the group lists each filing or choose-the-deal decision once, as its newest suggestion. Accepting or dropping it settles every pending suggestion with the same kind and deal on the partner's messages in that conversation, filing or dropping each message. The decision locks all of those suggestions together, so a second partner deciding another of them gets `409 already_decided`. A completion review belongs to its own card kind. The inbox read does not decide any suggestion.

The current Home read can show old `email_lead` proposals. With [Leads from email](#leads-from-email) live, exclude that kind from Home groups, including when it has an email in the same Outlook conversation. An existing reply draft or other pending decision in that conversation stays available.

An open step shows as a completion review while its latest completion suggestion is pending. The card opens the exact deal step at `/deals?deal={deal_id}&focus=next-step&step={next_step_id}` and carries the source activity and evidence, whichever partner recorded the call. Use `completion_review.proposal_id` to accept or dismiss the review in door 21. If the step changed and is still open, the review can still be dismissed. A pending call completion review returns 404 from the decision door once the step ends. The review uses the underlying step's `item_id`, `base_version` and `action_version`. When the step is snoozed, its ordinary step card stays on the snoozed shelf and shows when it returns.

The read shows unread, undropped Home updates to their recipients. A checklist reminder leaves Home on the next read if its linked item is no longer open or its deal is parked or closed. It returns if the item and deal reopen and the reminder is still unread and undropped. A nightly reminder also leaves if its linked item is deleted. Older reminders without a matching checklist item stay visible on active, open deals. Under the Home screen spec, keep tour feedback and reminders for unfinished checklist items on Home. Informational updates stay on the deal's Timeline, where their source changes remain visible. A checklist update carries `kind: "update"`, its checklist reference and due date. A tour feedback update carries `kind: "tour_feedback"`, `kind_label: "Tour feedback"`, and links to `/deals?deal={deal_id}&property={property_id}` with `property_id` when a property is set. A snoozed update stays on the snoozed shelf with its return time until the snooze ends. The update's `base_version` uses its latest change ID, the latest change ID for its `home_update` mark, and the visible disposition. A missing change ID is `0`. Clearing the mark keeps its latest change ID.

The email proposal and draft detail reads give the selected source and a `thread` of messages from that source's Outlook conversation. The signed-in partner must have each message, including the source, in `next.email_mailbox` for it to appear in the thread. Home lists an email proposal or draft only when its source is available to the viewer under the same rule as its detail. A source with mailbox membership but absent from the viewer's mailbox stays off Home and returns 404 on detail. A legacy source without mailbox membership keeps its existing detail and has an empty thread. When the authorized source has no conversation ID, the thread contains just the source. Each entry gives `activity_id`, `sender_name`, `happened_at`, `body` (the visible text), and `folded` (a string of additional text, or `null`). The [authored text design](#one-entry-for-each-message) defines which messages appear and how these fields are filled. The Home list carries source references.

For a legacy message without a split, the [Home email cleanup rules in decision 200](decisions.md) still apply. When the surviving text all folds, it becomes `body` and `folded` is `null`. When removal leaves no surviving text, the read displays the original body.

From Home's reply review, **Talk to Doc about this draft** uses the authorized source email and exact pending draft ID and version. The partner confirms Doc's proposed text before the app applies it through the versioned [pending draft edit](#open-save-or-decline-a-reply-draft-from-home). A changed draft needs another review. The existing Doc flow for opening or creating a draft and the separate submission save keep their own rules.

Some copied steps lack a verified origin, and some completed steps lack a known completion time. The app uses new change IDs only with new-schema saves.

### Mail priorities

The redesigned Home does not call this read. The Doc mail lookup still uses it.

`GET /api/engine/home/mail-priorities` uses the same Home switch, signed-in partner and read-only database role. It takes no query parameters. It ranks pending inbound email filing, possible deals, next steps, phase moves and reply drafts that appear on Home. A snoozed card stays off the list until it returns to the inbox.

The read keeps one row per Outlook conversation. An email without an Outlook conversation ID is its own group, keyed by its source email ID. The earliest due suggestion comes first. Rows without a due date follow. At the same due date, including when dates are absent, it ranks the oldest source email first. The response is `{ ok, schema_version: "home-mail-priorities.v1", items, more }`. It lists at most 25 rows and sets `more` when more groups qualify.

A row gives `item_id` and `base_version` from its Home card, `kind`, `conversation_id`, `title`, `sender`, `subject`, `suggestion`, `due_on` and `source_activity: { kind: "email", id, happened_at }`. Use `proposal_id` or `reply_draft_id` to open the selected review from the matching Home card. The other ID is null. For a grouped suggestion, the sender and subject come from the newest visible email in the conversation. The selected proposal and source email can be older. When a pending draft leads the card, the row opens the draft and its email. An eligible nested suggestion can still determine the row’s due date and rank. Home formats the dates in Central time.

### Today panel

The redesigned Home uses only `meetings` for its **Today's meetings** tile. The shared frame can still read both sections on other screens. Home reminders come from the inbox above. An unavailable meetings read does not clear Home reminders.

`GET /api/engine/home/today` uses the same `HOME_INBOX_READ_SCHEMA=next` switch, signed-in partner, and read-only database role. Only the two partners may call it; another user gets `403 AUTHORIZATION_REFUSED`. Its optional `owner` query parameter accepts `all` (default) or one of the two partners (`joe`, `dell`), refusing anything else with `400 unknown_owner`.

The response is `{ ok: true, schema_version: "home-today.v1", today, viewer, owner, partners, meetings, reminders }` with no Deal pulse section. `meetings` and `reminders` fail independently: a read failure in either section returns `state: "unavailable"` with a plain `reason` while the other section still loads.

`meetings.items` lists the signed-in partner's stored occurrences that overlap today's Central calendar day, including those that already ended. Each row carries its Outlook event `key` when present. The row includes attendees and location from the snapshot. Non-user organizer and attendee emails match to open deals and parties (`possible_deals`, `context_label`, `open_url`).

`meetings.meetings_may_be_missing` is true when a successful agenda has an `undecoded` warning. The shared Today panel shows meetings when present. A valid read with no items says “No meetings today.” Today never shows the flag to partners (owner, 2026-11-25). A failed or invalid read says “Couldn’t load meetings.”

`follow_up_reviews` omits dismissed and confirmed reviews. Answering a review leaves its meeting in `meetings.items`. If the snapshot has only a review for an occurrence, the engine uses its key, title, and times for that meeting. The complete list and full details for ended meetings depend on the Outlook publisher sending their calendar rows. [Outlook publisher change](https://github.com/DoctorCRE/carr-system/issues/3265)

`reminders` splits the two partners' open dated `next_step` rows due on or before today (excluding future holds, active snoozes, and dropped call follow-ups; deal critical dates are not reminders) into `late` and `upcoming`. Like the Home dashboard, it lists steps on deals, buildings, parties with a vendor role, and people at one or more current vendor companies. A vendor qualifies even when the party or one of the person's companies also has a lead role. A company link is current if its start is unknown or on or before the request's Central date and its end is absent or after that date. Steps tied only to leads or other records are excluded. Holds and snoozes end by the request time. A healthy section has `reason: null`. Each `late` item carries calendar `days_late` and `late_label` (`"1 day late"`, `"N days late"`). Each step owned by the signed-in partner carries its canonical Home card `home_item_id` for the inbox without a query parameter, even when the inbox list cuts it off. Other steps have `home_item_id: null`. Today includes call follow-ups whichever partner recorded the call. The link opens the call or its step. A step on an ended deal also carries `deal_ended: true` so the Deals Today panel can omit it.

Each reminder's `title` uses the Home step card's title rules. The `why` field carries the full display instruction and is null when the instruction is empty or matches the heading. When the linked card shows a completion review, the reminder uses that review’s title and why. The panel keeps a meeting read's failure status visible without directing partners to Outlook.

### Calendar read

`GET /api/engine/calendar/critical-dates` returns `calendar-critical-dates.v1`. With `DEALS_READ_SCHEMA=next`, either signed-in partner can read both partners' dates and steps in one call. Each `critical_dates` row carries `id`, `deal_id`, `deal_name`, the deal owner's slug as `owner`, a display label as `kind`, `due_on` and `done: false`. The read includes only confirmed open dates on open deals, from any month, so a past date shows when the partner pages back to it. The legacy read retains its existing date scope and omits `next_steps`.

The new-schema `next_steps` list uses the same rules as Today reminders for open status, partner ownership, eligible records, a finite due date, holds, snoozes, and dropped follow-ups. Calendar also includes future due dates. Each row carries `id`, `what`, `why`, `owner`, `record_kind`, `record_label`, `due_on`, `display_on`, `days_late` and `open_url`. `what` is the Home card title. `why` carries the full display instruction and is null when the instruction is empty or matches the heading. `record_kind` is `deal`, `vendor` or `building`, the kind of record the step is on, and `record_label` is that record's name. `display_on` is the later of the due day and today's Central day. Dates use `YYYY-MM-DD`, and `days_late` is zero unless the step is overdue. A deal step links to its deal, including a call follow-up. A vendor or vendor contact step links to the Relationships record. A building step links to its sole open deal when there is one and otherwise has `open_url: null`.

### Calendar meetings read

`GET /api/engine/calendar/meetings?from=YYYY-MM-DD&to=YYYY-MM-DD` returns `calendar-meetings.v1` behind the same `HOME_INBOX_READ_SCHEMA=next` switch and partner check as [Today](#today-panel). `from` and `to` are inclusive Central days, at most 42 days apart counting both ends; a bad or missing date returns `400 invalid_field_value`, and a reversed or longer range returns `400 invalid_range`. Both partners see every meeting (decision 210), so either partner gets the same answer.

The response is `{ ok, schema_version, from, to, meetings, tours }`. `meetings` has one section per partner, ordered by slug, each `{ partner, name, state, reason, observed_at, meetings_may_be_missing, notes, items }`. A section is built exactly as Today's `meetings` is, over the range instead of one day, with the same freshness and gap notes and the same deal matching. It leaves out `follow_up_reviews` and their notes, so a meeting awaiting its outcome review never makes a section partial. Each partner's section fails on its own. The Outlook snapshot covers only about the next fourteen days, so days before today and days more than about two weeks ahead come back with no meetings. `tours` lists planned tours on open deals in the range, neither completed nor cancelled, as `{ id, deal_id, deal_name, owner, planned_on, stop_count, open_url }`, and fails on its own.

## Invoice tracker reads for the app

Home and Invoices use `GET /api/engine/invoices` when `invoices-from-engine` is `on` in the app's `ENGINE_ROUTING` KV. Set the engine Worker's `INVOICES_READ_SCHEMA` to `next` to read through `DATABASE` as `engine_reader`, the same way the Deals reads do. An absent setting or any other value keeps the old-table read. Set the engine variable before routing the app to the new read. Remove the variable to return the engine to the old-table read, or set the app switch to `off` to route to CARR.

The tracker lists each commission payment on an included deal. An undated payment has status `expected` and shows as Awaiting invoice, including when another payment on the deal has been invoiced. A dated payment shows as paid when it has a receipt date. The amount and due date come from the payment. A closed or invoiced deal with no payments has one row with a blank amount. If the copy kept an invoice date for that deal, the row shows as invoiced. Undo uses the restored phase and invoice date on the next read. Expected commission is a pipeline estimate. Home shows only unpaid rows with an explicit past due date.

The payment's `base_version` in `invoice-tracker.v1` is its latest change ID, or `null` if the database has no recorded change for that payment. With `invoices-from-engine` on, the app also routes Mark paid to the engine (app #958). Keep the production tracker on the old read until the production writer can save to the populated new schema.

### Mark paid

`POST /api/engine/invoices/mark-paid` records the full receipt of one invoiced, unpaid commission payment for the signed-in partner. The JSON request requires `commission_id` (the payment ID from the tracker), `base_version` (the tracker row's change ID or explicit `null`), `received_on` (a calendar date in `YYYY-MM-DD`) and `idempotency_key` (a UUID). The receipt date runs from the payment's invoice date through the database's current date, inclusive.

The save locks the payment and checks the version before recording the receipt. A `null` version matches only while the payment has no recorded change. A successful save answers `200 { ok, id, received_on, base_version }` with the new change ID. Same-key retries return the original answer with `replayed: true`. Refreshing the tracker shows the received date and removes the payment from Home's overdue invoices.

An omitted or invalid `base_version` returns `400`. Valid versions are `null` and nonnegative safe integers. An unknown payment returns `404 invoice_not_found`. An outdated version returns `409 version_conflict { current_version }`. A payment already received or awaiting an invoice returns `422 invoice_not_unpaid`. A date before the invoice or after today returns `422 payment_date_out_of_range`. Invoice-email application and undo control when the deal was invoiced. Mark paid records when the money was received.

## Vendors directory reads for the app

The vendor meeting planner (`GET /api/engine/vendor-meetings`) serves only Research, including when no view is supplied. Suggested is refused like any unknown view. The empty Suggested answer kept in PR #1110 is gone. [Meeting planner](screens/vendors.md#meeting-planner).

The Vendors directory (`GET /api/engine/vendors`) and drawer (`GET /api/engine/vendors/:id`) read from the new schema through `DATABASE` as `engine_reader` when the engine Worker's `DIRECTORY_READ_SCHEMA` variable is `next` (and `clients-vendors-from-engine` is `on` in the app). When `DIRECTORY_READ_SCHEMA` is absent or any other value, the old-table read stays active.

The list and drawer show live vendors and their current relationship partner. Copied vendors open by their old UUID, their party UUID or their copied `V-*` ref. The drawer returns the requested UUID as `id` for UUID lookups and the party UUID for ref lookups, and always answers `party_id`, the party UUID the party and vendor saves take. Intro notes show the copied intro text. Other notes appear in Conversations.

Track record counts come from `vendor_track_record`. A deal counts even when its work date is unknown. The first and last work dates use participation saved through the new system. A dated introduction that led to a deal can also set the Last deal date. The old coverage verification timestamp, manual override and rating edit version return null.

**Later.** The copy leaves vendor work dates unknown. Preserving them needs a design decision about a nullable participation date or a field for its source.

### Vendors redesign reads

The [Vendors](screens/vendors.md) redesign the owner approved on 2026-11-25 uses the reads below. Each reads live vendors from the new schema, and `owner` is `all`, `joe` or `dell`, the vendor's relationship partner. A vendor with no partner counts only under `all`. Relationship is the word from § "Vendors", and quiet is the vendor rule of `GET /api/engine/quiet-follow-ups`: an active vendor with no contact in 90 days and no open next step.

**Vendor summary.** `GET /api/engine/vendors/summary`, optional `owner`, `all` when absent, answers the category tiles in one read: `{ all, no_category, categories: [{ slug, label, ...counts }] }`. Each `counts` is `{ count, relationship: { core, established, building, prospective, not_rated, parked, avoid }, quiet }`. `categories` lists every vendor category in label order, with zeros for one no vendor holds. A vendor counts once in each category it holds, so the categories can add up to more than `all`. `no_category` counts vendors that hold only the vendor role. An unknown `owner` returns `400 value_not_in_declared_vocabulary`.

**A vendor's relationships.** `GET /api/engine/vendors/{id}/relationships`, a signed-in read of the new schema that takes no query parameters, answers the card's Relationships diagram. `{id}` is the vendor's party ID; a malformed ID, or a party with no vendor role, answers `404 not_found`. A partner is answered by sign-in name, `joe` or `dell`, and any other user, or none, is null, never guessed. It answers:

- `relationship_partner`: the vendor role's relationship partner, or null.
- `sent_us`: each introduction the vendor made, newest first by date, undated last: `introduction_id`, `client` (`{ party_id, name }`, whom it introduced), `deal` (`{ id, name, open }` by the Deals board's open-deal rule, or null) and `introduced_on`.
