- `we_sent_them`: each introduction the team made to the vendor, in the same order and shape, with `introduced_by`, the partner who made it, and `chosen`, the client deals read's rule: the vendor is on that deal in a vendor role. One with no deal is listed with `deal: null` and is not chosen.
- `worked_with`: the deals the vendor is on in a vendor role, by the track record's rule, that no introduction with the vendor names, open first, then by name: `{ id, name, open, client }`, the client `{ party_id, name }` or null.
- `knows`: the parties in the vendor's current knows links, either way round; `can_introduce`: the parties its current can-introduce links say it can introduce the team to. Both `{ party_id, name }` by name. A link is current while it has no end date.
- `counts`: `{ sent_us, we_sent_them, chosen, knows }`, the lengths of those lists and the chosen introductions.

**The list read.** `GET /api/engine/vendors` changes in these ways:

- `no_category=true` narrows the list to the vendors the summary counts under `no_category`. It combines with the other filters, is echoed in `query` only when asked for, and is refused with `category` and on the legacy list.
- With `category`, `no_category=true` or `markets=true`, it answers every matching vendor in one page. Other lists keep pages of 25.
- Each row answers `categories`, every category the vendor holds as `{ slug, label }` in label order, beside today's single category until the app stops reading it.
- `quiet=true` narrows the list to the vendors the summary counts as quiet, by the same rule at the default 90-day cadence. It combines with the other filters, is echoed in `query` only when asked for, and is refused on the legacy list.
- Each row answers `quiet`, true or false, and `quiet_days`, the whole Central days since its last contact while quiet, otherwise null.
- `sort=relationship` orders by relationship order, then by name, and is the default. The other sorts stay. The legacy list keeps `name` as its default.

**Markets a vendor covers.** With `markets=true`, the next list answers all matching vendors in one page and echoes the option in `query`. Each row has `markets`, a name-ordered list of `{ id, name }`, and `territory`, the comma-separated names of its current territories or null. Both fields are present with or without a `contract` parameter. The legacy list refuses the option. A market piece names the market directly. A state piece covers any market with a county in that state. Each market appears once per vendor.

Each summary count also answers `markets` as `[{ id, name, count }]` in name order, with one entry per covered market and one count per vendor in it. `elsewhere` counts vendors with a current territory and no covered markets, including typed territories without pieces. `no_territory` counts vendors without a current territory. The reads include memberships with no end date or one on or after today in Central time. Matching typed territories to markets requires the owner's approval of the reviewed file.

## Doc conversations and turns

The [Doc screen](screens/doc.md) calls `/api/engine/doc/*` on the app's origin through the current [partner session door](sign-in.md#serve-pages-and-partner-calls-through-the-app). Each call from the app resolves the signed-in partner. The app's POSTs require the partner write proofs. Doc responses use `Cache-Control: no-store`. Only the app route serves partner chat and confirmation calls. The engine owns the tables in `next`, and Doc starts with empty history. Apply [the Doc storage migration](../schema/add-doc-conversations.sql) to an existing `next` before enabling these routes.

### Private storage

Apply [the claim-state migration](../schema/add-doc-turn-claim.sql) after the conversation storage migration on an existing database. It preserves conversation history and starts existing working claims with zero reads used.

All columns below are required unless marked nullable. The engine assigns IDs, the owner, positions, timestamps, and answer fields. It reads and writes chat tables through the stamped writer transaction, with the owner on every lookup. Revoke the general reader grant on both tables. Row security limits reads and writes to the transaction's stamped user, with no job access. Keep chat text out of general change history and request logs.

| Table              | Columns and rules                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `doc_conversation` | `id` UUID primary key, `owner_user_id` UUID, `is_account_partner` generated true, `created_at` timestamp with time zone. The owner and flag reference `app_user (id, is_account_partner)`. Unique `(id, owner_user_id)` for the turn reference. Index `(owner_user_id, created_at desc, id desc)`.                                                                                                                                      |
| `doc_turn`         | `id` UUID primary key, `conversation_id` and `owner_user_id` UUIDs referencing the matching pair on `doc_conversation`, `position` positive integer, `created_at` timestamp with time zone, `idempotency_key` UUID, `request_hash` text, `question` nonblank text of at most 4,000 characters, and `context` nullable JSON object. Unique `(conversation_id, position)`, `(owner_user_id, idempotency_key)`, and `(id, owner_user_id)`. |
| Turn result        | On `doc_turn`, `state` is `queued`, `working`, `failed`, or `answered`. `claim_id` UUID, `claim_until` timestamp with time zone, and `claim_state` JSON object are present only while `working`. The claim state holds the read count and current sources. `answer` is a JSON object present only when `answered`. A partial unique index on `conversation_id` allows one `queued` or `working` turn per chat.                          |
| Confirmed save     | On `doc_turn`, `confirmation_key` is a nullable unique UUID with an initially deferred foreign key to `save_receipt.idempotency_key`. It requires an answered turn with a proposal. The receipt holds the saved record link and result.                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

A turn holds one partner question and at most one Doc answer, so the caller never supplies a role. Positions start at 1. The first ask reserves the key and creates the conversation and turn in one transaction. Later asks lock the conversation to assign the next position. Serialize the owner and key before lookup and reservation, using the writer's transaction lock convention. The stored hash covers the normalized request, including whether the conversation ID was omitted. The turn is the ask receipt. Same-key replay returns the same IDs and the current state. A different request with that key returns `409 key_reuse`.

The engine saves a new turn as `queued`. The engine commits the claim and changes the turn to `working` before the Mac or backup starts the model. Keep the 45-second model and read deadline inside the 60-second claim. Repeating the same key returns the saved turn. A different key for a chat with a queued or working turn returns `409 turn_in_progress`. An unclaimed turn keeps its place in the queue. An expired claim appears queued on the next app read and keeps the turn's place. Only the current, unexpired claim can finish a turn. Retrying the latest failed question puts the same turn back in the queue.

### Partner calls

All paths below begin `/api/engine/doc`. UUIDs identify conversations, turns, and records. `ref` is `{ kind, id }` with `kind` of `deal`, `lead`, `client`, or `email`. Deal and email use deal and email activity IDs. Lead and client use canonical party IDs. A `source` is `{ ref, label, href }` using the engine's label and same-origin record or review link. The browser may give an optional `ref` as a context hint. The engine checks access and resolves the record again for each read or save.

| Request                                                 | Answer                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `GET /conversations`, optional `before` conversation ID | `200 { conversations: [{ id, title, created_at, last_message_at, state }], next_before }`. Up to 30, newest message first, in descending `(last_message_at, id)` order. `title` is the first 80 characters of the first question. `next_before` is the last listed ID if older chats remain, otherwise null. |
| `GET /conversations/{id}`                               | `200 { conversation, turns }`. `conversation` has the same fields as the list entry. `turns` are in `position` order.                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `GET /records?query=`                                   | `200 { records: [{ ref, label, href, city }] }`. Search deals, leads and clients through the same new-database read Doc uses. The query is nonblank and at most 200 characters. Returns up to 10 matches.                                                                                                    |
| `POST /turns`                                           | Send `{ idempotency_key, question, conversation_id?, context? }`. Omit `conversation_id` to start a chat. `200 { conversation_id, turn }` for an answered turn and `202` while queued or working. The conversation read shows a saved failure. Retry sends the original fields and key.                      |
| `POST /turns/{id}/confirmation`                         | Send `{ idempotency_key, edits }` with a separate UUID for this save. The engine uses the stored proposal's kind and target. `200 { result }` gives the receipt. The reply-draft result also carries `existing`, true for an unchanged draft.                                                                |

To start a draft chat, send `context: { kind: "reply_draft", id, version }` with the exact draft ID and the version shown in review. The engine verifies pending status and joins the draft's owner and email to their mailbox row, then stores the context with `activity_id` and `mailbox_user_id`. An unknown or foreign draft returns 404. A follow-up can omit context to keep the same draft and take its current pending version. Send a different draft in a new chat.

Before requeuing a failed draft turn, the engine checks its stored draft, source email, mailbox, and version. As for a new draft ask, a draft that has left pending returns `409 draft_changed { version }`. A pending draft with a different version returns `409 version_conflict { version }`. An unavailable binding returns `404 not_found`. An answered turn still replays its saved answer.

Every returned `turn` has `id`, `position`, `created_at`, `idempotency_key`, `question`, nullable `context`, `state`, nullable `answer`, and nullable `confirmation` with the saved result. To retry the first turn after reload, omit `conversation_id` again. While queued, the turn carries `waiting_reason` with the existing display text and `waiting_reason_code` with the same machine code as the link's `readiness_code`. Both fields are null outside the queue. Each turn also carries the current runner `backend` when known. This field describes the configured runner, while `answered_by` describes where an answer came from. The app rereads the conversation to show progress and the saved result. The app opens new chat links as `/doc-chats?chat=<id>` so legacy links can show their own message. An older failed turn stays visible and retry applies to the latest question.

To refresh marks for chats already on screen, send `GET /conversations?ids=` with up to 30 comma-separated conversation IDs. The response uses the same list shape, with only the signed-in partner's matching conversations and `next_before: null`. Use `ids` without `before`.

A chat's `last_message_at` is the `created_at` of its latest turn. `state` is `working` while that turn is queued or working. It is `waiting_ok` if the latest turn asks for clarification or any answer's proposal still needs confirmation. Otherwise it is null. `working` wins when both hold. The engine derives both from the turns on each read. A `before` cursor uses the chat's current `last_message_at`, so a chat that gets a new message while the partner pages moves to the top.

Each returned turn also has `draft_review`. It is null for a turn without draft context. For a draft turn it contains `{ draft_id, version, current }`, where `draft_id` and `version` come from the saved turn context. The engine computes `current` when it reads the turn. It is `pending_same` if the draft is still pending at that version, `changed` if it is pending at a newer version, or `gone` if it has left pending or is unavailable to the partner. The app uses `changed` or `gone` to ask the partner to open a fresh review for a failed draft turn.

`answer` is `{ kind, text, sources, proposal }`. `kind` is `answer`, `clarification`, or `refusal`. `text` is plain text. `sources` lists the records used, or accessible choices for a clarification. Deal, lead and client choices carry a nullable `city`. Lead and client choices also carry `deal_count`, using the same links as Global Search, including a person's current company. A refusal has empty sources. `proposal` is null or one of the following on an `answer`.

| `kind`           | Stored proposal                                                                                                                | Allowed `edits`       |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------- |
| `note`           | `{ kind, target: source, text }` for a deal, lead, or client                                                                   | `{ text }`            |
| `next_step`      | `{ kind, target: source, what, due_on }` for a deal, lead, or client. `due_on` is `YYYY-MM-DD` or null.                        | `{ what, due_on }`    |
| `reply_draft`    | `{ kind, target: source, subject, body }` for an email                                                                         | `{ subject, body }`   |
| `reply_revision` | `{ kind, target, subject?, body? }`. The target is a source with `ref: { kind: "reply_draft", id }` and the draft's `version`. | `{ subject?, body? }` |

`result` is `{ kind, id, href }` for a note or step, with `id` the saved activity or step ID and `href` the record link. For a reply draft it is `{ kind, id, href, existing }`, with `id` the draft ID and `href` the existing review for the exact draft (`/inbox/card/<encoded reply_draft:id>`). The link opens the [Home card](#home-inbox-reads-for-the-app) or shows the draft's recorded disposition and reason if it has finished. A revision returns `{ kind: "reply_revision", id, href, version }` with the new version and a link to the same review. For a draft no longer listed on Home, the review reads `GET /api/engine/reply-drafts/{id}` directly and shows its current status with the existing action rules.

Session failures return the existing partner responses, including `401 { error: "unauthorized", state: "sign_in_required" }` and `403 { error: "forbidden" }` for missing write proofs. Route refusals use `{ error, correlation_id, hint, ...details }` and the shared validation codes. Use `404 not_found` for an unknown ID or one outside the partner's access. A stale retry returns `409 turn_superseded`. A turn without a proposal returns `409 no_proposal`. An already confirmed proposal returns `409 already_confirmed` on a different save key. If a note or step target is no longer eligible, return `409 target_changed`. For a lead, confirmation checks the same eligibility rule as Doc search, including whether the lead has been linked to a client. An existing open step for the confirming partner returns `409 open_step_exists { next_step_id, href }`. The ID is null if the step ceased to conflict before the lookup. The engine retries a confirmation transaction once after a deadlock or serialization failure. If a concurrent manual party merge fails with `40P01`, rerun the merge to move the saved step to the survivor. On `503 database_unavailable`, retry with the same request and key. An unsupported action, ambiguous name, or unavailable source can return a normal reply with no proposal.

### Model reads and links

After committing the claim, the engine returns the question and up to 12 prior answered turns in the response to the owner's Doc link poll. Each partner has a revocable Doc link credential issued by the engine at install. The engine uses that credential to identify the partner on the poll and on its MCP Streamable HTTP endpoint. For chat, Doc link allows the six tools below from that server. Claude Code uses `--mcp-config`. Codex uses `mcp_servers.doc.enabled_tools`. The Mac sends MCP JSON-RPC requests to `POST /api/engine/doc/mcp` with its Bearer link credential, `Doc-Claim-Id: <uuid>`, and no cookies or browser Origin. The engine checks the credential and the partner's working claim on every request. It returns JSON over Streamable HTTP without issuing an MCP session ID. An invalid credential returns `401`. A missing, foreign, or expired claim returns `404`. Tool calls end 15 seconds before the claim expires.

- `find` takes `{ query, kind }`, with `kind` set to `deal`, `lead`, `client`, or `all`. Search by name or ID, including the copied lead ID from an app link. A follow-up finds a record from history or turn context again by its ID. Matches carry a source key and exact app link. If names collide, the engine returns a clarification with the accessible choices.
- `read` takes `{ source }` and returns the facts for a key found in this claim. In a draft chat, `reply_draft:<id>` reads the bound pending version and its authorized source email. The result includes the draft text and up to 2,000 characters of the email body. Ordinary records follow the signed-in app's sharing rules. Link credentials, claims and history are private to their partner.
- `mail_priorities` takes `{}` and returns up to 10 ranked Home mail priorities with source keys for their exact review emails. `read_mail` takes `{ activity_id }` from a priority or an exact email context. It returns the reviewed email's facts and source key, or null when its mailbox membership or review is missing. The engine uses the link credential's partner for both calls.
- `propose_save` takes `{ kind: "note", source, text }` or `{ kind: "next_step", source, what, due_on }`. For an authorized email source, it also takes `{ kind: "reply_draft", source, subject, body }`. For a read draft source, it takes `{ kind: "reply_revision", source, subject?, body? }` with at least one revised field. It returns an editable proposal for an authorized target and writes no business record. Confirmation stays on the signed-in app route.
- `validate_answer` takes `{ kind, text, sources, proposal? }`. The engine resolves every citation to an authorized source from the current claim. An answer requires a citation if the claim returned a record, even if access is later revoked. A save proposal also requires a source. Validation alone leaves the turn and business records unchanged.

The tool route supports deals, leads, clients, and private mail. The signed-in route confirms note, next-step, new reply draft, and draft revision proposals.

Engine code handles unattended work that can be decided from saved facts. The engine queues summaries, action titles, and filing judgments for the partner whose mail or record it is. For a shared record, use the deal or client owner, or the partner who saved the record if there is no such owner. The Mac handles [mail judgment jobs](#mail-judgment-jobs), and [Doc backup](#doc-backup) can take eligible work. Unresolved work appears on Home for a partner to handle with Doc's help.

Each ask gets at most four read calls total and sends no saved tool output from earlier turns. The engine validates tool names, shapes, and IDs and projects just the facts needed for the reply. It builds source links only from authorized read results for the current ask and accepts a proposal only for one of those sources. Treat email text as evidence. A URL or identity supplied by the model carries no authority.

| Read                            | Source and allowed facts                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Find                            | The `next` implementation of `/api/engine/find`, limited to 10 deal, lead, and client matches with IDs, display names, and the app record links. Use the same authorized IDs for detail reads.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Deal, lead, or client           | The `next` detail readers behind `/api/engine/deals/{id}`, `/api/engine/leads?lead_id=…`, and `/api/engine/clients/{id}`. Project the name, known owner, phase or stage, next steps, and up to five recent non-mail activity summaries when those facts exist on the record. Resolve a lead's or client's canonical party ID to the reader's lookup key server-side.                                                                                                               |
| Mail priorities or exact review | Use the Home priorities ranking and existing Home suggestion or reply-draft review. Require `(activity_id, user_id)` in `next.email_mailbox` for the partner's exact source email. Priorities return the message's sender display, subject, date, review link, suggested action and due date. The exact read returns the message's sender display, subject, date, review link and up to 2,000 characters of its body. Attachments and recipient lists stay out of the model input. |

Doc uses only `next` readers. A failed read fails the ask rather than passing an empty result to the model. With no match, ask for a record the partner can name or open. With several plausible matches, ask which one. A reply proposal needs one exact, authorized email source. The synthetic staging rehearsal checks a first question and follow-up across all four kinds, exact links, ambiguous names, other-mailbox refusal, and mail text that tries to direct a save. Proceed only if the answers are grounded, the links open, and each ask finishes within its deadline.

### Connect a Mac to Doc

A signed-in partner issues a credential from the app and gives it to their Mac's installer. The named app door gets the owner from the partner session. The POSTs require the usual partner write proofs and take no arguments. Responses use `Cache-Control: no-store`.

| App request                        | Answer                                                                                                                              |
| ---------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `GET /api/engine/doc/link`         | `200 { connected, issued_at, status, readiness_code, backend }`. The issue time is null when the partner has no current credential. |
| `POST /api/engine/doc/link`        | `201 { credential }`. Shows the new credential once, replaces any previous credential, and resets presence.                         |
| `POST /api/engine/doc/link/revoke` | `204`. Revokes the partner's credential. Safe to repeat.                                                                            |

The status is `not_set_up` without a current credential and `mac_off` without a poll in the last 30 seconds. Otherwise `connected` is true and the last report gives `signed_out`, `plan_limited`, or `ready`. A connected Mac with no report shows `ready`. The matching `readiness_code` values are `doc_not_set_up`, `doc_mac_off`, `doc_signed_out`, `doc_plan_limit`, and `doc_ready`. The `backend` is `claude`, `codex`, or null until the runner reports one. Use the code to choose app copy. Queued turns use the same status to explain the wait.

The Mac polls the engine directly over HTTPS about every two seconds using `POST /api/engine/doc/link/claim` with `Authorization: Bearer <credential>` and no cookies. It sends `Doc-Link-Work-Readiness` as a JSON record with `chat` and `mail`. Each value is `ready`, `signed_out`, `plan_limited`, or null when unknown or paused. The engine offers a job only when the Mac reports readiness for its kind. The Mac also sends its backend and the chat value in the legacy `Doc-Link-Readiness` header when known. When an older Mac sends only that header, the engine uses the value for both kinds. The engine keeps the last backend through polls that omit the header and clears it when it issues a new credential. An unrecognized backend returns `400 invalid_backend`. The engine derives the owner from the credential. A poll with neither header records presence when due and keeps the last readiness report. A Codex poll reporting only the backend means its startup check failed. It clears any prior readiness and presence so queued turns show `doc_mac_off` and the backup can take eligible work immediately. An unrecognized value returns `400 invalid_readiness`. An invalid credential or a request with cookies returns `401`. Rotation and revocation take the same owner lock as polls and results. A request that locks the current credential first may finish before a pending rotation or revocation. After the change commits, the old credential returns `401`.

When a poll reports `ready` and the link has no unexpired claim, the link takes the oldest turn from queued turns and turns with expired claims. Creation time and ID determine their order. A claim returns `200 { kind: "turn", claim_id, claim_until, conversation_id, turn, history }`. The turn includes `id`, `position`, `question`, and nullable `context`. History contains up to 12 prior answered questions and answers in conversation order. A poll with no available work returns `204`. Each link runs one claim at a time.

The Mac posts completion to `POST /api/engine/doc/link/result` with the same Bearer credential and a JSON body. It sends `{ claim_id, kind: "answered", answer }`, or `{ claim_id, kind }` with `kind` of `failed`, `signed_out`, or `plan_limited`. A successful result returns `204`. A sign-in or plan blocker releases the claim to the queue and records chat readiness. A failed turn stays visible for partner retry. The engine validates an answer against that claim's saved sources before storing it. A missing, foreign, expired, or already completed claim returns `404`. A malformed result returns `400` and an invalid answer returns `422`, leaving the current claim available for a corrected result until it expires.

#### Mail judgment jobs

`doc_judgment_job` keeps an email activity ID, the mailbox partner, and a stable idempotency key unique to that partner. A filing job requires at least one `doc_judgment_destination` that references an existing deal or vendor role. A vendor merge moves the destination to the surviving vendor, including for completed jobs. Summary and split jobs have no destinations. Row security limits access to the stamped owner, and a job leaves when its mailbox membership is removed. Summary intake uses the email activity ID as the key. Filing and split use separate keys derived from the same ID. Each enqueue function checks the stamped mail capture job and its mailbox partner. The engine reads the email subject and body at claim time and keeps a hash of that input for the lease.

A ready Mac sends `Doc-Link-Work: judgment` to claim these jobs. Turns and jobs share one 60-second claim slot per partner. One database statement checks for an active lease and chooses the next item, with queued or expired turns first. Within each queue, creation time and ID break ties. The link returns a mail job's `kind`, `claim_id`, `claim_until`, `activity_id`, `subject`, and `body`. A Mac that also lists `email_split` (`Doc-Link-Work: judgment, email_split`) claims split jobs too. Others and the backup leave them queued, so splits run only on the partner's own plan. An `email_filing` claim also includes `destinations`, each with a `kind` of `deal` or `vendor` and an `id`. Expiry makes work claimable with a new claim ID. If the stored input cannot be claimed, the engine marks the job failed and continues in the same transaction.

For a job, the link posts `{ claim_id, kind: "judgment", outcome }` to the same result door. The outcome is `{ kind: "answered", result }` or `{ kind }` with `failed`, `signed_out`, or `plan_limited`. The summary result has `summary` (up to 300 characters), nullable `action_title` (up to 90 characters), and `quote`. Filing returns `{ facts: [{ destination, text, quote }] }`. A split returns `{ authored: ["..."] }`, the sender's paragraphs as in [Split by judgment](#split-by-judgment). Each destination must be on the claim. Each summary or filing quote must occur in the claimed email's body or subject. A result can name one deal and any verified vendors. If the email gives conflicting evidence or has no grounded facts, return an empty list for partner review.

Success returns `204`. A blocker requeues the job and records mail readiness. Failure ends the job. An invalid result returns `422` and allows correction within the lease. An answer to a changed source, a foreign or expired claim, or a replay of a completed claim returns `404`. An accepted summary uses the existing stamped summary save in the claim's transaction. An accepted filing saves the source email links and stamped activity notes in the claim's transaction. An accepted split stores the built pair as the job's result and fills `email.authored` and `email.history`, stamped `mail_capture` for the job's partner, only while both are null. The engine verifies vendor affiliations against the email's Central sent date both when it queues the job and when it saves facts. The save receipt connects each note to the source email, quote, destination, and judgment job. Mailboxes share the filing decision for one Message-ID. A later job creates no facts or review after a partner files or dismisses that email, or a job files its facts.

A failed, invalid, empty, or newly unverified result leaves a filing review when there is a destination to review. For the engine's fallback, Home and the proposal detail return `filing_choices` with the applicable deal and vendor options. The decision door accepts `deal_id` or `vendor_id` for those choices and records the partner's decision. Filing or dismissing supersedes all other pending filing proposals for the exact email in the same transaction. Home removes them and the decision door returns `409 already_decided` for those IDs. A partner can accept a new correction proposed by the legacy router before or after the filing migration. Reply drafts and independent suggestions remain reviewable.

For a pending review that offers **Choose deal**, `GET /api/engine/proposals/:id?deal_id=<uuid>` previews the selected open deal. It returns `{ reason_required, hint }`. The hint explains why a reason is required, or is null when a reason is optional. The read has the same mailbox and visibility checks as proposal detail and returns `404 not_found` when the review or deal cannot be used. Without `deal_id`, the route returns the original proposal detail. The preview and the decision save use the same database rule. Apply [add-deal-filing-reason.sql](../schema/add-deal-filing-reason.sql) before releasing this read against an existing database.

The Mac runner opts in when ready and handles summary, filing, split, and introduction jobs on either backend. Older runners continue to claim turns only. Judgment sessions use fixed prompts and pass the email as untrusted JSON on stdin. They have no tools, MCP servers, or Doc link credential. The runner submits the result for engine validation.

With lead-source sender storage and lead filing supersession in place, apply [the judgment queue migration](../schema/add-doc-judgment-job.sql), then [summary enqueue](../schema/add-queue-email-summary.sql), [filing enqueue](../schema/add-queue-email-filing.sql), [authored email storage](../schema/add-email-authored.sql), and [split jobs](../schema/add-email-split-job.sql). The split migration needs the owner's yes on its sha before production. Capture queues splits once it is installed. After the Outlook recovery apply, queue the rest once as the schema owner: `psql -X -f schema/queue-email-splits.sql` counts unsplit emails as `queued`, `already_queued` and `not_queued` (lead notices and email in no partner's mailbox) and rolls back, and `-v apply=on` commits. After applying [the introduction queue migration](../schema/add-introduction-job.sql), run `DATABASE_URL=... node scripts/queue-introductions.ts --dry-run` for read-only counts using an account that can read all introduction jobs (including through row-level security), then run as the schema owner without `--dry-run` to apply after the owner approves the script and its sha. The script skips lead mail and existing jobs. Set `EMAIL_FILING_JUDGMENT` to `true` after the filing enqueue function is installed to enqueue filing jobs for new deliveries. Production has the flag on since 2026-11-21.

Presence is true for 30 seconds after the last recorded contact. The first successful poll records it, and subsequent polls refresh it once the stored time is at least 15 seconds old. A result reporting a blocker also records contact. Readiness changes take effect immediately and use the same row update. An unchanged poll inside 15 seconds needs no row update. The app gives an offline message for an issued credential without recent presence. With recent presence, it shows a sign-in, plan-limit, or free-slot message. If there is no credential, it shows the setup message.

Apply [the Doc link migration](../schema/add-doc-link.sql) and then [the link readiness migration](../schema/add-doc-link-readiness.sql) to an existing database before deploying queued Doc turns. Apply [the link backend migration](../schema/add-doc-link-backend.sql) after readiness. Apply [the readiness-by-kind migration](../schema/add-doc-link-readiness-by-kind.sql) before deploying the separate chat and mail readiness. A database missing the link table returns `503 { error: "doc_link_unavailable" }` for authenticated link management and well-formed Mac polls.

`next.doc_link` keeps one row per account partner with an owner ID, a SHA-256 credential hash, the issue time, the last recorded contact time, a nullable readiness record keyed by kind, and nullable backend. The chat status retains the existing app response shape. The engine can read the legacy scalar readiness for rows with no record. Issuing a replacement clears contact, readiness, and backend. The engine uses the hash to find a candidate owner, then compares fixed-length digests in constant time under the row lock. The partner's stamped writer manages the row. General readers and jobs have no access.

#### Doc link on macOS

**Before the visit.** Put the engine checkout where it will stay on the Mac. Install [Node.js 26](https://nodejs.org/en/download), make it available in Terminal, and run `npm ci` in the checkout. Prepare the command with the HTTPS engine origin for the app that issues the credential. Rehearse with the staging origin and a sourced answer about a test record. The coordinator confirms acceptance of Anthropic's Commercial Terms for Claude Code setup and sets up the first Mac before the second.

**Choose a CLI.** For first-time Codex setup or to switch to Codex, follow **Switch a Mac to Codex** below. To use Claude Code, open Terminal in the partner's macOS login and run the following command from the prepared checkout with `DOC_ENGINE_ORIGIN` set to the supplied HTTPS engine origin.

```sh
bash scripts/install-doc-link.sh "$DOC_ENGINE_ORIGIN"
```

1. Setup uses [Anthropic's official installer](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/setup) if Claude Code is missing. If setup asks you to sign in, open `claude` (try `~/.local/bin/claude` if needed). Sign in with your own Claude subscription through Anthropic, keep usage credits off in your Claude account, and run the same command again.
2. If setup prompts for a Doc link credential, issue one in the app. Paste it at the hidden Terminal prompt and press Return. A prepared command can also read one line from standard input. Keep the credential out of command arguments and shell history. Setup verifies the credential with the engine before saving it and starting the job. If the new code is refused, make another one in the app and run setup again.
3. When Terminal says **Doc link is ready.**, open Ask Doc and ask about a deal you can see. Open the source link in the answer to confirm it goes to that deal.

Rerun setup to refresh the list of signed-in CLIs or change the default. Setup verifies a saved credential against its original engine and keeps it if the engine accepts it. If you link to a different engine, have no saved credential, or the engine refuses it, setup prompts for a new one. Setup writes `~/Library/Application Support/DoctorCRE Doc/link.json` with mode `0600`. It records the absolute paths of the signed-in CLIs it finds in `backends` and sets `default_backend` from the setup command. The runner uses that default and can still read earlier single-backend configs. It checks file ownership and permissions before reading the credential, and stops polling on `401`. Mail judgment receives no link credential.

The [LaunchAgent template](../scripts/launchd/com.doctorcre.doc-link.plist) starts the link under the partner's macOS login. The installer supplies absolute paths to Node 26, [the runner](../scripts/doc-link.ts), the configuration, and `link.log` in the same folder as the configuration. If a reload fails, setup restores the previous files and restarts the old job.

**Claude Code.** The partner signs in through Claude Code and keeps plan usage credits off. Setup and the link use `claude auth status` to confirm a personal Pro, Max, or Ultra subscription. Team, Enterprise, and unrecognized plans stop setup or pause the link. The link checks the sign-in with the same settings it uses for chat. It also checks mail judgment with the settings isolation used for that work. The link checks every 30 seconds and before starting a turn or job. It reports an expired sign-in from a failed session. On a reported plan limit, the work returns to the queue and the Mac waits until the reported reset before claiming again. If Claude Code gives no reset time, the link checks again after five minutes.

Before offering and starting Claude Code mail judgment, the runner checks these locations for settings that can add tools or instructions (decisions 217 and 219).

- Any entry in `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode/` other than `managed-settings.json`, including MCP configuration, instructions, agents, skills and subdirectories. An empty or missing directory is allowed. [Managed settings locations](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/managed-settings#where-each-mechanism-stores-the-policy)
- A `managed-settings.json` there that is not a regular file holding a JSON object whose only key is `permissions`, whose only key is `deny`, a list of rule strings. A deny rule can only take a tool away. A symbolic link, another key, a rule that is not a string, or invalid JSON pauses mail judgment.
- The `com.anthropic.claudecode` configuration profile or managed preference domain under `/Library/Managed Preferences/`. [macOS managed preferences](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/managed-settings#where-each-mechanism-stores-the-policy)
- A `policyHelper` configured in managed settings or the profile. [Settings reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference#policyhelper)
- The server policy cache at `~/.claude/remote-settings.json`. [Server-managed settings](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/server-managed-settings)

If the runner finds a disallowed source or cannot verify its absence, it offers to run chat only and leaves mail work on the queue. Claude Code mail jobs disable settings sources, hooks, browser integration, connectors, and built-in tools. They use an empty MCP configuration.

Doc chat starts in the partner's home directory and uses that CLI's configured tools and permissions. Claude Code runs `claude -p` and Codex runs `codex exec --json`. The runner adds the claim-scoped Doc MCP connection and reads the final JSON result within the 45-second window. The engine validates the answer and requires confirmation for saves to business records. Mail judgment receives no Doc link credential, and the runner's `allowMailTools` setting is off.

**Switch a Mac to Codex.** Open Terminal in your usual macOS login. Use the prepared checkout and the HTTPS engine origin supplied with your setup command.

If this Mac already runs Doc, update the checkout its LaunchAgent runs before running setup.

```sh
doc_agent="$HOME/Library/LaunchAgents/com.doctorcre.doc-link.plist"
doc_runner=$(/usr/libexec/PlistBuddy -c 'Print :ProgramArguments:1' "$doc_agent")
doc_checkout=$(git -C "$(dirname "$doc_runner")" rev-parse --show-toplevel)
git -C "$doc_checkout" pull --ff-only origin main
cd "$doc_checkout" && npm ci
```

1. Use the absolute path to a Codex CLI 0.172.0 binary as `DOC_CODEX_BIN`. If you need the binary, get it from the [Codex CLI 0.172.0 release](https://github.com/openai/codex/releases/tag/rust-v0.172.0).
2. If Codex asks you to sign in, use `"$DOC_CODEX_BIN" login` with your personal ChatGPT plan. Keep plan usage credits off. Setup uses the `CODEX_HOME` you set in Terminal, a previously saved Codex home, or `~/.codex` in that order.
3. Run setup. If it prompts for a credential, issue one in the app and paste it at the hidden prompt. Issuing a new credential revokes the previous one.

   ```sh
   bash scripts/install-doc-link.sh --codex "$DOC_CODEX_BIN" "$DOC_ENGINE_ORIGIN"
   ```

4. When Terminal says **Doc link is ready.**, ask Doc about a visible test deal and open a source from its answer.

**Switch back to Claude Code.** Run the one-argument setup command above. Ask Doc for a sourced answer.

Codex checks `login status` and reads the personal plan type through app-server's `account/read`. Setup saves the Codex home in the private configuration and the LaunchAgent so reruns and the background job use the same sign-in and configuration. Codex mail judgment uses the verified model catalog, a read-only sandbox, and an ephemeral session. It skips user config and rules, sets the project instruction budget to zero, and disables shell, patch, image, web, agent, app, plugin, and hook capabilities. It connects to no MCP server. The runner leaves Codex mail jobs queued if the catalog is missing or changed, or if it finds managed Codex settings or Codex home instructions. When Codex supplies a plan reset time, the runner waits until then. Otherwise it checks again after five minutes.

The link retries a dropped claim or completion request after two seconds. While the model runs, a separate poll refreshes presence without asking for work. A completion retry reuses the original claim and answer until the claim expires. The link ignores a `404` completion and polls for new work. The engine can then give an expired turn a fresh claim.

### Doc backup

- A Cloudflare Cron Trigger wakes the engine Worker every minute, even when the app is closed.
- The dispatcher checks each partner for an eligible chat turn before it checks any partner for a mail judgment. Each tick runs up to one item per partner. Work is eligible when the Mac is unready for its kind or it has been queued for at least two minutes. For chat turns, the timer starts again on requeue. For mail judgments, it uses the job's creation time.
- The Mac and backup share the same claim path and one 60-second lease slot per partner. A chat claim uses the same `claim_state` for reads and sources, and a mail job uses the same source hash. The engine locks the selected row before checking its state, lease, grace period, and the Mac's current readiness. It skips the item if its state or lease changed or if it is no longer eligible. Only the current, unexpired claim can save the result. The backup follows the same deadline, read tools, citation and proposal rules, answer validation, and stamped saves.
- The backup uses the prepaid Claude Messages API with `claude-sonnet-5-5`. Summary and filing jobs use the same prompts, evidence validation, and stamped saves as the Mac. The API receives the email as data and gets an empty tool list.
- Exhausted credits stop sends for the tick and return the work to the queue when completion commits, preserving the Mac's readiness. Later ticks send at most one request while credits remain exhausted. Other failures use the Mac's failed state, with the same filing review for invalid or failed judgments. A partner can retry a failed chat turn. The dispatcher's writer transactions wait up to one second per database lock request. If completion fails to commit, the work can be reclaimed after its lease expires. The tick continues to the next partner unless credits ran out.
- [The migration](../schema/add-doc-answered-by.sql) adds `doc_turn.answered_by` for the app's **Answered by backup** marker and `requeued_at` for the grace period. Existing answers default to `'mac'`. The migration requires the owner's approval before production.
- `DOC_BACKUP` defaults to `false` in every environment. Enable it after applying the migration and installing the owner's prepaid API key as the `DOC_BACKUP_API_KEY` secret. `DOC_BACKUP_MODEL` names the model. See decisions 195, 196, and 204 in [Decisions](decisions.md).

### Confirmed saves and receipts

Apply the [Doc confirmation grant](../schema/add-doc-confirmation-grant.sql) before deploying a confirmation save to an existing database.

The confirmation locks the partner's turn and uses the existing `runNextSave` receipt rules. It rechecks access and exact target, then writes the business record, receipt, and turn's `confirmation_key` in one transaction. The same key and edits return the original result, even if business state later changed. Same key and different edits return `409 key_reuse`. The model has no access to this route. The browser never sends job headers or credentials. Omit an edit field to keep the proposed value. Use `due_on: null` to clear a proposed date.

- **Note.** Use the partner activity save in [door 19](#19-email-and-activity-in), with `kind: "note"`, the confirmed text as summary, and one `about` entry for the deal or party. Stamp the confirming partner.
- **Next step.** Use the ordinary step save in [door 18](#18-next-steps), with the confirmed text and due date for the deal or party. The confirming partner owns it. A conflicting open step stays in place and the partner gets its ID and the record link. Either partner can confirm their own proposal for a shared record.
- **Reply draft.** Use the [pending draft rules](#22-reply-drafts-and-their-delivery) and the partner's mailbox membership in `next.email_mailbox`. Both the intake store and Doc take the existing mailbox and Outlook conversation lock before any draft check or insert. If a draft exists for this email, return it unchanged. Otherwise, if a pending draft exists for this mailbox and Outlook conversation, return it unchanged. If neither exists, insert one pending draft at revision 1 with the confirmed subject and body. The first 2,000 characters of the source email's body supply the evidence, with the sender as a fallback for blank text. The existing review supplies recipients. A missing email or mailbox membership returns `404 not_found`. Retain `unique (email_activity_id, owner_user_id)`. Add nullable `confirmed_doc_turn_id` to `reply_draft`, unique and paired with `owner_user_id` in a foreign key to `doc_turn (id, owner_user_id)`. Exactly one origin is required, either `proposed_by_job` or `confirmed_doc_turn_id`. The Doc insert requires the signed-in owner and the matching turn's reply proposal for that email. The confirm route never approves a draft or queues delivery.
- **Reply revision.** **Save revision** locks the exact draft and uses its proposal's version in the ordinary [pending draft edit](#open-save-or-decline-a-reply-draft-from-home). It changes only the subject and body. A field omitted from both the edits and proposal keeps its saved text. A newer version returns `409 version_conflict`. A draft at that version that has left pending returns `409 draft_changed`. A retry with the same key and edits returns the original receipt. Submission remains a separate save.

### Planned Doc changes

Decisions 218 to 220 add these. None is built yet. The behavior above holds until each unit lands and moves its part into the sections above.

#### Model choice

`next.doc_model_choice` keeps at most one row per account partner: `owner_user_id`, `first_provider`, nullable `first_model` and `first_effort`, nullable `second_provider`, `second_model` and `second_effort`, and `updated_at`. A provider is `claude`, `codex`, `grok` or `gemini`. A model is nonblank text of at most 100 characters and never `default` in any case. A null model runs the tool's own default. An effort is `minimal`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh` or `max`, or null. The second provider differs from the first, and its model and effort are null without it. No row means `claude` with its defaults. The partner's stamped writer manages the row, with the same row security as `next.doc_link`. Replacing or revoking the link credential keeps the choice.

`next.doc_link_provider` keeps one row per partner and provider the link reports: `readiness` of `ready`, `signed_out` or `plan_limited`, nullable `resets_at`, present only with `plan_limited`, `checked_at`, and `models`, the JSON list the tool reported, each entry `{ id, label, efforts }`. Issuing a replacement credential removes the partner's rows.

The claim request may carry a JSON body `{ providers: [{ provider, readiness, resets_at, models }] }`. The engine replaces the partner's rows with it. A poll with only `Doc-Link-Readiness` reports `claude`, so older runners keep working. The link counts as `ready` when its first or second choice is ready. Otherwise it is `plan_limited` with the earliest reset among the limited choices, or else `signed_out`. Backup eligibility in decision 204 uses that kind's readiness. A claim answer adds `choice: { first, second }`, each `{ provider, model, effort }` or null for the second.

| App request                        | Answer                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ---------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `GET /api/engine/doc/link`         | Adds `resets_at` to the link status, `providers: [{ provider, readiness, resets_at, checked_at, models }]` and the saved `choice`.                                                                                                                                                           |
| `POST /api/engine/doc/link/choice` | Send `{ idempotency_key, first, second }` with the write proofs. `200 { choice }`. A provider the link has never reported, or a model or effort missing from that provider's last reported list, returns `422 invalid_field_value { fields }`. A `second` equal to `first` returns the same. |

The runner skips a saved model or effort the tool no longer lists and runs the tool's default. A first choice that reports `plan_limited` or `signed_out` during a turn posts `{ claim_id, kind, provider, resets_at }` to the result door. The engine records that provider's readiness and returns the turn to the queue, so the next claim runs the second choice. An answered result adds `provider`. `doc_turn.answered_by` becomes one of the four providers or `backup`, and the migration changes `'mac'` to `'claude'`. History stays plain questions and answers, so a chat continues on whichever provider answers. A new choice applies from the next claim. Mail judgment jobs use the backend selected in the Mac runner config (decision 218).

Each driver follows decision 219. Doc chat uses the selected CLI's normal tools and permissions plus the claim-scoped Doc MCP server. Mail judgment runs without tools. The Codex driver uses the app-server-backed CLI. The planned Grok and Gemini drivers use the Agent Client Protocol. A plan limit is its own outcome with the tool's reset time, never a generic failure. The runner stops a tool only after the tool says its turn started. Completion, stop, error and process exit each end the claim.

#### Live answer and Stop

The runner posts `POST /api/engine/doc/link/progress` with the Bearer credential about once a second while a turn runs, sending `{ claim_id, text }` with the answer text so far, at most 8,000 characters. The engine keeps it in `claim_state` and never in a business table. The engine sets the current step itself from its own tool route, such as "Looking up" and the record label a `find` or `read` resolved, never from model text. Each returned turn adds nullable `progress: { text, step }` while working. Partial text is unvalidated. The app shows it as plain text, with no links, visuals or proposal, and the finished answer replaces it. A missing, foreign or expired claim returns `404`.

The progress answer is `200 { cancel }`. `POST /api/engine/doc/turns/{id}/cancel` takes `{}` with the write proofs. A queued or waiting turn becomes `cancelled` at once. A working turn records the request in `claim_state`, the next progress answer says `cancel: true`, and the runner stops the tool and posts `{ claim_id, kind: "cancelled" }`. A working turn whose claim expires with a cancel request becomes `cancelled`, not queued. The door answers `200 { turn }`, again for an already cancelled turn. An answered or failed turn returns `409 turn_finished`. The database allows `cancelled`, and a cancelled turn has no claim fields or answer.

#### The next question

`POST /turns` while the chat has a queued or working turn saves the new turn as `waiting`. A second partial unique index allows one `waiting` turn per chat, and another ask returns `409 turn_waiting`. When the turn ahead is answered, fails or is cancelled, the same transaction moves the waiting turn to `queued` with a fresh queue time. `POST /api/engine/doc/turns/{id}/question` takes `{ question }` and changes a waiting turn's text. Any other state returns `409 turn_started`. The conversation read leaves out a turn cancelled while waiting.

#### Suggestions

An `answer` or `clarification` may carry `suggestions`, at most three plain-text follow-up questions of up to 60 characters each, with no links. They arrive in the same answer and cost no extra request. `validate_answer` checks them. A refusal has none.

#### Attachments

Attaching a PDF or photo gets its own design here before any build: storage for the file, a table tying it to its turn, and a `read_attachment` Doc tool that returns the PDF's text or the image, since Doc's own file tools stay off.

#### Visual answers

An `answer` may carry `visuals`, at most three blocks shown after `text` in order. Each has a `title` of up to 80 characters and a `kind`.

| `kind`    | Shape and limits                                                                                                                                                                                |
| --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `table`   | `columns`, 2 to 6 headings, and `rows`, 1 to 20, each one cell per column. A cell is `{ text }` of up to 120 characters, or `{ text, source }` to link a record.                                |
| `chart`   | `chart` of `bar` or `line`, nullable `unit` of `usd`, `sqft`, `percent` or `days`, and `points`, 1 to 24, each `{ label, value, sources }`. A line chart's labels are dates or months in order. |
| `diagram` | `mermaid`, at most 2,000 characters, and `sources`, the records it draws. The first line is `flowchart TD`, `flowchart LR` or `timeline`. Node labels are plain text.                           |

The engine validates visuals with the rest of the answer, in `validate_answer` and again on the result. Every `source` is a key from an authorized read in this claim, resolved to the engine's label and link. A chart point's value is either a number in its one source's read facts or the count of the distinct sources it lists, so the engine keeps each read's numeric facts in `claim_state`. A diagram refuses `click`, `href`, `%%{`, `style`, `classDef`, `<` and any URL. A bad block returns the same `422` as any invalid answer, naming the block, so the model can correct it within the claim. History carries visuals as JSON. The fixed system prompt, on the Mac and on the backup, asks for a visual when it explains better than text and never for a number Doc did not read.

## Doors the old system calls

These are the engine requests old-system programs 26, 27 and 28 will make, fixed before either side is built so both can be built at once. Where the engine side is unbuilt, this is the shape it implements. Old callers were read at `carr-system` `e5966a7`, which already keeps each email's Message-ID (slice 2).

### What every door shares

- **Two entrances.** The app and a partner's own calls use `/api/engine/...` with the partner's session, so the save is stamped with that user. Studio jobs use `/jobs/...`, behind one Cloudflare Access service token for the Studio, like `/intake/*` (decision 106), and name themselves in two headers: `Engine-Job: <job slug>` and, when the job works for a partner, `Engine-For-User: <user slug>`. `runNextSave` turns either into its `Stamp`. A door listed under one entrance only refuses the other with 404.
- **Saves.** Unless a door documents its own retry key, a save is a JSON `POST` with `idempotency_key`, a UUID the caller makes once per intended change and reuses on every retry, for example a UUIDv5 of the source and the change. A retry gets the first answer back with `replayed: true` and changes nothing. The same key with other arguments or another stamp is `409 key_reuse`.
- **Refusals.** A refusal is `{ error, correlation_id, hint, ...details }`. Every door can answer `400 invalid_json`, `400 missing_required { fields }`, `400 invalid_field_value { fields }`, `401 unauthorized`, `403 unknown_job`, `422 actor_not_provisioned`, `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule }` with the constraint's name when a database rule refuses, and `503 database_unavailable`. A `5xx` is retried with the same key; a `4xx` is not, except where a door says so.
- **Ids.** Deals and activities keep their old ids in the copy, so ids a caller already holds still work. Parties, people and users are named by new ids or, for a user, by `app_user.slug`.
- **Timestamps.** Invoice capture, next-step completion, email lead suggestions, reply delivery and calendar agenda refuse impossible dates, year `0000` and offsets outside PostgreSQL's range with `400 invalid_field_value`.

Jobs that stamp these saves: `mail_capture` for intake, the router and the outbox, each working for the mailbox's partner; `lead_stage` for the nightly lead stages; `cadence` and `call_capture` for the nightly cadence and call capture, which the owner adds (see the list at the end); `agent` for the assistant, working for its partner.

### Invoice email capture and list

`POST /jobs/invoice-emails` uses `Engine-Job: mail_capture` and `Engine-For-User: <mailbox partner slug>`.

- The JSON body requires `idempotency_key` (UUID), `evidence_ref` (nonblank source reference), `sender` (email address), `deal_name` (nonblank text), and `occurred_at` (instant with zone). `client_name` and `property_address` each take nonblank text or null, and may be omitted. The save takes `email_date` from the calendar date in `occurred_at`. The input carries no amount or payment.
- The answer is `200 { invoice_id, evidence_ref }`. The shared retry rules apply. With a fresh key and the same reference, the answer still names the first capture. The stored facts and capture stamp stay as first saved. Omitting the mailbox partner returns `422 actor_not_provisioned`.

`GET /jobs/invoice-emails` uses `Engine-Job: lead_stage` and takes no arguments.

- The answer is `200 { invoices: [...] }`, in ascending `occurred_at`, then `invoice_id` order. Each captured row has `invoice_id`, `evidence_ref`, `sender`, `deal_name`, `client_name`, `property_address`, `occurred_at`, `email_date`, and `target`. The instant is in UTC and `email_date` is `YYYY-MM-DD`.
- `target` is `{ deal_id, payment_id, for_user }` when the database finds one deal with prior phase history and one eligible payment under the automatic checks above. `for_user` is the deal owner's slug, which the job uses as `Engine-For-User` on apply. A null `target` leaves the evidence waiting. The apply save checks again under lock.

### 19. Email and activity in

`POST /jobs/activities` and `POST /api/engine/activities`

| Field                   | Type                                                              | Required                                                                    |
| ----------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `idempotency_key`       | UUID                                                              | yes                                                                         |
| `kind`                  | `email`, `call`, `meeting` or `note`                              | yes                                                                         |
| `happened_at`           | instant with zone                                                 | only for email and calendar reviews                                         |
| `about`                 | list of `{ deal_id }`, `{ party_id }` or `{ property_id }`        | yes for a call, meeting or note; for an email, added to the matched parties |
| `summary`               | text                                                              | no; an email shows its subject                                              |
| `from_party_id`         | UUID                                                              | no; an email's comes from its sender                                        |
| `reached`               | boolean or null                                                   | no; calls only                                                              |
| `calendar_review_key`   | 16 hex characters                                                 | no; a meeting confirmed from a calendar review (25)                         |
| `email.message_id`      | the nonblank Message-ID header, without angle brackets, case kept | yes for an email                                                            |
| `email.sender`          | address                                                           | yes for an email                                                            |
| `email.sender_name`     | the display name, as intake reads it                              | no                                                                          |
| `email.recipients`      | list of `{ address, kind: to or cc }`                             | yes for an email, may be empty                                              |
| `email.subject`         | text, may be empty                                                | yes for an email                                                            |
| `email.body`            | full captured plain text, including history when available        | yes for an email                                                            |
| `email.authored`        | text the sender wrote, with paragraphs kept                       | optional pair with `email.history`                                          |
| `email.history`         | text of reply headers, quotes, signatures, footers and banners    | optional pair with `email.authored`                                         |
| `email.conversation_id` | nonblank Outlook conversation id, case kept                       | no                                                                          |

- **Activity time.** When a call, ordinary meeting, or note omits `happened_at`, the engine uses the time of the first save. Retries keep that time.
- **Links.** The engine links an email to every party whose `party_email` matches its sender or a recipient, other than the users, plus `about`. The sender's party becomes `from_party_id`. An email whose sender is a user's address is outgoing; no direction field is sent. The caller no longer matches addresses itself, so its contact list never goes stale.
- **Retry and second mailbox.** `message_id` is unique. A second request for a stored Message-ID, from a retry with a new key or from the other partner's mailbox, adds any new links and answers the stored activity with `existing: true`. The first email's body and conversation id stay. A complete capture can fill a missing `authored` and `history` pair as specified under [Capture and storage](#capture-and-storage). Intake sends an email once its full text decodes, or with its preview when the wait for full text expires, so today's "Full text of email" follow-up note retires.
- **Kept for the router.** The engine stores the sender's address and name, each recipient's address and `to` or `cc` in the order sent, the subject, the Outlook conversation id when supplied, and each partner whose mailbox the email arrived in, a second mailbox adding its partner. Door 21's queue rebuilds the thread and match inputs from these.
- **Stamp.** A job's email is `mail_capture` for the mailbox's partner. Nothing is edited here, so there is no version check.
- **Answer.** `201 { activity_id, existing, about: [...], unmatched: [addresses] }`. `unmatched` lists sender and recipient addresses no party has, so intake can still find new leads.
- **Refusals.** `422 about_nothing` when an email matches no record and names none: nothing is stored and the caller marks it done. `422 message_id_invalid` for a blank or space-padded id. `404 not_found { about }` for a named record that does not exist.

**Assistant deal update.** `POST /jobs/activities/deal-note` takes `{ idempotency_key, deal_id, text, plan? }`. Open this door as `agent` with `Engine-For-User`. The engine stores the trimmed update as a deal note at the time of its first save. A retry returns the original activity and plan or review result.

- With `plan: { what, due_on }`, both fields are required. The save records the note and replaces the sponsoring partner's ordinary step under door 18 in one transaction. It records the previous step as cancelled. It returns `201 { activity_id, next_step_id, next_step_required: false }`.
- Without `plan`, the save leaves the current plan in place. If the sponsoring partner's open ordinary step is overdue, the save also creates a pending Home review that cites the note and the step (35). The sponsor decides whether the work was done. It returns `201 { activity_id, proposal_id, next_step_required }`. The proposal id is null when there is no overdue step, and `next_step_required` is true when the deal has no current plan date.
- An unknown deal returns `404 not_found`. A job other than `agent` or an agent with no named partner gets `403 AUTHORIZATION_REFUSED`.

`POST /jobs/activities/{activity_id}/about` and `POST /api/engine/activities/{activity_id}/about` add links to a stored activity: `{ idempotency_key, about }`. The router uses it to file a reply to the deal its thread is filed to. A link already there is kept, not refused. Answer `{ activity_id, about }`, the full list. `404 not_found` for an unknown activity.

`GET /jobs/filed-emails?after=&limit=` pages, by activity id and with no age limit, the emails in the job's partner's mailbox that are linked to a deal: `{ emails: [{ activity_id, deal_ids, outgoing, body, happened_at }], next_cursor }`. Intake reads it, as it reads `email-router-queue` with `purpose=links` today (`outlook_intake.py:1497`), to write the "Links from matched email" note for a filing that landed after intake and to find an outgoing email's deal for a delivery report.

The "Links from matched email" note stays an ordinary note through this door, about the deal, keyed by email and deal. Flagged mail becomes a deal checklist item through door 18, and the sent copy of a reply arrives as an outgoing email here, so the outbox's own activity log retires.

### 20. Attachments upload and open

| Route                                                       | Request                                                                                                                     | Answer                                                                          |
| ----------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `PUT /jobs/files/{sha256}` and `/api/engine/files/{sha256}` | the file's bytes, `Content-Length` required                                                                                 | `{ sha256, content_type, bytes }`; `201` when stored, `200` when already stored |
| `POST /jobs/attachments`                                    | `idempotency_key`; `sha256`; `name`; `deal_id`; `kind`, a `document_kind` slug (`other` from intake); optional `message_id` | `201 { document_id, existing }`                                                 |
| `GET /jobs/documents/{id}` and `/api/engine/documents/{id}` | none                                                                                                                        | the bytes, with the stored type and the file name                               |

- The upload checks the bytes against the path's SHA-256, reads the type from the bytes as post media does, and stores to the `deal_documents` bucket under a key made from the hash. A file no row names is kept and never read, so an upload with no attachment request costs nothing.
- The attachment converges on the deal and hash. The same bytes on two emails of one deal are one deal file linked to both emails; different deals have separate filings. The same file again answers the stored document with `existing: true`. Stamp: `mail_capture` for the mailbox's partner, or the user.
- Refusals: upload `411 length_required`, `422 sha256_mismatch`, `422 unsupported_file`, `422 too_large`; attachment `409 email_not_stored`, retried after the email is sent, `409 file_not_uploaded`, `404 not_found` for the deal; open `404 not_found`. A missing bucket binding answers `503 database_unavailable`.

A partner files a document to a deal with `PUT /api/engine/files/{sha256}` and then `POST /api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/documents`. Both partners can see the document on the deal. The JSON body is `{ idempotency_key, sha256, name, kind, supersedes_id? }`. The name is nonblank, the kind is a `document_kind` slug, and the optional predecessor is a document UUID. The signed-in partner stamps the new row.

- A new filing answers `201 { document_id, deal_id, name, kind, sha256, supersedes_id, filed_at, filed_by }`. The predecessor is null for a first version. `filed_at` is the document's creation time in UTC. `filed_by` is the stamped user or job slug. A same-key replay answers `200` with the original receipt and `replayed: true`. A changed request or stamp reusing the key returns `409 key_reuse`.
- The same hash on the same deal with matching name, kind and predecessor answers `200` with the existing filing. A difference returns `409 already_filed { document_id }`. This check precedes the predecessor checks. The same bytes may be filed separately on another deal.
- When copied history has several filings for a deal and hash, both filing routes use the earliest filing by creation time, breaking ties by the lowest document id.
- A replacement adds the next version and keeps its predecessor. A missing predecessor or one on another deal returns `409 predecessor_not_found`. A different kind returns `409 predecessor_kind_differs`. A predecessor with a replacement returns `409 predecessor_replaced`, including when two replacements race.
- A missing deal returns `404 not_found`. Invalid input, including an unknown kind, follows the shared validation refusals. An unfiled hash with no uploaded object returns `409 file_not_uploaded`. Uploaded bytes are shared by hash.

### 21. Suggestions stored and decided

| Route                                        | Who       | Request                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| -------------------------------------------- | --------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `GET /jobs/router-queue?days=&after=&limit=` | job       | emails whose row in the job's partner's mailbox was stored in the last `days` (1 to 7, default 3) and that happened in the last 30 days, oldest first                                        |
| `POST /jobs/email-summaries`                 | job       | [Email summary result](#record-an-email-summary)                                                                                                                                             |
| `POST /jobs/proposals`                       | job       | `idempotency_key`, `kind`, `activity_id`, `evidence`, one of `deal_id`, `party_id` or `property_id` (none for `possible_new_deal`), and the kind's fields below                              |
| `GET /api/engine/proposals/{id}`             | user only | none                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `POST /api/engine/proposals/{id}/decision`   | user only | `idempotency_key`, `decision` (`accept` or `dismiss`), `edits` for a next step, `deal_id` or `vendor_id` for an offered filing choice, and `reason` for a filing correction or any dismissal |

The queue answers `{ emails, open_deals, next_cursor }` with what `email_matching.py` `thread_match` and `match` and the router's prompt read today (`email-proposals.js:957`):

- An email is `{ activity_id, message_id, happened_at, outgoing, mailbox_user, subject, body, conversation_id, lead_source, sender, recipients, about, deals, suppressed_deal_ids, thread }`. `mailbox_user` is the mailbox user's slug. `lead_source` is true when the email's activity id is in `lead_email_source`. `deals` are the open deals it is linked to, each `{ deal_id, name, phase, kind }`. `suppressed_deal_ids` are deals a partner took it off after an automatic filing (slice 7); the router never files or suggests it there again.
- A person, the sender or a recipient, is `{ address, name, kind, internal, party_id, client_party_ids, role }`. `kind` is `to` or `cc` for a recipient. Recipients keep their stored order on emails and thread entries. The sender uses the stored display name when no party is resolved. `internal` is an address at a user's work mail domain; personal addresses supply no internal domain. An address held by several parties stays unresolved. `client_party_ids` are the clients the person is a contact of or a lead linked to. `role` is `client`, `landlord_side`, `lender`, `attorney` or `other_vendor` from the party's client role or vendor category, as `resolveContact` reads it, or null for no party.
- `thread` lists earlier emails linked to a deal, each `{ activity_id, deal_id, happened_at, conversation_id, subject, sender, recipients }`. An Outlook conversation id matches the same id at any age. When both emails lack an id, the router matches the normalized subject (ignoring case, extra spaces and `Re:`, `Fw:` or `Fwd:`) within 30 days. The router still checks the outside recipients and whether the deal is unambiguous before filing.
- An open deal is `{ deal_id, name, phase, kind, client_party_id, client_name, property, city, participants: [{ party_id, name, role }] }`, where `property` comes from its first linked property and `city` is `market.name` through `deal.market_id`.

The suggestion kinds:
