| `kind`              | Fields                                                                  | On acceptance                                                    |
| ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `next_step`         | `what`; optional `owner` or `owner_party_id`, and `due_on`              | the next step, with the proposal's id                            |
| `next_step_done`    | `deal_id` and `next_step_id` with a note, call, or recorded meeting     | completes the existing next step                                 |
| `log_call`          | `what` (summary) and a linked `deal_id` or `party_id` on a call         | a note for a shared copied call; otherwise the call's summary    |
| `phase_move`        | `deal_id`; for a call or meeting, optional `to` and `on`                | the phase change                                                 |
| `new_lead`          | `party_id`, `how_found`                                                 | the lead role at New                                             |
| `email_lead`        | Legacy `candidate_id` from the email-lead route                         | Historical only after the lead intake change                     |
| `lead_stage_move`   | `party_id`, `from_stage`, `to_stage`, `strength`; `reason` for archived | the applied stage evidence                                       |
| `file_to_deal`      | `deal_id`, the suggested deal                                           | the email's link to the deal the partner names (slice 7)         |
| `possible_new_deal` | none                                                                    | the email's link to the deal the partner names; never a new deal |

- **Store.** The router stamps `mail_capture` and call capture `call_capture`; the database refuses a proposal whose job differs from the stamp. The same source and payload converge on the stored proposal, answered `200 { proposal_id, status, existing: true }`, so a dismissed suggestion is never made again. A new one answers `201 { proposal_id, status: "pending" }`. Refusals: `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule }` for a payload the kind does not allow or a party the activity is not about, and `404 not_found { activity_id }`.
- **Phase moves.** The router sends only the deal and its quote, as today (`email_router.py:135`). The engine reads the deal's phase when storing and fills `from_phase` and `to_phase` by today's rules (`email-deal-progress.ts:12`): a tour scheduled, confirmed or set moves research to site selection; an LOI moves site selection to negotiation; a signed or executed LOI, or on a lease kind a lease sent or out for review, moves negotiation to legal; on a lease kind a signed or executed lease moves legal to closing. A quote no rule moves is `422 phase_not_eligible`. Accepting derives the move again from the deal's current phase and the quote; when the phase has moved it is `409 version_conflict { current_phase }` and stays pending.
- **Stated phase moves.** A call or meeting may send a known target phase as `to`, with an optional Central calendar date as `on`. Only call and meeting phase suggestions take `to` or `on`. Supplying either from an email, or supplying `on` without `to`, returns `400 invalid_field_value`. An unknown target, the current phase, a move to dead without a reason, or a date before the current phase began returns `422 phase_not_eligible` when storing. The engine saves the current phase as `from_phase` and checks it again on acceptance. With `on`, the move follows door 16's date rule. A date before the current phase began or after the day of acceptance returns `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "proposal_phase_date" }` and leaves the suggestion pending. Without `on`, the phase begins on acceptance.
- **Legacy intake.** The current `POST /jobs/proposals/email-lead` takes `idempotency_key` (UUID), `address`, `summary` (up to 300 characters), `first_seen_at` (time with zone), `message_ref` (up to 100 characters), `applies_to: "new_matter"` and `confidence` (0 to 1). The optional `display_name` is up to 200 characters or null. The optional literal `evidence_quote` is 10 to 500 characters. Program 26 skips suggestion storage for `existing_deal`, `existing_party` and `not_business` readings. Its normal activity filing still uses independently matched records. Door 19 can refuse a message with no record links, so the suggestion cites the candidate and needs no `activity_id`.
- Mail capture can also send the optional `message_id` and `conversation_id` on the lead request. Use the same nonblank Message-ID header without angle brackets and the same nonblank Outlook conversation ID as door 19. Keep the case of both values. Home verifies matches against stored emails in the partner's mailbox. The engine keeps `message_ref` as an intake reference.
- Mail capture sends `Engine-For-User` for the mailbox owner. In the legacy intake, the engine saves the sender and reading in `candidate` with one candidate per normalized address and mailbox. A new suggestion answers `201 { proposal_id, status: "pending" }`. The same request replays its answer. A new key for the same candidate returns `200 { proposal_id, status, existing: true }`. A previously copied candidate without a suggestion returns `200 { candidate_id, status: "already_queued" }`. An address already on a contact returns `200 { status: "known_party" }`.
- **New intake.** `POST /jobs/leads/email-intake` requires `Engine-Job: mail_capture` and `Engine-For-User` for the mailbox partner. Send `idempotency_key` (UUID, scoped to the mailbox), `happened_at` (time with zone), the door 19 `email` object with the exact Message-ID, and `kind` (`direct_inquiry`, `lead_source`, `uncertain`, `internal`, `system` or `existing_relationship`).
- Keep the full captured text in `email.body`. When capture supplies a split, send both `email.authored` and `email.history` as strings without NUL characters. Send an empty history string for a message with no quoted text, signatures, footers, or banners. Requests with only the full body still work. A capture that adds a second mailbox can fill a missing pair on the original source. Later copies keep the first stored pair and full body.
- Send a literal body quote as `evidence` and `prospect` with `kind` (`person` or `company`), `name`, and the recorded `email`, `phone` (E.164) or `street_address` with `city`. A direct inquiry uses the prospect's sender address. For a lead-source notice, take the name and contact facts from the body. Ineligible requests can omit missing prospect facts or evidence.
- Intake answers `201 { party_id, activity_id, created_lead: true }` for a new lead and `200 { party_id, activity_id, created_lead: false }` when it reuses a lead. A same-key retry replays the original answer. Ineligible intake records the evidence and answers `422 { error: "lead_email_not_eligible", hint }`. The historical decision route remains available during the transition. See [Leads from email](#leads-from-email).
- **Stored notices.** `POST /jobs/leads/email-reconcile` uses the same job and mailbox headers. Send `activity_id` and `idempotency_key` (UUIDs). With `dry_run: true`, it answers only `{ eligible_emails, filing_decisions }` and writes nothing. A copied notice can give the street and city together in an `Address:` field on the prospect's contact line, in street, city, state and ZIP order. The engine reads contact facts through the first bullet segment after the prospect name. A phone or an em dash in that slot can be followed by a bare email address in the next slot. The contact block ends after that email token or at the first later segment, received date, or quoted reply. The engine keeps a street and city when the street is at most 80 characters and has a house number, street name and recognized street type such as Street, Ave or Rd. A numbered route may use its type and route number in place of a street name and type. The street type may end in a period. A direction and a unit identifier may follow. A street that matches the prospect name is dropped. Case and spacing differences in a repeated city are accepted. A conflicting city drops the address while keeping other qualifying contact facts. Apply links a recognized, eligible notice through lead intake and supersedes that email's pending filing proposals in the same save. Copied mail can use its stored activity ID when the Message-ID is missing. It also returns `party_id` when the email is linked. Reply drafts and independent decisions remain pending.
- Accepting a `new_lead` suggestion for an existing contact replaces `not_recorded` with the recorded how-found value.
- **Automatic filing.** Engine code files mail when saved routing evidence identifies a single eligible destination. Door 19 checks the deal links on every stored message with the exact Outlook conversation ID and can file if they name one deal that is open. Capture and the router's `/about` save skip a deal link when the email is already on a different deal or a partner suppressed the match. Existing party and property links stay in place. On first delivery, if the thread has no deal links and the exact sender address identifies one party with one vendor match through its own role or a current company in `party_company`, the engine files to that vendor. Home hides a filing proposal once its deal link exists or a partner has suppressed that match. Queue uncertain filing that can use judgment for the mailbox partner's link. Keep unresolved filing and possible-deal proposals for a partner.
- **Relevant facts from known mail.** When enqueueing is enabled, mail capture queues one filing job per email and mailbox partner. Eligible deals come from stored links on the email or its exact Outlook conversation. Eligible vendors come from the sender's exact address and a verified vendor role or current vendor company. Lead-source mail stays in Leads. The engine checks every result against the claim, current links, and partner suppressions. It saves the original email links and the relevant facts as stamped activity notes on their destinations. An unresolved result keeps the existing filing review or opens one on the source email. A prior unlink or dismissal prevents repeating that filing question. A successful filing removes the resolved filing review. Reply drafts and other suggestions keep their decisions.
- **Filing.** A filing suggestion stores no deal name and creates no deal. Accepting moves the email to the deal the partner names in `deal_id`, which defaults to the suggested deal for `file_to_deal` and is required for `possible_new_deal`; the deal must be open. Other deal links are removed in the same save; party and property links stay. `reason` is required when the email is already linked to another deal or the named deal's client is not one it is about, as today (`email-proposals.js:291`). Today's "mark handled" also files the email to the named deal (`:305`), so it is this accept, not a separate outcome. A partner who needs a new deal makes it first (17) and then names it. Slice 7 adds both kinds to `proposal.kind` and lets `proposal_one_record` name no record for `possible_new_deal` alone, whose subject is the email.
- **Decide.** Only a direct partner accepts or dismisses a proposal, once. Mail capture can supersede a pending filing whose exact email is linked to a lead. The job records the time and the reason “Moved to Leads.” `edits` (`what`, `owner_party_id`, `due_on`) change the accepted next step in the same transaction, so history shows the suggestion and the edit and the suggestion itself never changes. On `dismiss`, a nonblank `reason` is saved in `dismiss_reason` for every suggestion kind (filing dismissals included), and a blank `reason` is refused. On `accept`, `reason` is allowed only on filing suggestions. A decision settles its pending siblings from the same source: one email thread in the partner's mailbox, or one call or meeting. Siblings are filings of the same kind and deal, or next steps for the same `party_id` and owner. Dismissing gives them the same reason. Accepting a next step dismisses its siblings with reason “Replaced by the accepted next step for this person”, and their linked cards read as replaced. Answer `{ proposal_id, status, decided_at, created: { next_step_id | deal_id | activity_id | party_id } }`. Refusals: `409 already_decided { status, decided_by }`; `409 version_conflict { current_phase }` or `{ current_stage }` when the deal or lead moved since the suggestion, leaving it pending; `403 user_decides` from a job; `404 not_found`.
- **Next-step owner.** A supplied `owner` user slug resolves to that user's person. On store, supplying both owner fields or using `owner` on another kind of suggestion returns `400 invalid_field_value`. An unknown slug returns `400 unknown_owner`. An owner ID in `edits.owner_party_id` overrides the suggestion's owner. Omitted, blank, and `null` owner edits keep the suggested owner. If the suggestion has no owner, the step belongs to the partner who accepts it.
- **Accept a deal step from a call or meeting.** Storing a call's `next_step` suggestion for a deal requires the call to be about that deal. Acceptance joins the final owner's open ordinary step when the final text matches after collapsing Unicode whitespace, trimming and lowercasing, with punctuation preserved. Held steps qualify. The answer returns the existing `created.next_step_id`, and the step's date changes only for `due_source = 'stated'`. For a different action, a native proposal replaces the open step and a copied proposal returns `409 open_step_exists { next_step_id }` and stays pending. Other suggestions that conflict with an open step return the same refusal.
- **Held steps (35).** A job acting for the step's owner can store a completion suggestion about an open, overdue deal step outside the checklist. The note, call, or recorded meeting must be linked to the same deal. The due date must precede the activity's Central calendar day. One suggestion is kept per activity and step. Storing it leaves the step open.
- Only the step's owner can click Resolve (`accept`) or Dismiss (`dismiss`). The decision answers with `next_step_id` and an empty `created` object. Resolving stamps and completes the step in the same save. When a step ends, its pending call suggestion becomes hidden and a decision returns `404 not_found`. If the suggestion remains visible after the step changed, resolution returns `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "proposal_step_changed" }`. Dismissal closes the suggestion and leaves the step intact.
- The deal detail derives its date from the current step. A note's resolution advances that date only if it completes the current step. A call or recorded meeting's resolution preserves the current date until a newer ordinary step takes its place.
- **Read on Home (35).** `GET /api/engine/home/held-steps` answers `{ proposals, automatic_resolutions }` for the signed-in partner. Each pending hold on an open step includes `proposal_id`, `deal_id`, `deal_name`, `next_step_id`, `what`, `due_on`, `evidence`, `activity_id`, `activity_kind`, `happened_at`, `summary` and `can_resolve`. Both partners see the source activity fields and evidence. An edited step can still be dismissed. Resolved and dismissed holds and ended steps leave the list. The Undo list shows automatic completions recorded in the last seven days while the completion is still the step's latest change. It omits a completion if the partner already has an open ordinary step on that deal. Each entry includes `next_step_id`, `what`, `done_at`, `change_id`, `changed_by_job`, `deal_id` and `deal_name`. In both lists `what` reads an automatic check-in as on Home. App wiring is step 30.
- Snoozing a suggestion is a Home mark (slices 10, 24). Stored email summaries move in the email copy (decision 177).

#### Record an email summary

`POST /jobs/email-summaries` takes the jobs Access token, `Engine-Job: mail_capture`, and `Engine-For-User` with the mailbox partner's slug. Send a JSON object with these fields.

| Field               | Value                                                                      |
| ------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `activity_id`       | UUID of the stored engine email in that partner's mailbox                  |
| `source_hash`       | Captured-content hash as `sha256:` followed by 64 lowercase hex digits     |
| `generator_version` | Version of the writer that produced the result                             |
| `status`, `text`    | `available` with 1 to 300 characters of text, or `unavailable` with `null` |
| `provenance`        | JSON object with the recorded details, including the model when recorded   |
| `recorded_at`       | Time the result was recorded, with a zone                                  |

A save returns `200` with the stored fields and `source_activity_id`, equal to `activity_id` for a new result. The response expresses `recorded_at` in UTC. PostgreSQL stores and returns provenance numbers at `jsonb` precision. The activity ID, hash, and generator version form this door's retry key. Replay returns the first stored answer, including its time. Different status, text, or provenance for that key returns `409 email_summary_result_conflict`.

**Home action at intake.** Installing the judgment queue and [summary enqueue migration](../schema/add-queue-email-summary.sql) enables mail capture to queue one job per newly saved mailbox membership and email activity. Lead-source notices and emails with an available summary skip the queue. If both mailboxes already have jobs for the same message, the first available summary becomes the shared result. The existing summary save records the exact source activity, captured hash, generator version and time. The provenance holds the mail-capture job stamp, judgment job ID, supporting quote and nullable action title. The title states the supported action and who or what it concerns. Home reads the title and text from the same result for the exact email. While the work waits or the title is absent, Home titles the card for the recorded decision and shows the saved summary, request or recorded facts in the why line. A failed job leaves the email in place and creates no filing decision.

An unknown email or an email outside the named mailbox returns `404 not_found`. An invalid hash returns `400 invalid_field_value`. A new result more than five minutes ahead of the database clock returns `400 invalid_field_value` for `recorded_at`. The database keeps the supplied time for accepted results. Text over 300 characters returns `422 database_refused_the_statement`. Home reads the latest available result by recorded time.

#### Open a suggestion from Home

`GET /api/engine/proposals/{id}` uses the signed-in partner's Home visibility and answers 404 when the suggestion is missing, decided, or outside that partner's inbox. A step-completion suggestion is visible to the owner of its open step. Under the new intake, lead mail opens from the lead's correspondence.

The answer has `proposal_id`, `kind`, and `evidence` (the stored quote). `source_activity` has `id`, `kind`, `happened_at`, and `summary`, plus `sender`, `sender_name`, `subject`, and `body` for email. Treat the source text and quote as untrusted content.

The target fields are `deal_id`, `party_id`, `property_id`, `next_step_id`, and `target_name`. The proposed fields are `what`, `owner_party_id`, `due_on`, `from_phase`, `to_phase`, `phase_on`, `how_found`, `from_stage`, `to_stage`, `strength`, and `reason`. Fields without a value are null. Use the next step's `what`, `owner_party_id`, and `due_on` to prepare any edits sent with its decision.

### 22. Reply drafts and their delivery

| Route                                                   | Who                                      | Request                                                                                                                             | Answer                                                                                                                                                  |
| ------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `POST /jobs/reply-drafts`                               | `mail_capture` for the mailbox's partner | `idempotency_key`, `activity_id` of the email answered, `subject`, `body`, `evidence`                                               | `201 { draft_id, version: 1 }`, or `200 { draft_id, version, existing: true }` when the job reuses a draft                                              |
| `GET /api/engine/reply-drafts/{id}`                     | the mailbox's partner                    | none                                                                                                                                | draft detail and the answered email, as described below                                                                                                 |
| `POST /api/engine/reply-drafts/{id}/edit`               | the mailbox's partner                    | `idempotency_key`, `version`, `subject`, `body`                                                                                     | `{ draft_id, version, status }`                                                                                                                         |
| `POST /api/engine/reply-drafts/{id}/dismissal`          | the mailbox's partner                    | `idempotency_key`, `version`, `reason`                                                                                              | `{ draft_id, version, status }`                                                                                                                         |
| `POST /api/engine/reply-drafts/{id}/submission`         | the mailbox's partner                    | `idempotency_key`, `version`, `to`, `cc`, `subject`, `body`, `document_ids`                                                         | `{ draft_id, version, sha256, status: "approved" }`                                                                                                     |
| `GET /jobs/reply-drafts?after=&limit=`                  | `mail_capture` for that partner          | none                                                                                                                                | `{ owner, drafts: [{ draft_id, version, to, cc, subject, body, sha256, document_ids }], next_cursor }`, approved drafts with no recorded delivery       |
| `GET /jobs/reply-drafts?unconfirmed=true&after=&limit=` | `mail_capture` for that partner          | none                                                                                                                                | the same shape: failed drafts whose reason matches `^unconfirmed_after_[0-9]+$`, at most 100 a page, for the outbox to look for in the Sent folder only |
| `POST /jobs/reply-drafts/{id}/delivery`                 | `mail_capture` for that partner          | `idempotency_key`, `version`, `sha256`, `status` (`sent` or `failed`), `sent_item_id` and `sent_at` when sent, `reason` when failed | `{ draft_id, version, status }`                                                                                                                         |

- **One draft per email.** A second store for an email that already has a draft for that partner answers the first draft unchanged, whatever its key, as today's store keeps one per email (`email-proposals.js:806`). The job and Doc share a lock for the mailbox and Outlook conversation, or the mailbox and exact email when there is no conversation ID. Doc can reuse a pending draft for another email in the conversation if that email is in the partner's mailbox. The job can reuse a pending Doc draft on the same terms.
- **One open draft per conversation after a new store.** When a store creates a draft, only the draft for the newest email stays pending for that partner and Outlook conversation id. The newest email has the latest recorded email time (`activity.happened_at`), with the greatest activity UUID breaking ties. A draft stored late for an older email is dismissed in the same save. The save records dismissals with the reason and job in change history. Approved, sent, and failed drafts keep their status. Emails without a conversation id are independent. Home skips `reply_draft` rows with `status = 'dismissed'`, so a cleared draft no longer appears.
- **Never twice.** A draft is sent at one version, its `revision`, which every change its partner makes advances, approval included, and a recorded try never does. The outbox keys its send and receipt on draft id and version, as today, and checks the Sent folder before sending anything the list still shows. The engine records one `sent` per version: the same `sent_item_id` again converges, a `failed` whose reason matches `unconfirmed_after_` may later become `sent` when the outbox finds it in the Sent folder (`outlook_outbox.py:223`), and `sent` never becomes `failed`. A failed draft returns to the approved list only when its partner submits it again after `aborted_before_dispatch` or `submission_recipients_invalid`, and any other failure stays failed, so it is never sent again on its own. The sent mail itself arrives through door 19.
- **Digest.** `sha256` is the lowercase hex SHA-256 of the UTF-8 bytes of `JSON.stringify({ subject, body })`, subject first, with nothing else in it. That is the same as `json.dumps({'subject': subject, 'body': body}, separators=(',', ':'), ensure_ascii=False)`, which the outbox recomputes and checks before sending anything (`outlook_outbox.py:34`). No column holds it: the door computes it from the approved subject and body, which a try cannot change.
- **Checks.** Delivery checks `version` and `sha256` against the approved draft and returns `409 draft_changed { version }` if the draft changed. A different sent item returns `409 already_sent { sent_item_id }`. A submission for a dismissed draft returns `409 draft_changed { version }`. Other stale submissions return `409 version_conflict { version }`. A partner who does not own the draft gets `403 not_mailbox_owner`. Attachments are documents, linked in `reply_draft_document` and fetched through door 20.

#### Open, save, or decline a reply draft from Home

`GET /api/engine/reply-drafts/{id}` answers `{ draft_id, version, status, failure_reason, to, cc, subject, body, attachments, source_activity }` for the draft owner. Each attachment has `document_id`, `name`, and `content_type`. The answered email in `source_activity` has `id`, `kind`, `sender`, `sender_name`, `happened_at`, `subject`, and `body`.

While pending, To is the answered email's sender. CC uses the other partner's mailbox address when stored. The new mailbox table stores no address, so the read uses the other user's primary work email when exactly one qualifies, or null. After approval, the answer uses the frozen addresses.

- `POST /api/engine/reply-drafts/{id}/edit` requires `idempotency_key`, `version`, `subject`, and `body`. It saves the text and leaves the draft pending.
- `POST /api/engine/reply-drafts/{id}/dismissal` requires `idempotency_key`, `version`, and a nonblank `reason` of at most 500 characters, as the old decline operation did. It records the reason and changes the draft to dismissed.

Both saves answer `{ draft_id, version, status }` with the new revision. A stale revision returns `409 version_conflict { version }`. A current revision on a draft that has left pending returns `409 draft_changed { version }`. A missing draft is 404. Another partner gets `403 not_mailbox_owner` on the read or either save.

### 25. Calendar agenda, review dismissals and meeting confirmations

| Route                                                         | Who                                       | Request                                                                                    | Answer                                                            |
| ------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `PUT /jobs/calendar/agenda`                                   | `mail_capture` for the calendar's partner | today's agenda snapshot: `schema`, `observed_at`, `items`, `follow_up_reviews`, `warnings` | `{ observed_at }`                                                 |
| `GET /jobs/calendar/agenda` and `/api/engine/calendar/agenda` | job for a partner, or the user            | none                                                                                       | the stored snapshot, with `dismissed` and `confirmed` review keys |
| `POST /api/engine/calendar/reviews/{key}/dismissal`           | user only                                 | `idempotency_key`                                                                          | `{ key, dismissed_at }`                                           |

- The agenda is one row per partner, replaced whole, with no idempotency key: storing the same or a newer `observed_at` replaces it, and an older one is `409 agenda_superseded { observed_at }`, which the caller drops. A snapshot whose JSON is over 262,144 characters is `422 agenda_too_large`, as today (`board-answers.js:120`); the producer trims it to 200,000 bytes first (`calendar_agenda.py:16`).
- Outlook identifies each occurrence with its event `key` in both `items` and `follow_up_reviews`. Home keeps that identity and prefers the calendar row when both are present. The publisher needs to include full `items` rows for every occurrence overlapping today, including ended meetings that have no review. [Outlook publisher change](https://github.com/DoctorCRE/carr-system/issues/3265)
- A dismissal converges on partner and key: a second answers the first. `404 review_not_found` when the key is not among the partner's current reviews.
- "It happened" is a meeting through door 19 with `calendar_review_key`. One meeting per partner and key: a second answers the stored one with `existing: true`.
- Calendar, Today, and vendor meetings read `next.calendar_agenda`.
- Vendor meetings uses Calendar's stored agenda read for the signed-in partner. Each possible meeting matches the vendor contact's exact organizer or attendee email. With no stored agenda, the vendor list still loads and the calendar says it is unavailable.

### 16. Deal fields and phase moves

`POST /jobs/deals` and `POST /api/engine/deals` add a deal.

- `call_capture` cannot add a deal. Its request returns `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "deal_phase_change_call_source" }`.
- Ordinary Add deal requires `idempotency_key` (UUID), `name` (nonblank text), `client_id` (an existing client's party UUID), `owner` (user slug), and `phase` (an open phase slug). It accepts `kind` and `reason` (slugs), `market_id` (market UUID), and `renews_lease_id` (lease UUID). Omit an unknown kind, reason, or market, or send null. A renewal reason requires the source lease. Any other reason, including an unknown one, requires the source lease field to be absent or null.
- The deal, its client link, and its first phase save together. National status follows the client's account membership.
- To turn a prospect into a deal, the signed-in form adds `prospect_id` (the prospect's party UUID). It sends either `client_id` (an existing party with a stored `how_found`) or `client: { name, kind, how_found }` for someone new. The new client's kind is `person` or `company`. See [Prospects and the Monday review](#prospects-and-the-monday-review).
- The signed-in market deal form also sends `national_account_id` and requires `market_id`. Send `client_id` for a location currently in the account or `new_location: { name, kind }` to add a person or company as a franchise client. The partner who saves it owns the new client. Its brand membership starts today in Central time and how found is `national_account` (National account or corporate lead). An optional `market_agent_id` names an existing person. The client, membership, deal and agent assignment save together.
- A missing account returns `404 not_found`. A client from another account or without a current membership returns `409 location_not_in_account`. A matching location name in the account returns `409 location_name_exists { candidates }`. It matches without regard to case. All deal creation paths return `409 deal_name_exists` for a matching open deal name. A rename or reopening that would use another open deal’s name returns the same refusal.
- The answer is `201 { deal_id, change_id, phase_changed_at }`. A retry answers `200` with the same fields and `replayed: true`. An unknown owner returns `400 unknown_owner`. An ended phase returns `400 invalid_field_value { fields: ["phase"] }`. The database refuses an unknown vocabulary value or an invalid renewal link. Ordinary Add deal requires the selected party to already have the client role.

The signed-in market deal form reads its choices from the new schema. `GET /api/engine/markets` returns every market as an array of `{ market_id, name }`. `GET /api/engine/national-accounts/{account_id}/locations` returns an array of `{ party_id, name, kind }`, where kind is `person` or `company`. It includes only memberships current today in Central time, with an inclusive start and an exclusive end. An unknown account returns `404 not_found`. Both lists are ordered by name and return an empty array when there are no choices.

The [national account saves](#national-account-saves) describe how a partner adds an account or changes its owner.

For a renewal, Add deal reads `GET /api/engine/clients/{client_id}/leases` after the partner picks a client. This signed-in read takes no query parameters and uses `DATABASE` as `engine_reader`. It returns an array of `{ lease_id, address, expiration_on, renewed_by }` for leases whose recorded tenant is that client, including leases negotiated elsewhere. The address is the property's address. The expiration comes from the latest version and is null when the lease has no version. The list is ordered by address, latest expiration first at the same address, then lease ID. `renewed_by` contains `{ deal_id, name }` for the most recently created open or closed won renewal linked to the lease, with ties resolved by descending deal ID. It is null when all linked renewals were lost or there are no linked renewals. The app shows "already renewed by <deal>" using the returned name. Use `lease_id` as `renews_lease_id` in the save. A malformed client ID returns `404 not_found`. A valid ID with no eligible leases returns an empty array. A successor tenant's renewal cannot be picked through this list. The save still accepts a successor tenant's source lease as described in [Leases and options](#leases-and-options).

`GET /jobs/deals/{deal_id}` answers `{ deal_id, phase, phase_saved_at, change_id, next_step: { id, due_on } | null }` for a registered job. Use `phase` as `phase.from` and `change_id` as `seen_change_id` on a save. `phase_saved_at` is the current phase's save timestamp with an offset. It comes from `changed_at` on the same `deal_phase_change` row. Call capture can compare this timestamp with the call time even when a move has a backdated `on` date. Both phase fields are null before the first move, and the change id is 0 before the first deal change. The next step is the newest open step outside the checklist, with a null date when undated. An unknown job gets `403 unknown_job`, and an unknown deal gets `404 not_found`.

`POST /jobs/deals/{deal_id}/changes` and `POST /api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/changes`

| Field             | Type                                                                                                                                          | Required            |
| ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- |
| `idempotency_key` | UUID                                                                                                                                          | yes                 |
| `seen_change_id`  | integer: the deal's latest change the caller read, 0 for none                                                                                 | yes                 |
| `fields`          | any of `name`, `kind`, `reason`, `owner` (user slug), `market_id`, `closed_value`, `parking_reason`, `parking_note`, `needs_attention`        | `fields` or `phase` |
| `phase`           | `{ from, to, on?, reason?, activity_id? }`: the phase the caller saw, the new one, a date in Central time, a reason, and the source call UUID | `fields` or `phase` |

- `changeDeal` applies `fields` with its rule: a later change to one of the same fields is `409 version_conflict { current_version }`; a later change to other fields stands and the answer is `{ deal_id, rebased: true, intervening_change_ids }`, so the caller re-reads. A phase move is refused `409 version_conflict { current_phase }` when the deal's phase is not `from`, as accepting a phase suggestion checks it, under the same row lock.
- Answer: `{ deal_id, change_id }`, or the rebased answer, plus `phase_changed_at` for a move.
- For Won, send `phase: { from, to: "closed", on }` and, when known, `fields: { closed_value }` with the positive amount as a JSON number. An omitted value leaves the field as it was. For Lost, send `phase: { from, to: "dead", on, reason }` with a nonblank reason. Both moves require a real `YYYY-MM-DD` date in Central time, from the day the current phase began through today. A missing date returns `400 missing_required { fields: ["phase.on"] }`. The phase and any field changes save together.
- With the new Deals reads on, a new loss shows the dead phase's date as `closed_on` and its reason as `lost_reason`. The deal page answers the recorded `closed_value` as a decimal string or null.
- Stamp: the user, or a job for a partner. A job alone may not park, revive or flag a deal: `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "deal_parked_or_flagged_by_a_user" }`. `404 not_found` for an unknown deal; `400 unknown_owner`.
- Callers: call capture's automatic phase moves (`call_capture`, with today's confidence bar, which moves into the engine in slice 35), the assistant (`agent`) and the app. The nightly lead stages use the invoice application below.
- For an automatic phase move, `call_capture` must send the UUID from door 19 as `phase.activity_id`. An omitted ID returns `400 missing_required { fields: ["phase.activity_id"] }`. An automatic move must leave a previous phase for Undo, so a null `from` or a target identical to `from` returns `400 invalid_field_value { fields: ["phase.from"] }` and goes to suggestion review.
- An unknown activity ID or a non-call activity returns `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "deal_phase_change_source_is_call" }`. A call without a link to this deal returns `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "deal_phase_change_call_on_deal" }`. If a partner or another job sends `phase.activity_id`, the save returns `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "deal_phase_change_call_source" }`.
- When a partner accepts a call suggestion through door 21, the phase row has that partner's stamp and gives no automatic receipt. The call stays linked to the suggestion.

#### Invoice application

| Route                                               | Who          | Request                                                |
| --------------------------------------------------- | ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| `POST /jobs/invoice-emails/{invoice_id}/apply`      | `lead_stage` | `idempotency_key`, `deal_id`, `payment_id` (all UUIDs) |
| `POST /api/engine/invoice-emails/{invoice_id}/undo` | Direct user  | `idempotency_key` (UUID)                               |

- Both saves answer `200 { invoice_id, deal_id, payment_id, status, application_id }`. The application takes a captured invoice and uses its email date. The status is `applied` for an original, `attached` for a resend with the original's ID in `application_id`, or `undone` after a partner's undo. `application_id` is null for an original.
- The database checks the match and the selected payment as described in "Invoices found in email". It appends the Closed phase, dates the payment and saves both undo references together. A doubtful invoice remains captured with `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "invoice_email_needs_user" }`.
- Undo appends the prior phase and restores the selected payment date. A recorded payment receipt returns `422 invoice_already_paid`. Newer work on the phase or invoice date returns `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "invoice_email_undo_current" }`. Repeating a completed transition with a new key returns `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "invoice_email_status_moves" }` for the same target. Same-key retries get the original answer. An unknown invoice returns `404 not_found`.

### 18. Next steps

| Route                              | Request                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Answer                                              |
| ---------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| `POST .../next-steps`              | `idempotency_key`, `what`, and one of `deal_id`, `party_id`, `property_id` or `old_role`. Optional fields are `owner` or `owner_party_id`, `due_on`, `hold_until`, `replaces: "current"` and `item: { title, bullets, source_ref }` for a deal checklist item. | `201 { next_step_id, change_id }`                   |
| `POST .../next-steps/{id}/changes` | `idempotency_key`, `seen_change_id`, any of `what`, `owner_party_id`, `due_on`, `hold_until`, `item_bullets`                                                                                                                                                   | `{ next_step_id, change_id }` or the rebased answer |
| `POST .../next-steps/{id}/end`     | `idempotency_key`, `base_version`, `resolution` (`done` or `cancelled`), `done_at` optional, `reason` required for cancelled                                                                                                                                   | `{ next_step_id, status, change_id }`               |
| `POST .../next-steps/{id}/reopen`  | `idempotency_key`, `base_version`                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `{ next_step_id, status: "open" }`                  |

`...` is `/jobs` or `/api/engine`.

- **Owner on creation.** Use `owner` for a user slug, which resolves to the user's person, or `owner_party_id` for a person ID. Both fields together return `400 invalid_field_value`, and an unknown slug returns `400 unknown_owner`. On creation without an owner, the database uses the signed-in partner's person or the person named by `Engine-For-User`.
- **Job follow-up wording.** On creation by a job, a `what` ending in `, <Weekday> <Mon> <D> <h:mm> <AM|PM>` is stored as `Follow up on the <Mon> <D> meeting: <subject>`. Weekdays are `Mon` through `Sun`, months are `Jan` through `Dec`, days are 1–31 with an optional leading zero, hours are 1–12, and minutes are 00–59. A leading zero is kept in the stored wording, so `Oct 07` stays `Oct 07`. The subject is everything before the trailing comma and date, including any ` | company` text. For example, `Site visit with A. Example | Example Co, Tue Oct 27 4:30 PM` becomes `Follow up on the Oct 27 meeting: Site visit with A. Example | Example Co`. All other wording and every partner save stay unchanged. Subsequent reads show the stored wording.
- **Call follow-up on creation.** A recorded call can supply `call: { activity_id, evidence_quote }` with the step's `deal_id`. Both values are required when `call` is sent. The activity must be a call about that deal. Accepting a call's deal-step suggestion in door 21 also keeps its original activity and evidence on the step, even if the partner edits the step's words.
- **Replace the current step.** Create with `replaces: "current"` and one of `deal_id`, `party_id` or `old_role`. The save cancels that owner's open ordinary step as superseded and creates the new one in the same transaction. The previous step stays in history. Omit `due_on` to leave the new step undated. A replacement with `property_id` or `item` returns `400 invalid_field_value`.
- **Version a replacement.** Signed-in replacements with `deal_id` or `party_id` require `seen_current_step: { next_step_id, change_id }`. Omission returns `400 missing_required`. Send `null` if the partner saw no open ordinary step. Omit the owner fields on a versioned replacement. A stale step ID or change ID returns `409 version_conflict { current_version }`. The current version is null when the partner has no open ordinary step. A job may omit `seen_current_step`. A job sending it must name a partner through `Engine-For-User` or the save returns `422 actor_not_provisioned`.
- **Replace your own deal step.** Use the `id` and `change_id` of the partner's open ordinary step in `next_actions`.
- **Replace a prospect follow-up.** Use the step ID and change ID from the prospect read. Supply a due date. A party that has left the lane returns `409 not_a_prospect`.
- **Complete by subject.** `POST .../next-steps/complete` takes `idempotency_key` and one of `deal_id`, `party_id` or `old_role`. It marks the signed-in partner's or sponsoring partner's open ordinary step done, including a held step. It returns `{ next_step_id, status: "done" }`, `404 not_found` for an unknown deal or party ID, or `409 no_open_step` if that owner has no matching step. A retry with the same key returns the original completion even if a new step has since opened.
- **Use a copied role ID.** On create and complete, `old_role: { kind, id }` stands in for the subject. `kind` is `lead`, `client` or `vendor`, and `id` is the old role row's UUID. The `outside_id` primary key `(source, value)` keeps each old ID on one record. The save uses the `old_system` entry and requires its party to hold the named role. It returns `404 old_role_not_found` when no party matches. A successful response also includes the resolved `party_id`. Use it to file a separate outcome note about the same party.
- An owner can have one ordinary open step on each deal or party. Lead, client, and vendor steps use their party record. Held steps count as open. Ended steps, titled deal checklist items, property steps, and steps without an owner are outside this rule. A create, owner change, or reopen that would break it returns `409 open_step_exists { next_step_id }`. The id names the step to use and is null if it ceased to conflict before the lookup.
- **Retry.** The cadence keys each step on its rule, record and due date, intake keys a flag's item on mailbox, message and state, and call capture keys on the call and its suggestion, so a rerun converges on the first step.
- **Concurrent edits.** A change uses `seen_change_id` on the step's change history with the deal rule above. For `end`, Home sends the card's numeric `action_version` as `base_version`. For Undo, send the completion's `change_id` as `base_version` to `reopen` with a fresh key. Both saves lock the step and return `409 version_conflict { current_version }` if its latest change ID differs. With the current version, ending an ended step returns `409 step_ended { status }` and reopening an open step returns `409 step_open`. Jobs may omit the version on both saves. Signed-in Deals checklist completions may also omit it. Same-key retries return the original receipt.
- **Undo an automatic resolution (35).** `POST /api/engine/next-steps/{id}/undo-automatic-resolution` takes `{ idempotency_key, change_id }` from Home. The owner can undo a job's completion of their ordinary deal step. A later change to the step, an already undone completion, or a direct partner's completion returns `409 automatic_resolution_changed`. The step lock keeps the history check and reopen in one save. A replacement open step still returns `409 open_step_exists`. Success stamps the owner, reopens the same step and answers `{ next_step_id, status: "open" }`. The deal date follows the resulting current step.
- **Stamp.** The user, or `cadence`, `mail_capture`, `call_capture` or `agent` for a partner.
- **Refusals.** `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule }` for a checklist item of the wrong shape (`next_step_item_shape`), a cancel without a reason, or a step about two records; `404 not_found` for the step or its record.

#### Cadence inputs

`GET /jobs/cadence?collection=clients|leads|vendors|next_steps&after=&limit=` is read-only and requires the registered `cadence` job behind the jobs Access door. For `next_steps`, set `status=open|done`. Each collection answers `{ items, next_cursor }` in id order. `after` excludes the last id already read, and the final cursor is null. `limit` defaults to 100 and accepts 1 through 500. Invalid queries return `400 invalid_field_value`. Another or unregistered job gets `403 unknown_job`.

- Subjects answer `{ id, name, ref, client_status, owner, owner_party_id, old_ids }`. Use the subject's `id` as `party_id` in door 18. `old_ids` lists `old_system` outside ids as `{ value }`. `ref` is the first retained `old_system_ref` with the collection's C-, L- or V- prefix, or null. `owner` is the owner's user slug.
- Clients and converted leads answer the party's stored `cold` or `paused` status when set, and its derived client status otherwise. Leads without a client role answer null for that status. Leads also answer `estimated_lease_on` as a date or null. Converted parties appear in both collections because the active lead rules use lease dates and only the client and vendor rules use completions. Vendors answer null for client status. They answer their relationship partner's user slug as `owner` and that user's party id as `owner_party_id`. Both owner fields are null for an unassigned vendor.
- Open party steps answer `{ id, party_id, owner_party_id }`, including held steps. Done party steps also answer `{ description, done_at }`. Skip completions with a null `done_at`.

If a cadence save returns `409 open_step_exists`, skip that reminder for this run and continue with the other records. The existing step, including a held one, occupies its place. Rules and lookback stay on the old side. The schedule's eligibility checks and date calculation stay with the saved follow-up. Home keeps each open step while automatic schedule maintenance is pending. Only the owner provisions the `cadence` job.

### Deals participants and critical dates

These doors use the signed-in partner entrance. They share the save and retry rules above.

| POST path                                               | JSON body                                          | Success                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| `/api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/parties`                   | `{ idempotency_key, party_id, role }`              | `201 { deal_id, party_id, role }`          |
| `/api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/parties/remove`            | `{ idempotency_key, party_id, role }`              | `200 { deal_id, party_id, role }`          |
| `/api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/critical-dates`            | `{ idempotency_key, kind, due_on, source, note? }` | `201 { critical_date_id, status: "open" }` |
| `/api/engine/critical-dates/{critical_date_id}/confirm` | `{ idempotency_key }`                              | `200 { critical_date_id, status: "open" }` |
| `/api/engine/critical-dates/{critical_date_id}/resolve` | `{ idempotency_key, resolution, reason }`          | `200 { critical_date_id, status }`         |

- All IDs are UUIDs. `party_id` names the existing person or company and `role` is its role slug on this deal. Each save adds or removes exactly that link. A missing link on removal returns `404 not_found`. The database records the removal in deal history. Deal ownership changes use door 16.
- A date the partner adds opens in the same save, with the database recording that partner and the confirmation time. `kind` is `loi_expiry`, `option_notice`, `lease_commencement`, `rent_start`, `lease_end`, `due_diligence`, `phase_effective_date` or `other`. `due_on` is a valid `YYYY-MM-DD` date, `source` is nonblank text, and `note` is optional nonblank text or null. The deal page read returns a plain label for each kind, using the same labels as the checklist.
- Confirm opens a date proposed by automation. Resolve takes `done`, `dropped` or `passed` as `resolution` and returns it as `status`. A proposal can be dropped, and an open date can take any of the three resolutions. `reason` must be nonblank. The database stamps the decisions and keeps the confirmation when resolving.
- A missing date on confirm or resolve returns `404 not_found`. A fresh key targeting the date's current status returns `409 version_conflict { status }`. Other disallowed transitions return `422 database_refused_the_statement { rule: "critical_date_status_moves" }`.

### Deal tour plans

Both signed-in partners can use these doors for any deal. The paths below start with `/api/engine/deals/{deal_id}/tours`. Deal, tour, and property IDs are UUIDs.

- `GET` on that path returns `{ tours: [{ id, planned_on }], past_tours: [{ id, activity_id, happened_at, properties }] }`. `tours` holds open plans in date and ID order; completed and cancelled plans leave it. `past_tours` holds completed plans, read-only, newest meeting first. `activity_id` is the meeting, `happened_at` its UTC time, and `properties` the visited `{ property_id, address, name }` linked to that meeting, by address, empty when none are known. Cancelled plans appear in neither. A deal without plans returns two empty lists. Copied old-system tours are not listed: the copy turned them into meetings with their old IDs and kept nothing in the new schema that marks them as tours.
- `GET /{tour_id}` returns `{ id, deal_id, planned_on, notes, revision, completed_activity_id, cancelled_at, stops }`. Each stop has `{ property_id, position, address, name }`, in saved order with current property details.
- `POST` on the list path creates a plan and returns `201 { id, revision }`. `POST /{tour_id}` replaces a plan's date, notes, and stops and returns `200 { id, revision }`. Both take `{ idempotency_key, planned_on, notes?, stops }`. An edit also requires the last-read `revision`. The date is `YYYY-MM-DD` and omitted or null notes clear the notes. Each item in the nonempty `stops` array is `{ property_id }` for an existing property or `{ address, name? }` to create one. Array order sets the stop order. Reuse the shared retry key on retries.
- `POST /{tour_id}/complete` takes `{ idempotency_key, revision, happened_at, visited_stops, party_ids? }` and returns `200 { id, activity_id }`. Send the confirmed local date and time with its UTC offset in `happened_at`. Use the same nonempty stop shape for `visited_stops`, including any off-plan properties. New stops with the same trimmed address and name create one property. An omitted name and a null name count as the same name. The meeting links the deal, each distinct visited property, the deal's client when known, and any existing parties named by `party_ids`. A second completion returns the first meeting.
- `POST /{tour_id}/cancel` takes `{ idempotency_key, revision }` and returns `200 { id, revision, cancelled_at }`. Deal history shows a `cancel-tour` event with the cancelling partner.
- `GET /{tour_id}/stop-list` returns `{ text }` with the saved date in words and the numbered stops in saved order. It uses the current property names and addresses and omits internal notes.
- An unknown deal or a tour outside the named deal returns `404 not_found`. A save on a changed or finalized plan returns `409 version_conflict { current_revision }`, except completion of an already completed plan.

### 24. Home marks saved

`POST /api/engine/home/marks` saves one card's mark for the signed-in partner. Every request includes `idempotency_key` and the card's `item_id` and `base_version` from the new Home inbox read. Item IDs are `next_action:<UUID>`, `capture_post_call_action:<UUID>`, `notification:<UUID>`, `suggestion:<UUID>` and `reply_draft:<UUID>` for a step, call follow-up, update, suggestion card and reply draft. A suggestion uses the proposal ID that heads the card. A completion review uses the step's ID and mark version. An unavailable card returns `404 home_item_not_found`. A sent draft has no mark actions.

| `action`        | Extra fields and effect                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `snooze`        | `until`, a future instant with zone. The app picks the time for the chosen day in Central time.                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `unsnooze`      | Clears the snooze disposition and time while keeping any waiting details.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `drop`          | Required nonblank `reason`. Permanently drops a call follow-up (`capture_post_call_action:`), Home update (`notification:`, including `tour_feedback`) or reply draft that failed for good (`reply_draft:`). A draft that may still be approved again or found sent returns `422 invalid_field_value`. |
| `done`          | Marks a `tour_feedback` Home update read (`read_at = now()`), removing it from Home.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `undo_done`     | Clears `read_at` on a `tour_feedback` Home update so it returns to Home.                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `set-waiting`   | `target: { kind: "party", id }` and a nonblank `reason`. The ID is that of a person or company in the new database.                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `clear-waiting` | Clears the waiting person or company and reason.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

The version is `<source change ID>:<mark change ID>:<visible disposition>`, where the visible disposition is `inbox`, `snoozed`, or `done` (for a read `tour_feedback` update). For a suggestion card, the source change ID comes from the latest change to its visible group, including decisions that remove members. A draft uses its own change history. Missing history starts at `0`. A cleared mark keeps its latest history ID. A change after the card was read returns `409 version_conflict { current_version }`. A valid instant that has already passed returns `422 invalid_snooze_time` on a snooze. Waiting changes leave the disposition intact. When both parts of a mark are empty, the save deletes its row. A dropped card records `drop_reason` with the partner's stamp in change history, clears any snooze or waiting mark, leaves Home for that user for good (no Undo), answers `200 { ok: true, item_id }`, and returns `404 home_item_not_found` to later mark saves.

For all other actions, the answer is `200 { ok, item_id, base_version, previous }`. The previous mark has `disposition`, `snoozed_until`, `waiting_on_party_id` and `waiting_reason` with nulls for absent values. Undo uses a fresh key, the returned version and the previous value for the affected part of the mark (`undo_done` for `tour_feedback` Done). Clear a prior snooze whose time has passed. Shared retry rules apply. App routing (slice 30) submits one request per card for a selection and refreshes Home after saves.

#### Drop reasons

`GET /api/engine/home/drop-reasons?category=` answers `{ category, reasons }`, at most five one-tap reasons for Drop on Home. `category` is one of `next_step`, `filing`, `phase_move`, `lead`, `call_note`, `reply_draft`, `checklist_reminder` and `tour_feedback`. A missing category is `400 missing_required` and an unknown one `400 value_not_in_declared_vocabulary`. The read counts the signed-in partner's own stored drops: dismissed suggestions by `proposal.kind` (`next_step`; `file_to_deal` and `possible_new_deal` for filing; `phase_move`; `new_lead`, `email_lead` and `lead_stage_move` for a lead; `log_call` for a call note), with one grouped suggestion drop counted once per save; cancelled next steps (`next_step.resolution_reason`) as `next_step`; dropped Home marks by drop time (`changed_at`, where a call follow-up counts as `next_step`, a dropped failed reply draft counts as `reply_draft`, and a Home update counts by its kind); and dismissed reply drafts. System-written reasons (when a newer step or draft replaces one, or copied move placeholders) are not counted. Reasons match ignoring case and repeated spaces and show as last typed. Each category has a few starter reasons in `src/home-drop-reasons.ts`. A typed reason joins the choices once it was used twice. Choices are ordered by uses, then most recent use, then starter order, so starters fill the list until enough reasons are recorded. A starter keeps its own wording.

On an existing `next` schema, apply [the failed reply drop migration](../schema/add-failed-reply-drop.sql) after the owner approves it for production, before deploying the engine that drops failed replies. It can be applied twice. Fresh schemas include the rule.

A suggestion group uses the most recent mark revision among its proposals, including a clear. A new suggestion in the group keeps its snooze until the saved time, even when the new proposal becomes the card's ID.

### Updates and notification settings

The app's Settings page, opened from the account menu, reads and saves notification preferences (`/preferences`). The app's Updates page lists and marks updates (`/updates`, `/updates/read`, `/updates/changes`). It reads preferences only to show quiet hours and times in the saved zone, and does not save them. Both use the new schema directly, with no engine switch. The app's own routing moves the pages. The coordinator settled the rules below on 2026-11-21 (#1014).

`GET /api/engine/updates` lists the signed-in partner's own Home updates, read and unread, newest first by creation time, at most 100. The answer is `{ unread_count, updates }`. `unread_count` counts every unread update the list would show, not just the first 100. Each item is the update card Home gives (§ "Home inbox reads for the app"), plus `read_at`, null while unread. `unseen` is true while unread. A read update's `base_version` ends in `:done`. A Home update the partner dropped stays off Updates too, so it never counts as unread. Updates and the unread count use the same checklist eligibility as Home. The stored reminders remain in the database as history.

`POST /api/engine/updates/read` takes `{ idempotency_key, update_ids }`, one to 100 update ids. It sets `read_at` on each unread one. Reading is one-way and needs no version: an update already read keeps its first read time. The answer is `200 { ok: true, updates: [{ id, read_at }], unread_count }`, in id order. Any id that is not the partner's own update returns `404 not_found`, and nothing is marked. A read update leaves Home.

`GET /api/engine/preferences` gives the signed-in partner's `{ time_zone, quiet_from, quiet_until, push_to_device, base_version }`. Quiet hours are `HH:MM` in that zone, or both null. With no saved row, the read gives the defaults: `America/Chicago`, the engine's Central day, no quiet hours, `push_to_device` false, and `base_version` 0. `base_version` is the latest change ID of the partner's `user_preference` row.

After owner approval, run [the one-time preference repair](../schema/repair-preference-time-zone.sql) as the schema owner. It requires exactly one saved `UTC` row and change history showing that the old copy set its zone. It refuses if a user changed that preference after the copy or any Preferences save receipt exists, because receipts do not identify the partner and their timestamps can precede the save. The repair changes only the zone and records the save under the `old_system` job (#1037).

`POST /api/engine/preferences` saves all four fields with `idempotency_key` and the `base_version` read. The time zone must be one the database knows; otherwise the save returns `400 invalid_field_value { fields: ["time_zone"] }`. Quiet hours are both set or both null, and they may run past midnight, such as `22:00` to `07:00`. Equal or half-set quiet hours return `400 invalid_field_value { fields: ["quiet_from", "quiet_until"] }`. A change after the read returns `409 version_conflict { current_version }`. The answer is `200 { ok: true, time_zone, quiet_from, quiet_until, push_to_device, base_version }` with the new version. The shared retry rules apply.

- [ ] In Settings, quiet hours that are not set show empty boxes with the placeholder "Not set", never times that look saved.

#### Who did what

`GET /api/engine/updates/changes` lists recent changes to the records both partners share, newest first, 50 a page, for the Updates page (#1014). The answer is `{ changes, next_before }`. Each change is `{ id, changed_at, who, job, record, what, open_url }`: who made it, by name, or null when a job made it; the job's slug, such as `agent`, or null for a person; the name of the deal, or of the person or company a step or old change is about; what changed in plain words; and where it opens. `next_before` is null on the last page; otherwise pass it back as `?before=` for the next. Any other `before` returns `400 invalid_field_value`. These are settled rules, coordinator decisions (seat bl, 2026-11-21, #1014):

- From change history: a deal opened, moved to a phase, renamed, given a new deal owner, market or value, flagged or cleared, parked with its reason or revived; a next step on a deal, person or company added, finished, cancelled, reworded or moved to a new due date. One saved statement is one entry, its fields joined in one sentence.
- From `imported_change`: a partner's or the assistant's field changes in the old system that this read can name, such as "changed the deal owner" or "added a note", without their old values, whose shape varies.
- Every page has 50 entries except the last, with no time window. Both partners get the same list.
- Who is the user's name. A job gives its slug and the app names it, as it does on notes and the timeline, so the assistant's work reads "Written by the assistant" (#1281).
- Left out: rows the copy itself wrote as `old_system`, old changes the old system's own automations made (imported as `old_system`; they stay on the deal timeline, decision 213), old changes credited to a partner whose recorded cause is one of the old system's automatic causes, because its jobs also ran under a partner's sign-in (decision 216), changes to fields no partner reads about (stamps, parking times, holds), records since removed, and each partner's own settings, Home marks, updates, drafts and Doc conversations.

The app gets one list and one cursor. The two histories are merged by time, because imported history is not always older: a copy rerun before the switch brings in old events dated after engine saves. `next_before` holds where each history stopped, change history by its id, which grows with time, so that read follows its primary key without a new index, and imported history by its time.

### Deal watches and nightly checklist reminders

CARR still owns watch settings and delivery. A new subscription sets a baseline quietly. The five-minute sweep groups pending changes and lease reminders, queues one in-app update for a subscriber, and records the queued changes so a retry does not repeat the batch. A filed reply can alert the subscriber whose mailbox received it. The CARR sweep continues to read old facts for copied records. The old sources are `migrations/1166_subject_watch_changes.sql:7` and `migrations/1167_subject_watch_delivery.sql:90`.

`GET /api/engine/deals/:deal/watch` returns `{ watch, health }` from CARR's v2 watch read. Either field can be null. `POST` to the same path requires the session's CSRF token. Both requests use the signed-in partner from `PartnerApp` and require the deal's UUID in the copy's `old_system` map. The engine calls CARR's private `DealWatchSettings` entrypoint in the matching environment.

Every write takes `command` and a lowercase UUID `idempotency_key`. The `watch` and `replace-policy` commands take the v2 `policy`. The `pause`, `resume`, `replace-policy` and `unwatch` commands take `subscription_id` (the watch's lowercase UUID) and `base_version` (its `lifecycle_version`, a positive 32-bit integer). The policy lists distinct `change_kinds` from `reply_arrived`, `lease_threshold_crossed` and `listing_status_changed`. `include_self_changes` defaults to true. A lease threshold needs `lease_threshold_days`, `lease_threshold_months`, or both, with 1–20 distinct integers in each supplied array. Days range from 0 to 3650 and months from 0 to 120.

A successful write returns `{ ok: true, schema_version: "subject-watch.v2", watch, health, rows_changed }`. Retry the same write with its original key within 30 days to recover the prior answer with `replayed: true`. `watch_stale_version` and `key_reuse` return 409. On a stale version, read the watch again before editing. A deal outside the copy or a target CARR cannot resolve returns 404. A binding failure, malformed reply, or unrecognized CARR refusal returns 502 `watch_settings_unavailable`.

`GET /jobs/deal-watches/:kind/:id?subscriber=<slug>&since=<timestamp>` reads a deal or lease by UUID. The subscriber is a partner's slug. The timestamp includes a time zone and the boundary is inclusive. The answer has `kind`, `id`, `deal_id`, `href`, `changes`, and `reply_activity_ids`. A lease without a linked deal has a null `deal_id`. The link opens the deal or the lease's client.

Each change has `change_id`, `table_name`, `record` (the row's key), `action`, `field`, `old_value`, `new_value`, `actor`, `for_user`, and `changed_at`. The actor is the user or job that saved it. A job's sponsoring user is named where recorded. Changes are in timestamp, change ID, and field order. The read returns the saved fields behind a deal's phase, outcome, close date, owner, parking, open critical dates, and next step owners and dates. A deleted next step keeps its stamped history with the old field value and a null `new_value`. The watch shows a step-completion review only while the subscriber can open its proposal detail. It includes lease versions and option notice dates. A lease watch reads the watched lease's own dates and options, plus the linked deal's changes.

For a phase move, `old_value` is the preceding recorded phase of the same deal, and `new_value` is the phase it moved to. The preceding phase can come from copied history or from a move saved before `since`. If the deal has no earlier recorded phase, `old_value` is null.

Reply IDs come from inbound emails still filed to that deal and present in the subscriber's mailbox. The later of the filing time and that mailbox's capture time sets the boundary.

With `ENGINE_DEAL_WATCH_CHANGES=true`, the CARR sweep also reads engine changes for deal watches. The switch stays off until switch day. This path consumes changes only, not the returned reply IDs: engine-filed replies belong to engine Updates. It excludes H2 lease watches. CARR groups material fields by change ID into one receipt per subscription and consumes it once. CARR keeps watch policy and in-app delivery.

The intake floor is the latest of subscription, first activation of the engine sweep, and resume. Changes at or before that floor are excluded. A change committed while a resume is being saved may alert. The sweep overlaps reads by five minutes and assumes engine write transactions commit within five minutes.

The old nightly script took distinct deal IDs from the Home agenda in ID order (`tools/deal-checklist-reminders.py:40`). It queued checklist updates for the deal owner's morning window. That step is off so frozen old checklists cannot prompt for items already finished in the engine.

`GET /jobs/deals/:deal_id/checklist-reminders` answers with `deal_id`, `owner_user_id`, `owner` (slug), `observed_on` (Central date), and `items`. Each item has `item_ref`, `title`, and `due_on`. Parked, closed, lost, or invoiced deals and those without a partner owner return no items. The read lists open confirmed standard deadlines due by today in date order, then open sourced stored checklist items due by today in creation order.

The read excludes a reminder if `home_update` already has the recipient, deal, item reference, and due date. It requires the jobs Access token and a registered job.

`POST /jobs/deals/checklist-reminders` requires the jobs Access token and `Engine-Job: nightly_checklist`. Send a Central calendar date as `run_date` (`YYYY-MM-DD`) and an `idempotency_key` UUID. The engine checks all eligible deals for deadlines due through `run_date` and queues the reminders in the same transaction. The answer is `{ queued, already_queued }`. The database's unique key uses the owner, deal, item reference, and deadline's due date. A new run counts matching reminders as `already_queued`, including any queued by a concurrent run. A same-key retry returns the first counts with `replayed: true`.

The job saves “A deal checklist item is due. Open the deal checklist for details.” A reminder queued during the owner's four-hour morning window is visible immediately, including when the previous day's window is still open after midnight. At other times it stays off Home, Updates, and the unread count until the next window opens. The window opens at the end of quiet hours, or at 07:00 if quiet hours are unset. Opening and closing follow local calendar dates and clock times in the owner's time zone, which defaults to America/Chicago.

Existing databases need [the checklist reminder schema update](../schema/add-checklist-reminder-door.sql) before deploying the engine change. The Studio caller enables its separate switch after the route is ready (carr-system #3271).

### What can start when this merges

- Program 26, mail intake: email and activity and the filed-email read (19), attachments (20), the agenda (25) and the unknown-sender lead suggestion (21).
- Program 27, the router and reply send: suggestions (21) and reply delivery (22).
- Program 28 includes the nightly cadence (18), call capture (16, 18, 19, 21) and the assistant's step and activity saves (18, 19). Call capture must send `call: { activity_id, evidence_quote }` on its automatic deal step, using the id returned by door 19 and the update's evidence quote. The morning brief reads door 25's agenda. The nightly lead stages wait for the invoice application's door.

### Owner decisions

1. May the assistant accept or dismiss a suggestion when a partner asks it to? It dismissed 9 in the last 30 days; the new database lets only a partner decide directly. Recommended no: the assistant asks the partner instead.
2. Add the jobs `cadence` (nightly next steps) and `call_capture` (call suggestions and their automatic steps); only the owner adds a job.
3. Phase effective date is its own critical-date kind for new saves (owner, 2026-11-18). The [critical dates section](#critical-dates) records the rehearsal count for the copy.
4. Each national account's owner is Joe or Dell (owner, 2026-11-18).
5. A new location client under a national account has "national account or corporate lead" as how found (owner, 2026-11-18).
6. The 13 national-account memberships for person clients with no proven start date use the opening date of each client's first deal (owner, 2026-11-18).
